וְהֵשִׁיבוּ לוֹ: "מֵחֲמַת שֶׁבְּכָל עֶרֶב־שַׁבַּת־קֹדֶשׁ הַנְּשָׁמָה זוֹ עוֹלָה לְמַעְלָה וּנְשָׁמָה הַיְתֵרָה בָּאָה לְמַטָּה, וְאֶצְלְךָ הָלְכָה הַנְּשָׁמָה שֶׁלְּךָ וּנְשָׁמָה יְתֵרָה אֵינְךָ זוֹכֶה לְקַבֵּל עַל־כֵּן נִשְׁאָר גּוּפְךָ כְּאֶבֶן".


They replied, “Every Friday afternoon, as Shabbat approaches, the soul goes up on high and the additional Shabbat soul descends (see *Beitzah* 15b; *Zohar* II, 135b, 204b). In your case, your weekday soul has left, but you are not worthy of the Shabbat soul. Therefore, your body is like a stone.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 10:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:9)


וְהֵשִׁיב לָהֶם: "אוֹי לָכֶם, וַאֲפִלּוּ בְּדָבָר קַל כָּזֶה אֵינְכֶם יוֹדְעִים מַה לְּהָשִׁיב".


The Rebbe mocked them, “Woe is to you! You cannot even answer a simple question.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 10:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:10)


וּבִקֵּשׁ אוֹתָם שֶׁיְּקַבְּלוּ אוֹתוֹ עַל שַׁבָּת אֶצְלָם וְלֹא רָצוּ. וּבְשַׁבָּת קִבְּלוּ אֶת הָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ אֶצְלָם עַל שַׁבָּת, וְאוֹתוֹ לֹא רָצוּ לְקַבֵּל וְהֻכְרַח לֶאֱכֹל מַאַכְלֵי חָלָב (כִּי לֹא רָצוּ לֶאֱכֹל שָׁם בָּשָׂר. רַק הֵם, הָיָה לָהֶם אֵיזֶה שׁוֹחֵט).


The Rebbe asked if he could spend Shabbat with them. They refused, inviting only his attendant. The Rebbe would not eat any meat there and was forced to eat dairy foods for Shabbat. They, however, had a *shochet* who provided them with meat.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 10:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:11)


וְגַם בְּשַׁבָּת הָיָה לָהֶם מַחֲלֹקֶת גָּדוֹל מְאֹד עִמּוֹ, וְרָצוּ לְדָחְפוֹ לַחוּץ כִּי הוּא נִכְנַס אֶצְלָם אַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁלֹּא אָכַל עִמָּהֶם, וְהֵם הִתְחִילוּ לָרִיב עִמּוֹ עַד שֶׁרָצוּ לְדָחְפוֹ לַחוּץ וְהָיָה רַעַשׁ גָּדוֹל שָׁם. וְכֵן בַּבֹּקֶר הָיָה גַּם־כֵּן כָּךְ.


That Friday night, they had a bitter dispute with the Rebbe, almost throwing him out of the house. Although he had not been invited to eat with them, he stopped in for a visit. But they began to quarrel with him, causing a great commotion and trying to throw him out of the house. This happened again during the day.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 10:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:12)


כְּלַל הַדָּבָר, כִּי הוּא ז"ל עָשָׂה בְּכַוָּנָה, וְהִנִּיחַ עַצְמוֹ לְבַזּוֹת בְּכָל מִינֵי בִּזְיוֹנוֹת. וְאָמַר לְהָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ: שֶׁאֵלּוּ הַבִּזְיוֹנוֹת יִהְיוּ לוֹ טוֹבָה גְּדוֹלָה בַּהֲלִיכָה וּבַחֲזָרָה. כִּי גֹּדֶל עֹצֶם הַמְּנִיעוֹת הַגְּדוֹלוֹת שֶׁהָיָה לוֹ לָבוֹא לְאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל אִי־אֶפְשָׁר לְשַׁעֵר וּלְהַעֲרִיךְ וּלְסַפֵּר. וְלֹא הָיָה לוֹ בְּאֶפְשָׁרִי לָבוֹא לְאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל כִּי אִם עַל־יְדֵי קַטְנוּת זֹאת, כַּאֲשֶׁר נִשְׁמַע אַחַר־כָּךְ מִפִּיו בְּפֵרוּשׁ.


All this was actually done intentionally. The Rebbe allowed himself to be insulted in every possible manner. He told his attendant, “These insults are very beneficial to me, clearing the way both there and back. Before I reach my goal, I must overcome great barriers beyond measure or description. Without this degradation, it would be utterly impossible for me to set foot in the Holy Land.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 10:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:13)


וְאָמַר: שֶׁאִם לֹא הָיוּ לוֹ אֵלּוּ הַבִּזְיוֹנוֹת וְהַקַּטְנוּת, לֹא הָיָה לוֹ בְּאֶפְשָׁרִי לָבוֹא לְשָׁם בְּשׁוּם אֹפֶן.


Later, the Rebbe said explicitly, “If not for these insults, it would have been absolutely impossible for me to reach my destination.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 10:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:14)


וְאָמַר: שֶׁרָאָה שֶׁהוּא מֻכְרָח לִשָּׁאֵר שָׁם בִּסְטַנְבּוּל, דְּהַיְנוּ לָמוּת שָׁם, אַךְ זֹאת הַקַּטְנוּת וְהַבִּזְיוֹנוֹת הִצִּילוּ אוֹתוֹ, כִּי קֹדֶם שֶׁבָּאִין לְאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל וְכוּ' כַּנַּ"ל.


Another time, he said, “I saw that I would have to die in Istanbul, but the humiliation and insults saved me. To approach the Holy Land, one must overcome many barriers” (cf. *Tzaddik* #36).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 10:15

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:15](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:15)


אַחַר־כָּךְ צִוָּה רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל לְהָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ, שֶׁיִּתֵּן לְהָאֲנָשִׁים הַנַּ"ל שֶׁבִּזּוּ אוֹתוֹ (כַּנַּ"ל) מֵהַמַּאֲכָלִים וּמַשְׁקָאוֹת וּמִינֵי מִרְקַחַת שֶׁלָּהֶם שֶׁהָיָה עִמָּהֶם, לִתֵּן לָהֶם מֵהַכֹּל.


The Rebbe told his attendant to give his detractors some of the food and preserves they were carrying. He said to give them a little of everything.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 10:16

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:16](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:16)


וְנָסְעוּ אֵלּוּ הָאַנָשִׁים לְבֵיתָם, וְלֹא רָצוּ לְקַבֵּל אִגֶּרֶת מֵרַבֵּנוּ לְבֵיתוֹ, רַק מֵהָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ קִבְּלוּ אִגֶּרֶת. וְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל הִנִּיחַ אִגֶּרֶת שֶׁלּוֹ בְּתוֹךְ אִגֶּרֶת שֶׁל הָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל.


The two men who had been so insulting to the Rebbe then set out for home. The Rebbe asked them to take along a letter to his family, but they refused. They did, however, take along a letter from his attendant.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 10:17

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:17](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:17)


וְאֵלּוּ הָאֲנָשִׁים הָיוּ רוֹצִים לִפְתֹּחַ אֶת הָאִגֶּרֶת שֶׁל הָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל אוּלַי יוּכְלוּ לְהָבִין מִמֶּנּוּ אֵיזֶה דָּבָר מֵרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, מִי הוּא וּמֵאַיִן הוּא, אֲבָל לֹא הָיוּ יְכוֹלִים לִמְצֹא הָאִגֶּרֶת אֶצְלָם, כִּי נֶעְלַם מֵהֶם הֵיכָן שֶׁהִנִּיחוּ אֶת הָאִגֶּרֶת.


As soon as they left, they wanted to open the letter to see if they could find any clue as to the Rebbe’s identity. But when they looked for the letter, they could not find it among their belongings. Try as they might, they could not remember where they had put it.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 10:18

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:18](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/10:18)


וְלֹא בָּא לְיָדָם הָאִגֶּרֶת עַד שֶׁבָּאוּ אֶל הַקַּאמֶער (תַּחֲנַת מַעֲבַר גְּבוּל) בְּוָלִיחַאי אָז מָצְאוּ הָאִגֶּרֶת אֶצְלָם, וְרָאוּ מִן הָאִגֶּרֶת שֶׁזֶּה הָיָה רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל. הֵמָּה רָאוּ כֵּן תָּמָהוּ, וְנִבְהֲלוּ מְאֹד. וְהָאִישׁ הַהוּא שֶׁבִּזָּה אוֹתוֹ הַרְבֵּה כַּנַּ"ל בָּא לְבֵיתוֹ וְנִפְטַר בְּסָמוּךְ לְבִיאָתוֹ מַמָּשׁ.


The letter did not appear until they arrived at the port of Walachia.²³ When they opened the letter, they discovered the Rebbe’s true identity. As they saw, so they wondered (Psalms 48:6), and they were very frightened. The man who had insulted the Rebbe the most died soon after returning home (see "*His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land*" #29).

footnotes:
²³ Or Wallachia. Until 1861, an independent principality located between the Transylvanian Alps and the Danube River; now part of Romania.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/11:1)


וּמִיָּד אַחַר נְסִיעָתָם מִסְּטַנְבּוּל, בָּא אֵלָיו הַסַּרְסוּר הַנַּ"ל וְהֶרְאָה לוֹ אִגְּרוֹת הַרְבֵּה מִצַּדִּיקֵי דּוֹרֵנוּ שֶׁשָּׁלְחוּ אֵלָיו בַּקָּשָׁתָם לִהְיוֹת לְעֵזֶר לַאֲנָשִׁים הַנּוֹסְעִים מֵהָכָא לְהָתָם [מכאן לשם], כִּי הוּא חָשׁוּב בְּעֵינֵי הַשָּׂרֵי מְלוּכָה.


Soon after these two left Istanbul, the agent to whom they had spoken came to the Rebbe and his attendant. He showed them letters from all the Jewish leaders, asking him to help people traveling through Istanbul. He did this to emphasize his importance in the government.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/11:2)


בְּכֵן תֵּדְעוּ, אִם תְּגַלּוּ לִי מִי אַתֶּם וּמֵאֵיזֶה מִשְׁפָּחָה אַתֶּם הֲרֵי טוֹב. וְאִם לָאו יֵשׁ לְאֵל יָדִי לַעֲשׂוֹת עִמָּכֶם רָעָה אֲשֶׁר לֹא תּוּכְלוּ כַּפְּרָהּ, וְלֹא יוֹעִיל הוֹן לְשַׁחֵד בְּמָמוֹן. וְלֹא דַּי שֶׁלֹּא תִּסְעוּ לְאֶרֶץ הַקֹּדֶשׁ גַּם תִּהְיוּ בְּבֵית הָאֲסוּרִים. וְכַיּוֹצֵא בִּדְבָרִים אֵלֶּה.


He told the Rebbe, “If you tell me about yourself and your family, good! But if not, the power is in my hands to do you evil (Genesis 31:29), and you will not be able to undo it (Isaiah 47:11). Not only will you not be permitted to continue your journey, but you will be imprisoned. Even a fortune in bribes will not help when I get through.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/11:3)


וְכַאֲשֶׁר שָׁמַע זֹאת רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, מִיָּד אָמַר לוֹ הָאֱמֶת. שֶׁשְּׁמוֹ רַבִּי נַחְמָן, וְהוּא נֶכֶד לְהַבַּעַל־שֵׁם־טוֹב ז"ל, וּלְרַבִּי נַחְמָן מֵהָרִידֶענְקֶא ז"ל.


When the Rebbe heard this, he immediately told the truth. He said that he was a great-grandson of the Baal Shem Tov and a grandson of Rabbi Nachman Horodenker.²⁴

footnotes:
²⁴ Rabbi Nachman of Horodenka (d. 1761) was a leading disciple of the Baal Shem Tov and a member of the latter's household. His son, Rabbi Simcha, was the father of our Rebbe Nachman.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 11:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/11:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/11:4)


מִיָּד נָסוֹג אָחוֹר הַסַּרְסוּר הַנַּ"ל וְהָלַךְ מֵאִתּוֹ. וּלְיוֹם הַמָּחֳרָת בָּא הַסַּרְסוּר בְּהַכְנָעָה אֵלָיו.


The agent’s attitude changed immediately and he left in a completely different mood. When he returned the next day, he was very submissive to the Rebbe.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 11:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/11:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/11:5)


וְאָמַר לְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל בִּלְשׁוֹן אַהֲבָה וְחִבָּה: "רַבִּי, ה' עִמָּכֶם. תֵּדְעוּ שֶׁכָּל הַטּוֹבוֹת תְּקַבְּלוּ מֵאִתִּי, וּשְׂכַרְכֶם יִהְיֶה כָּפוּל עַל זֶה שֶׁגִּלִּיתֶם לִי הָאֱמֶת. כִּי אִם לֹא הֱיִיתֶם מוֹדִים לִי, הָיִיתִי מוֹסֵר אֶתְכֶם לְבֵית הַסֹּהַר, וְהָיִיתִי נֶעֱנָשׁ בְּעָלְמָא דֵּין וּבְעָלְמָא דְאָתֵי. וּבִקַּשְׁתִּי אֶתְכֶם לְקַבֵּל טוֹב מֵאִתִּי, הֵן בִּשְׂכִירוּת הַסְּפִינָה, הֵן בִּשְׁאָר דְּבָרִים כִּי אֲנִי מוּכָן וּמְזֻמָּן לְשָׁרֵת אֶתְכֶם".


He spoke with great friendliness. “Rebbe, may God be with you. I will do everything I can, now that you have told me the truth. Had you not disclosed your identity, I would have imprisoned you, and I would have been punished in this world and the next. But now, please, let me help you. If you need a ship or anything else, I am ready to serve you in any way possible.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:1)


וּבְיוֹם מָחָר בָּא וְאָמַר: שֶׁבָּא סְפִינָה מֵחוּץ־לָאָרֶץ עִם יְהוּדִים וְיֵשׁ לָהֶם גַּם־כֵּן שׁוֹחֵט מֻבְהָק. וְשָׁם בְּאוֹתָהּ סְפִינָה הָיָה הָרַב הֶחָסִיד הַמְפֻרְסָם מוֹרֵנוּ הָרַב זְאֵב וָלְף נ"י וּשְׁאָר אֲנָשִׁים חֲשׁוּבִים.


The next day, the agent came and told them that a ship carrying some Jews had arrived from Europe. Among the group was an expert *shochet* who could provide them with proper kosher meat. The ship had also brought the renowned Rabbi Zev Wolf²⁵. See *Shem HaGedolim HeChadash* Z-9. of Charni, Austria, and other Jewish leaders.

footnotes:
²⁵ Rabbi Zev Wolf (d. 1823) was a disciple of Rabbi Dov Ber, the Maggid of Mezeritch, and the mentor of Rabbi Shlomo of Soroka, author of Arvey Nachal


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:2)


וְשָׁאֲלוּ עַל רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, וְגִלָּה לָהֶם הָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִם רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל הָאֱמֶת. וְתֵכֶף שָׁלַח ר' זְאֵב הַנַּ"ל אַחֲרֵי רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, שֶׁיָּבוֹא אֵלָיו לָאַכְסַנְיָא שֶׁלּוֹ וְיִקַבְּלוֹ בְּאַהֲבָה לְהִתְאָרֵחַ עִמּוֹ יַחַד. וְלֹא רָצָה רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל לָלֶכֶת אֶצְלוֹ, כִּי אָמַר: "כָּאן טוֹב לְפָנַי".


The Rebbe’s attendant was introduced to Rabbi Zev, and told him the truth. Rabbi Zev immediately sent for the Rebbe, inviting him to stay with them. They urged the Rebbe in the friendliest way imaginable, but the Rebbe would not go. He said, “It is good for me where I am.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 12:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:3)


כִּי הָיָה עוֹשֶׂה בִּסְטַנְבּוּל כָּל מִינֵי קַטְנוּת, וְהָיָה הוֹלֵךְ יָחֵף וּבְלִי חֲגוֹרָה וּבְלִי כּוֹבַע עֶלְיוֹן, וְהָיָה מְלֻבָּשׁ רַק בְּהָ"אִנְטֶער־שְׁלַאק" שֶׁהָיָה לוֹ מֵאֵיזֶה מַלְבּוּשׁ, וְהָיָה הוֹלֵךְ בַּשּׁוּק כְּדֶרֶךְ בְּנֵי הַנְּעוּרִים הָרָצִים בַּשּׁוּק וּמְצַחֲקִים, וְהָיָה עוֹשֶׂה מִלְחָמוֹת בְּדֶרֶךְ צְחוֹק כְּדֶרֶךְ בְּנֵי הַנְּעוּרִים. וְהָיוּ מְכַנִּים אֶחָד בְּשֵׁם הַצָּרְפַת, וְאֶחָד בְּשֵׁם אַחֵר, וְעָשׂוּ מִלְחָמָה וְהָיָה כְּעֵין תַּכְסִיסֵי מִלְחָמָה מַמָּשׁ. וְהָיָה עוֹשֶׂה עִנְיְנֵי קַטְנוּת הַרְבֵּה מְאֹד שָׁם בִּסְטַאנְבּוּל.


One reason why the Rebbe avoided Rabbi Zev was because he was doing all sorts of childish things in Istanbul. He went through the streets barefoot and hatless, wearing only his yarmulke and coat lining. Running in the street like a young child, the Rebbe played childish war games.²⁶" #77. He would name one child, "France," another something else, and engage in mock warfare using real battle strategy. This was one of the many childish things he did in Istanbul.

footnotes:
²⁶ Or maybe not so childish. See "His Wisdom


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 12:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:4)


אַחַר־כָּךְ נָפַל הַדֶּבֶר רַחֲמָנָא לִצְלָן בְּחַאן (חָצֵר) זֶה, וְהָיָה מֻכְרָח לִבְרֹחַ מִשָּׁם. וְהֻכְרַח לִכָּנֵס בָּאַכְסַנְיָא שֶׁל רַבִּי זְאֵב הַנַּ"ל. וְעָשָׂה עֲבוּרוֹ סְעֻדָּה גְּדוֹלָה. וְהָיָה הָרַב ר' זְאֵב הַנַּ"ל נוֹהֵג בּוֹ כָּבוֹד גָּדוֹל מְאֹד מְאֹד בְּלִי עֵרֶךְ.


Then an epidemic broke out on his street, forcing him to flee. He then had to move in with Rabbi Zev. Rabbi Zev celebrated the event with a great feast in the Rebbe’s honor, showing him immeasurable respect.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 12:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:5)


וְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, עָשָׂה כַּמָּה דְּבָרִים אָז שֶׁהָיָה כְּנֶגֶד רְצוֹן ר' זְאֵב הַנַּ"ל, אַךְ אַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן אַהֲבָה מְקַלְקֶלֶת הַשּׁוּרָה, וּמֵעֹצֶם הָאַהֲבָה לֹא הִסְתַּכֵּל עַל זֶה כְּלָל, אַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁהָיָה בְּעֵינָיו פְּלִיאוֹת גְּדוֹלוֹת.


The Rebbe did many things that may have annoyed Rabbi Zev, but as the saying goes, “Love breaks down all barriers” (*Bereshit Rabbah* 55:11; cf. *Sanhedrin* 105b). Rabbi Zev was so fond of the Rebbe that he ignored everything, even though he found some of his antics quite shocking.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 12:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:6)


כִּי בְּכָל־עֵת שֶׁהָיָה ר' זְאֵב מִתְפַּלֵּל לִפְנֵי הָעַמּוּד בְּשַׁבָּת כְּדֶרֶךְ הַמְפֻרְסָמִים, וְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל הָיָה אוֹכֵל בְּאוֹתוֹ הָעֵת. כִּי הָיָה מִתְפַּלֵּל מִקֹּדֶם עַד שֶׁבְּעֵת תְּפִלָּתוֹ שֶׁל ר' זְאֵב הָיָה הוּא ז"ל אוֹכֵל סְעֻדָּתוֹ. וְכֵן הָיָה בְּלֵיל שַׁבָּת, וּבְשַׁבָּת בְּשַׁחֲרִית, וּבִסְעֻדָּה שְׁלִישִׁית.


The first Shabbat that the Rebbe stayed with them, Rabbi Zev led the Friday evening prayers, as was the custom with many great tzaddikim. The Rebbe had prayed earlier and was already eating the Shabbat evening meal when Rabbi Zev began his worship. This happened again during the morning prayers and again at the late-afternoon *seudah shelishit*.²⁷ prayers.

footnotes:
²⁷ The third meal of Shabbat, which follows the afternoon Minchah


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 12:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:7)


וּבְעֵת שֶׁיָּשַׁב ר' זְאֵב לַסְּעֻדָּה הַשְּׁלִישִׁית, כְּבָר בֵּרַךְ רַבֵּנוּ בִּרְכַּת־הַמָּזוֹן. וְצִוָּה לְהָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ שֶׁיִּסְתַּכֵּל אִם יֵשׁ כּוֹכָבִים, וְהִתְפַּלֵל תֵּכֶף עַרְבִית, וְהִבְדִּיל, וְלָקַח הַלּוּלְקֶע וְנִכְנַס לְבֵית ר' זְאֵב.


As soon as Rabbi Zev sat down to *seudah shelishit*, the Rebbe began to recite the Grace after Meals. He asked his attendant to tell him as soon as the stars became visible, and then prayed the evening service. He recited the *Havdalah* ceremony ending the Shabbat, lit his pipe and entered Rabbi Zev’s house.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 12:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:8)


וְהוּא הִתְחִיל בְּסָמוּךְ הַסְּעֻדָּה הַשְּׁלִישִׁית כְּדֶרֶךְ הַמְפֻרְסָמִים, וְתֵכֶף כְּשֶׁנִּכְנַס רַבֵּנוּ לְשָׁם בְּלִי כּוֹבַע עֶלְיוֹן וּבְלִי חֲגוֹרָה וְהַלּוּלְקֶע בְּיָדוֹ, תֵּכֶף קִבְּלוֹ בְּכָבוֹד גָּדוֹל ר' זְאֵב הַנַּ"ל, וְבֵרַךְ בִּרְכַּת־הַמָּזוֹן מִיָּד, וְהִתְפַּלֵּל עַרְבִית, וְהִבְדִּיל, וְדִבֵּר עִם רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל כִּמְעַט כָּל הַלַּיְלָה. וְהָיָה אַהֲבָה גְּדוֹלָה בֵּינֵיהֶם. וְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל עָשָׂה שָׁם כָּל מִינֵי קַטְנוּת כַּנַּ"ל.


Rabbi Zev was just beginning *seudah shelishit* in the way of the renowned masters. In walked the Rebbe with his coat wide open, wearing only his yarmulke and smoking his pipe. Rabbi Zev still greeted him with great respect. He immediately said the Grace after Meals, prayed the evening service and recited *Havdalah*. Then he sat up all night speaking with the Rebbe with the utmost affection.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 12:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:9)


וְהָיָה שָׁם עַל קֶבֶר שֶׁל ר' נַפְתָּלִי ז"ל. וְכָל מַה שֶּׁעָבַר עָלָיו בִּסְטַנְבּוּל בְּגַשְׁמִיּוּת וּבְרוּחָנִיּוּת תִּקְצַר הֲמוֹן יְרִיעוֹת לְסַפֵּר.


All that happened in Istanbul, both spiritually and materially, would consume many volumes. There are the stories of all the Rebbe’s childish pranks, as well as the account of his visit to the grave of Rabbi Naftali.²⁸ I, 218.

footnotes:
²⁸ Rabbi Naftali ben Isaac Katz of Posen, who embarked on a journey to the Holy Land but passed away en route in Istanbul on 26 Tevet 5479/1719. Rabbi Naftali was an ancestor of Rebbe Nachman, he lived in Breslov for a while, and his journey paralleled that of Rebbe Nachman in many ways. His grave was considered a shrine for pilgrims to the Holy Land; there is a record that it was also visited by Rabbi Menachem Mendel of Vitebsk on his journey there. See Likutey Moharan


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 12:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:10)


וּכְפִי הַמּוּבָן מִדְּבָרָיו, שֶׁהָיָה שָׁם, בְּסַכָּנָה גְּדוֹלָה וַעֲצוּמָה וְהָיָה סָבוּר שֶׁבְּוַדַּאי יִהְיֶה נִשְׁאָר שָׁם דְּהַיְנוּ שֶׁיִּסְתַּלֵּק שָׁם אַךְ ה' יִתְבָּרַךְ עָשָׂה עִמּוֹ נִסִּים הַרְבֵּה וְעָבַר עַל הַכֹּל. וְהַקַּטְנוּת הַנַּ"ל הוֹעִיל לוֹ מְאֹד.


It is apparent from the Rebbe’s words that he was in tremendous danger in Istanbul. He was certain that he would remain there until he died, but God miraculously intervened and he was saved. The main things that helped were this childishness and degradation.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 12:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:11)


גַּם כְּשֶׁבָּא מֵהַקֶּבֶר שֶׁל ר' נַפְתָּלִי, נָפַל פִּתְאֹם עַל הָאָרֶץ וְשָׁכַב כָּךְ כַּמָּה שָׁעוֹת. וְאַחַר־כָּךְ הִנִּיחוּ אוֹתוֹ עַל הַמִטָּה וְשָׁכַב שָׁם כָּל הַלַּיְלָה עַד לְמָחֳרָת חֲצִי הַיּוֹם. וְהָיָה שׁוֹכֵב כְּמוֹ שֶׁנִּגְוַע עַד שֶׁעֲזָרוֹ ה' יִתְבָּרַךְ שֶׁקָּם מִמִּטָּתוֹ וְחָזַר לְאֵיתָנוֹ.


When the Rebbe returned from Rabbi Naftali’s grave, he fainted suddenly and lay still for several hours. They placed him in bed, where he lay all night until noon the next day. He was on the verge of death, until God helped him and restored his health.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 12:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/12:12)


וְהָיָה לוֹ סַכָּנוֹת גְּדוֹלוֹת רַבּוֹת וַעֲצוּמוֹת, וּמְנִיעוֹת אֵין מִסְפָּר בְּכָל־עֵת וּבְכָל רֶגַע שָׁם. וְאָמַר: שֶׁהַבִּזְיוֹנוֹת וְהַקַּטְנוּת הַנַּ"ל הוֹעִיל לוֹ מְאֹד כַּנַּ"ל.


This was just one of the great dangers and countless barriers that he encountered every day in Istanbul. The Rebbe said that the insults and childishness sustained him.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/13:1)


וְאָמַר רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: שֶׁכָּל כָּךְ נִכְנַס בְּהַקַּטְנוּת, וְנַעֲשָׂה רָגִיל בְּעִנְיְנֵי הַקַּטְנוּת כָּל־כָּךְ, עַד שֶׁאַחַר־כָּךְ כְּשֶׁבָּא לְאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל וְרָצָה לְהַשְׁלִיךְ זֹאת הָיָה מֻכְרָח לְהַכְרִיחַ עַצְמוֹ בְּכֹחוֹת לְשַׁבֵּר וּלְהַשְׁלִיךְ אֶת עִנְיְנֵי קַטְנוּת הַנַּ"ל, כִּי כְּבָר הָיָה קָשֶׁה עָלָיו לְהַשְׁלִיךְ זֹאת מֵחֲמַת גֹּדֶל הָרְגִילוּת. כָּל כָּךְ הָיָה נִכְנָס בְּהַקַּטְנוּת בִּתְחִלָּה.


The Rebbe said, “At the time, I became so involved in childishness that I became totally accustomed to acting that way. Later, when I reached the Holy Land, it was difficult to act otherwise. I actually had to force myself to break these childish habits.” It had become such a habit that it was very difficult to break.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/13:2)


גַּם אַחַר־כָּךְ כְּשֶׁבָּא מֵאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל הָיָה חִדּוּשׁ נִפְלָא בְּעִנְיָן זֶה. וְעֹצֶם הַפְלָגַת גְּדֻלַּת חָכְמָתוֹ בְּעִנְיָן זֶה, עָמֹק עָמֹק, לֹא נִשְׁמַע וְלֹא נִרְאָה כָּזֹאת.


Later, when he returned from his pilgrimage, he was very unique in this respect, lowering himself in order to reach higher levels. In this respect, his wisdom was outstanding.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 13:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/13:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/13:3)


כִּי זֶה יָדוּעַ שֶׁקֹּדֶם שֶׁיּוֹצְאִין מִדַּרְגָּא לְדַרְגָּה צָרִיךְ שֶׁיִּהְיֶה יְרִידָה קֹדֶם הָעֲלִיָּה וְכוּ', וְאָז צְרִיכִין לְעִנְיְנֵי קַטְנוּת לִהְיוֹת אִישׁ פָּשׁוּט לְגַמְרֵי וְכוּ'.


It is a well-known fact that one must lower himself in order to proceed from one step to a higher one. At such a time, one must make use of childishness and absolute simplicity.²⁹ in *Shevachey HaBaal Shem Tov*, p. 224. Rebbe Nachman said that the Land of Israel is greatness of greatness, and therefore must be approached with smallness of smallness (*Tzaddik* #37).

footnotes:
²⁹ See Tzava'at HaBaal Shem Tov


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 13:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/13:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/13:4)


וְהוּא ז"ל, מֵחֲמַת שֶׁכָּל יָמָיו לֹא הָיָה עוֹמֵד בְּשׁוּם פַּעַם עַל מַדְרֵגָה אַחַת, רַק בְּכָל פַּעַם בְּכָל יוֹם וּבְכָל שָׁעָה הָיָה עוֹלֶה תָּמִיד מִדַּרְגָּה לְדַרְגָּה, עַל־כֵּן הָיְתָה חָכְמָתוֹ בְּעִנְיָנִים אֵלּוּ גָּבוֹהַּ וְעָמֹק מְאֹד "עָמֹק עָמֹק מִי יִמְצָאֶנּוּ".


The Rebbe never remained on one level, but constantly raised himself step by step. His understanding of these concepts was therefore very profound – deep, deep, who can fathom it? (Ecclesiastes 7:24).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:1)


וְאָז בְּאוֹתוֹ הָעֵת הָיְתָה הַמִּלְחָמָה שֶׁל הַצָּרְפַת, שֶׁהָלַךְ הַצָּרְפַת לְאֶרֶץ הַתּוֹגָר לְמִצְרַיִם וּלְאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל (וְכַיּוֹצֵא כַּמְפֻרְסָם).


At that time, France was at war, having invaded the sultan’s territory in Egypt and Palestine.³⁰

footnotes:
³⁰ Napoleon’s forces had arrived in Egypt on July 1 and occupied Alexandria the next day. Turkey declared war on France in September of that year.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:2)


וְכַאֲשֶׁר נִשְׁמַע לְאַנְשֵׁי סְטַנְבּוּל שֶׁהַמִּלְחָמָה הַנַּ"ל מִתְעוֹרֶרֶת, וְהַצָּרְפַת הוֹלֵךְ וּבָא עַל הַיָּם, אֲזַי לֹא רָצוּ קְהִלָּה קַדִּישָׁא שֶׁל סְטַנְבּוּל לְהַנִּיחַ שׁוּם יְהוּדִי לָצֵאת מִקִּיר הָעִיר וָחוּצָה, לִפְרֹשׁ עַל הַיָּם. וְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל לֹא הִשְׁגִּיחַ עַל זֶה, וְרָצָה לְהַפְקִיר אֶת עַצְמוֹ.


When the news that war had broken out and that the French were patrolling the Mediterranean reached Istanbul, the Jewish community immediately forbade any Jew to leave the city and travel by sea. The Rebbe ignored all this and was willing to risk everything.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 14:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:3)


וְאָמַר לְהָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ: "תֵּדַע, שֶׁאֲנִי רוֹצֶה לְסַכֵּן אֶת עַצְמִי אֲפִלּוּ בְּסַכָּנוֹת גְּדוֹלוֹת וַעֲצוּמוֹת. אַךְ אֶת נַפְשְׁךָ אֵינִי רוֹצֶה לְהַפְקִיר. בְּכֵן, אִם תִּרְצֶה קַח לְךָ מָעוֹת עַל הוֹצָאוֹת וְשׂוּב בְּשָׁלוֹם לְבֵיתְךָ וַאֲנִי אֶסַּע לְבַדִּי בְּהֶעְלֵם וּבְהֶסְתֵּר מֵאַנְשֵׁי סְטַנְבּוּל. כִּי אֶת עַצְמִי אֲנִי מַפְקִיר יִהְיֶה אֵיךְ שֶׁיִּהְיֶה" וְכוּ'.


He told his attendant, “I am willing to expose myself to any danger, no matter how great, but I am not willing to risk your life. Take money for expenses and return home in peace, if you wish. I will travel alone, hidden and concealed from the people of Istanbul. For I am renouncing myself to whatever may happen.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 14:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:4)


וְהָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל מֵאֵן בָּזֶה וְאָמַר: "בַּמָּקוֹם אֲשֶׁר יִהְיֶה שָׁם אֲדוֹנִי, אִם לַמָּוֶת אִם לְחַיִּים, שָׁם יִהְיֶה עַבְדְּךָ וּבַאֲשֶׁר תֵּלֵךְ אֵלֵךְ גַּם־כֵּן".


His attendant refused to leave, saying, “Where you go, whether to life or to death, there your servant will be (cf. II Samuel 15:21). Where you go, there will I also go" (Ruth 1:16).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 14:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:5)


וה' יִתְבָּרַךְ בְּרַחֲמָיו הוּא מִסִּבּוֹת מִתְהַפֵּךְ, וְסִבֵּב בְּרַחֲמָיו, שֶׁאַחַר יוֹם אוֹ יוֹמַיִם נִשְׁמַע: הֱיוֹת שֶׁיּוֹשֵׁב שָׁם בִּסְטַנְבּוּל חָכָם הַגָּדוֹל מִירוּשָׁלַיִם אֲשֶׁר קִבֵּץ עַל יָד נְדָבוֹת עֲבוּר עֲנִיֵּי יְרוּשָׁלַיִם עִיר־הַקֹּדֶשׁ, וְהָיָה בְּדַעְתּוֹ לְהִתְעַכֵּב שָׁם עוֹד שָׁנָה אַחַת.


In His great mercy, God reversed things through His guidance (Job 37:12). It so happened that a great sage from Jerusalem lived in Istanbul. He had been collecting charity for the poor of Jerusalem and intended to remain in Istanbul for at least another year.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 14:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:6)


וּפִתְאֹם נִתְעוֹרֵר וְאָמַר לְאַנְשֵׁי סְטַנְבּוּל: "תֵּדְעוּ שֶׁנִּתְגַּלָּה לִי שֶׁאֲנִי מֻכְרָח מִיָּד לִנְסֹעַ לִירוּשָׁלַיִם, כִּי אֲנִי רוֹאֶה שֶׁהַיָּמִים מְמַשְׁמְשִׁין וּבָאִין לְהִסְתַּלֵּק מִן הָעוֹלָם, וְאַתֶּם אַל תִּירְאוּ אֶת עַם הַפְרַנְצוֹיז, וְאֵין לָכֶם לִדְאֹג עֲבוּר יִשְׂרָאֵל שֶׁיֵּלְכוּ לְהָתָם, כִּי בְּוַדַּאי לֹא יְאֻנֶּה לָהֶם שׁוּם הֶזֵּק בְּעֶזְרַת ה', וַה' יִשְׁמְרֵם בְּרַחֲמָיו עַד בּוֹאָם לְשָׁם. כִּי כְּבָר נִרְשַׁם מְקוֹם קְבוּרָתִי בִּירוּשָׁלַיִם".


One day, this sage got up and spoke to the Istanbul community. He said, “It has been revealed to me that I must return to Jerusalem immediately. I see that the day is approaching for me to be taken from this world. You need not fear that the French will harm any Jew going to the Holy Land. God will protect them from all injury. He will watch over all who travel with me and we will arrive in peace. For my burial place has already been marked in Jerusalem.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 14:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:7)


וּמִיָּד שָׁמְעוּ לִדְבָרָיו, וְהָלְכוּ וְשָׂכְרוּ מִיָּד סְפִינָה גְּדוֹלָה, וְהִכְרִיזוּ: "מִי שֶׁרוֹצֶה לֵילֵךְ עַל הַסְּפִינָה יֵלֵךְ". וְהָלְכוּ הַרְבֵּה אֲנָשִׁים וְנָשִׁים וָטַף, הֵן מֵאַנְשֵׁי סְפָרַד וְהֵן מֵאַנְשֵׁי אַשְׁכְּנַז, וְנָסַע רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל גַּם־כֵּן עַל סְפִינָה זוֹ לְאֶרֶץ הַקֹּדֶשׁ.


The community heeded the sage and immediately booked a large ship. They announced that whoever wished could leave on this ship. A large number of people embarked – men, women and children both of the Sephardic and Ashkenazic communities. Among the passengers going to the Holy Land was Rebbe Nachman.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 14:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:8)


וְדִבֵּר רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל עִם הֶחָכָם מִירוּשָׁלַיִם הַנַּ"ל שֶׁיִּקַּח אוֹתוֹ לִירוּשָׁלַיִם, כִּי אָמַר שֶׁאֵינוֹ רוֹצֶה לִהְיוֹת לֹא בִּצְפָת וְלֹא בִּטְבֶרְיָה. וְכֵן הִמְתִּיקוּ סוֹד בֵּינֵיהֶם.


The Rebbe spoke to this sage and asked him to take him along to Jerusalem, saying, “I do not wish to go to Safed or Tiberias.” The two then made a pact.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 14:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:9)


וּבְלֶכְתָּם עַל הַיָּם הָיָה רוּחַ סְעָרָה גְּדוֹלָה מְאֹד מְאֹד (שֶׁקּוֹרִין אַפִּירְטִינֶע גְּדוֹלָה מְאֹד), וְהָיְתָה הַסְּפִינָה בְּסַכָּנָה גְּדוֹלָה. "יַעֲלוּ שָׁמַיִם יֵרְדוּ תְהוֹמוֹת" וְכוּ' וְלֹא הָיָה עוֹד בְּלֵב אִישׁ מֵהֶם לְהִנָּצֵל מִן הַמִּיתָה. וְהָיוּ צוֹעֲקִים כֻּלָּם אֶל ה'. וְהָיְתָה לַיְלָה אַחַת כְּמוֹ יוֹם־הַכִּפּוּרִים מַמָּשׁ, שֶׁהַכֹּל בּוֹכִים וּמִתְוַדִּים וּמְבַקְּשִׁים כַּפָּרָה עַל נַפְשָׁם. וְאָמְרוּ סְלִיחוֹת וּשְׁאָר דִּבְרֵי תְּפִלּוֹת וְתַחֲנוּנִים.


As soon as they were at sea, a great storm rose up. The ship was in great danger. They mounted to the heavens and plunged to the depths (Psalms 107:26). In their hearts, all who were aboard felt it was the end. They cried out to God, making the night like Yom Kippur with weeping, confession and begging for forgiveness and atonement.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 14:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:10)


וְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל הָיָה יוֹשֵׁב דּוֹמֵם. וְהִתְחִילוּ כַּמָּה אֲנָשִׁים לוֹמַר לוֹ: "מִפְּנֵי מָה הוּא שׁוֹתֵק בְּעֵת צָרָה כָּזֹאת?", וְלֹא הֵשִׁיב.


All the while, the Rebbe sat in absolute silence. Several people began to question him, asking how he could keep still at a time like this. The Rebbe did not reply.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 14:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:11)


אַךְ אֵשֶׁת הָרַב דִּקְהִלַּת קֹדֶשׁ חָאטִין, שֶׁהָיְתָה מְלֻמֶּדֶת וּבָכְתָה וְצָעֲקָה כָּל הַלַּיְלָה, וְהִתְחִילָה הִיא גַּם־כֵּן לוֹמַר לוֹ כָּאֵלֶּה – 'מִפְּנֵי מָה הוּא דּוֹמֵם?'. וְכִמְדֻמֶּה שֶׁקִּלֵּל אוֹתָהּ.


The Rabbi of Hottin’s wife was a learned woman. She had been crying and screaming all that night. She berated the Rebbe for remaining silent. It seems that the Rebbe cursed her.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 14:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:12)


וְאָמַר לָהּ: "הַלְוַאי הֱיִיתֶם שׁוֹתְקִים גַּם אַתֶּם! הָיָה טוֹב לִפְנֵיכֶם! וּבְזֹאת תִּבָּחֲנוּ – אִם אַתֶּם תִּשְׁתְּקוּ – יִשְׁתֹּק הַיָּם מֵעֲלֵיכֶם גַּם־כֵּן".


The Rebbe said to her, “If you will only keep still, things will go well. This shall be your test (Genesis 42:15). Remain still, and you will see. The sea shall become calm for you” (Jonah 1:12).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 14:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/14:13)


וְכֵן הָיָה, וּפָסְקוּ מִלִּצְעֹק וְשָׁתְקוּ. וַאֲזַי מִיָּד כְּשֶׁהֵאִיר הַיּוֹם "יָקֵם סְעָרָה לִדְמָמָה וַיֶּחֱשׁוּ גַּלֵּיהֶם וַיִּשְׂמְחוּ" וְכוּ'


They took the Rebbe’s advice and everyone on board kept quiet. It was near daybreak, and immediately God turned the storm into calm, and their waves were still. And they rejoiced (Psalms 107:29-30).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:1)


וְאַחַר יוֹם אוֹ יוֹמַיִם, נִשְׁאֲרָה הַסְּפִינָה בְּלִי מַיִם שֶׁרְאוּיִין לִשְׁתִיָּה. כִּי פָּסְקוּ אֶצְלָם הַמַּיִם שֶׁל שְׁתִיָּה שֶׁהָיָה עַל הַסְּפִינָה. וְלֹא נִשְׁאַר לָהֶם, כִּי־אִם כְּלִי אַחַת שֶׁל מַיִם סְרוּחִים וַעֲכוּרִים וְתוֹלָעִים הָיוּ מְרַחֲשִׁין בָּהֶם. וְאֵלּוּ הַמַּיִם חִלְּקוּ לְאַנְשֵׁי הַסְּפִינָה בַּמִּדָּה, לָאִישׁ 'אַקִי' (מִדָּה קְטַנָּה) אַחַת. וְהָיָה לָהֶם סַכָּנָה גְּדוֹלָה מֵהַצִּמָּאוֹן יוֹתֵר מִכָּל הַסַּכָּנוֹת.


After a day or two, the ship found itself without drinking water. All the water had been used up except for one barrel of putrid, bad-smelling water that was crawling with worms. This water was carefully rationed to the passengers, one measure to each person. The danger of thirst grew more serious than all previous perils.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:2)


וַאֲזַי הִתְפַּלְּלוּ אֲנָשִׁים וְנָשִׁים וָטַף מִקִּירוֹת לִבָּם. וְנִתְעוֹרֵר עוֹד הַפַּעַם רוּחַ־סְעָרָה גְּדוֹלָה, וְהֵבִיא הַסְּפִינָה אַחַר שְׁנֵי מֵעֵת־לְעֵת לָאָרֶץ הַקְּדוֹשָׁה לִסְפַר יְרוּשָׁלַיִם, לְעִיר־הַקֹּדֶשׁ יָפוֹ.


All the passengers, men, women and children, began to pray from the depths of their hearts (see “*His Wisdom*” #39). Suddenly a great wind began to blow, sweeping the ship along. Within two days, they sighted land. They had arrived at Jaffa, the port of entry to Jerusalem.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 15:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:3)


וְרָצָה רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל לֵירֵד שָׁם מֵהַסְּפִינָה, לָלֶכֶת אֶל יְרוּשָׁלַיִם עִם הֶחָכָם הַנַּ"ל, בְּצַוְתָּא חֲדָא כַּנַּ"ל. וּמֵאֵת ה' הָיְתָה סִבָּה, וְלֹא הִנִּיחוּהוּ הַיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים שֶׁיִּכָּנֵס אֲלֵיהֶם, כִּי הִסַתַּכְּלוּ עַל מַלְבּוּשָׁיו וְעַל תֹּאַר פָּנָיו שֶׁיֵּשׁ לוֹ פֵּאוֹת אֲרֻכִּים כַּנָּהוּג בִּמְדִינָתֵנוּ, וְשֶׁאֵינוֹ יוֹדֵעַ לְשׁוֹנָם וְכַיּוֹצֵא בָּזֶה, וְאָמְרוּ שֶׁהוּא בְּוַדַּאי אֶחָד מֵהַמְרַגְּלִים שֶׁל הַצָּרְפַת. וְלֹא הוֹעִילוּ שׁוּם פִּיּוּס וּבַקָּשׁוֹת. וְנִשְׁאַר רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל עַל הַסְּפִינָה.


The Rebbe wanted to leave the ship immediately and accompany the sage to Jerusalem. But God willed otherwise (cf. I Kings 12:15), and the Turks would not let him enter the country. They saw his strange clothing and long *peyot*. This, together with his inability to speak their language, convinced them that he was a French spy. Neither persuasion nor pleading availed, and the Rebbe was constrained to remain on shipboard.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 15:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:4)


וְהָיָה בְּדַעַת הַקַּפִּיטָן לְהִתְעַכֵּב שָׁם עִם הַסְּפִינָה לִהְיוֹת עוֹמֵד לָפוּשׁ אֵיזֶה יָמִים. וְאָז הָיָה שְׁנֵי יָמִים קֹדֶם רֹאשׁ־הַשָּׁנָה.


It was two days before Rosh HaShanah, and the captain intended to remain in Jaffa for at least several more days.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 15:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:5)


אַךְ הַסְּפִינָה לֹא יָכְלָה לַעֲמֹד שָׁם מֵחֲמַת גַּלֵּי הַיָּם שֶׁרָצוּ לְהָפְכָהּ, וְלֹא הוֹעִיל שׁוּם עֵצָה וְתַחְבּוּלָה לָזֶה. וְהָיָה הַדָּבָר לְפֶלֶא גָּדוֹל בְּעֵינֵי הַקַּפִּיטָן. וְשָׁאַל: "מַה זֶּה, וְעַל מַה זֶּה?" וְהֵשִׁיבוּ הַחֲכָמִים שֶׁל הַסְּפָרַדִּים: שֶׁקַּבָּלָה בְּיָדָם אִישׁ מִפִּי אִישׁ, שֶׁבְּזֶה הַמָּקוֹם נִשְׁלַךְ יוֹנָה בֶּן אֲמִתִּי הַנָּבִיא. וְלֹא יָכְלָה הַסְּפִינָה לַעֲמֹד שָׁם אוֹתָהּ הַלַּיְלָה וְהֻכְרְחוּ לָזוּז עִם הַסְּפִינָה מִשָּׁם.


Soon a heavy surf arose, rocking the ship so much that it almost capsized. The captain was helpless and soon realized that he could not remain there. He was very surprised at this, and inquired. The old Sephardic sages told him they had a tradition from father to son that this was the place where Jonah had been cast into the sea. They were forced to lift anchor, for the ship could not remain there for the night.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 15:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:6)


וְהָלְכוּ מִשָּׁם. וּבָאוּ בָּעֶרֶב לֵיל "זְכוֹר בְּרִית" לְעִיר־הַקֹּדֶשׁ חֵיפָה. וְעָמְדוּ אֵצֶל הַר הַכַּרְמֶל נֶגֶד מְעָרַת אֵלִיָּהוּ.


They left Jaffa and arrived in Haifa on the eve of *Zakhor Brit*,³¹ which are recited on that day. the day before Rosh HaShanah. The ship rested at the foot of Mount Carmel, near the Cave of Elijah.

footnotes:
³¹ “Remember the Covenant” (of Abraham). It is so called because of the Selichot


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 15:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:7)


וּבְאַשְׁמֹרֶת הַבֹּקֶר אָמְרוּ כָּל הָעָם סְלִיחוֹת בְּשִׂמְחָה גְּדוֹלָה. וְאַחַר־כָּךְ הִתְפַּלְּלוּ תְּפִלַּת שַׁחֲרִית, וְאַחַר־כָּךְ הָלְכוּ כֻּלָּם לְעִיר־הַקֹּדֶשׁ חֵיפָה אֲנָשִׁים וְנָשִׁים וָטַף.


In the early morning, all the people on the ship said *Selichot* with great joy. Then they prayed the morning service. After their prayers, everyone disembarked in Haifa.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 15:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:8)


וְאָז נִכְנַס רַבֵּנוּ הַקָּדוֹשׁ ז"ל לָאָרֶץ הַקְּדוֹשָׁה, וּבָא אֶל הַמָּקוֹם אֲשֶׁר נִכְסַף אֵלָיו, וְהִשְׁתּוֹקֵק אֵלָיו בְּהִשְׁתּוֹקְקוּת וְכִסּוּפִין וְגַעְגּוּעִים גְּדוֹלִים מְאֹד מְאֹד, וּמָסַר נַפְשׁוֹ אֲלָפִים וְרִבְבוֹת פְּעָמִים בִּשְׁבִיל זֶה, וְהִשְׁלִיךְ נַפְשׁוֹ מִנֶּגֶד עֲבוּר זֶה.


It was then that the Rebbe first set foot on the Holy Land.³² He had come to the place for which he yearned. He had thirsted for this moment with the greatest longing. For this he had risked his life thousands of times, literally casting his soul aside (Judges 9:17).

footnotes:
³² Rebbe Nachman arrived on Monday, the day before Rosh HaShanah 5559 (September 10, 1798).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 15:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:9)


וְגֹדֶל עֹצֶם הַשִּׂמְחָה שֶׁהָיָה לוֹ בְּאוֹתוֹ הָרֶגַע שֶׁנִּכְנַס וְעָמַד עַל אַדְמַת הַקֹּדֶשׁ, אִי־אֶפְשָׁר לְשַׁעֵר בַּמֹּחַ. אִלּוּ כָּל הַיַּמִּים דְּיוֹ וְכוּ' לֹא יַסְפִּיקוּ לְבָאֵר אֶפֶס קָצֶה מִזֶּה.


He set foot on the Holy Land! He was actually standing there! The tremendous joy that he experienced at that instant is beyond all imagination. If all the seas were ink (*Shabbat* 11a), it would not describe it in the least.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 15:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:10)


כִּי תֵּכֶף וּמִיָּד הִשִּׂיג מַה שֶּׁהִשִּׂיג. כִּי אָמַר: שֶׁתֵּכֶף כְּשֶׁהָלַךְ אַרְבַּע אַמּוֹת בְּאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל פָּעַל מִיָּד מַה שֶׁרָצָה לְהַשִּׂיג.


In that instant, the Rebbe attained everything. He later said, “The moment I walked four steps in the Holy Land,³³ 111a. I achieved my goal.”

footnotes:
³³ Regarding the concept of four steps, see Ketubot


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 15:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:11)


וְאַחַר חֲצוֹת הַיּוֹם הָלְכוּ לַמִּקְוֶה, וְאַחַר־כָּךְ לְבֵית־הַכְּנֶסֶת, וְנִתְמַהְמְהוּ עַד הַלַּיְלָה.


That afternoon, the Rebbe went to the *mikvah*. Then he went to the synagogue and remained there until after the evening prayers.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 15:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:12)


וּכְשֶׁבָּא רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל לְבֵיתוֹ, אֶל הָאַכְסַנְיָא שֶׁלּוֹ, הָיָה לוֹ שִׂמְחָה גְּדוֹלָה וַעֲצוּמָה עַד מְאֹד. וּבְכָל רֶגַע וָרֶגַע אָמַר לְהָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ: "אַשְׁרֶיךָ שֶׁזָּכִיתָ לְכָךְ, לִהְיוֹת עִמִּי כָּאן".


When he returned to his lodgings, the Rebbe was overflowing with joy. Every minute, he looked at his attendant and exclaimed, “You are so fortunate that you were worthy to be here with me!”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 15:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:13)


וְצִוָּה לוֹ לִקְרוֹת וּלְהַזְכִּיר לְפָנָיו מֵהַצֶּעטִיל כָּל הַנִּרְשָׁמִים שָׁם, דְּהַיְנוּ כָּל הַנִּלְוִים אֵלָיו שֶׁנָּתְנוּ לוֹ צֶעטְלִיךְ מִשְּׁמוֹתֵיהֶם. וְאָכְלוּ שָׁם סְעֻדַּת הַלַּיְלָה שֶׁל רֹאשׁ־הַשָּׁנָה, וְהַכֹּל בְּשִׂמְחָה גְּדוֹלָה עַד אַחַר הַסְּעֻדָּה עַד שֶׁשָּׁכְבוּ שָׁם לִישֹׁן.


Then the Rebbe told his attendant to read all the missives they had brought along. All the Rebbe’s followers had given him slips containing their names so that he would pray for them in the Holy Land. The entire household shared the Rebbe’s joy throughout the Rosh HaShanah meal. This joyous mood continued until they finally went to bed.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 15:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/15:14)


וּבַבֹּקֶר הָלְכוּ לְבֵית־הַכְּנֶסֶת, וְאַחַר שֶׁבָּאוּ מִבֵּית־הַכְּנֶסֶת נִתְעוֹרֵר בּוֹ דְּאָגָה וְלֵב נִשְׁבָּר מְאֹד בְּלִי־עֵרֶךְ, וְלֹא דִּבֵּר מְאוּמָה עִם שׁוּם בֶּן אָדָם.


On the morning of Rosh HaShanah, they went to the synagogue again. When they returned to their lodgings, the Rebbe’s mood changed to one of immeasurable concern and brokenheartedness (see “*His Wisdom*” #41, #42, #45). He would not speak to a soul for the rest of the holiday (cf. “*His Wisdom*” #21).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/16:1)


וְתֵכֶף אַחַר רֹאשׁ־הַשָּׁנָה, הָיָה רְצוֹן רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל לַחֲזֹר תֵּכֶף לְבֵיתוֹ. וְלֹא הָיָה רוֹצֶה לִנְסֹעַ כְּלָל לְשׁוּם מָקוֹם, לֹא לִצְפָת וְלֹא לִטְבֶרְיָה.


As soon as Rosh HaShanah was over, the Rebbe was ready to return home. He wanted to leave at once, not wanting to visit anywhere – even the holy cities of Safed and Tiberias.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/16:2)


אַךְ הָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ הָיָה כָּסְפּוֹ חָזָק מְאֹד לָזוּן עֵינָיו בִּמְקוֹמוֹת אֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל. וּבָא אֶל רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל וְאָמַר לוֹ: שֶׁיֵּשׁ שַׁיָּרָה לֵילֵךְ עִמָּם לִטְבֶרְיָה.


The Rebbe’s attendant wanted very much to travel to and feast his eyes on all the sacred shrines in the Holy Land. He told the Rebbe that there was a caravan leaving shortly, and they could join it to Tiberias.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 16:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/16:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/16:3)


וְהֵשִׁיב לוֹ: "יֵשׁ לְךָ כִּסּוּפִים לִטִבֶרְיָה, לֵךְ שְׂכֹר חֲמוֹרִים וְעָשָׂה כֵּן". וְנָתַן לָהֶם אַדְרוּף (דְּמֵי קְדִימָה). וּבַחֲזִירָתוֹ מֵהַבַּעַל חֲמוֹרִים אָמַר לוֹ תֵּכֶף: "שָׂכַרְתָּ חֲמוֹרִים?", הֵשִׁיב לוֹ: "הֵן".


The Rebbe replied, “You want Tiberias? Then go hire donkeys!” His attendant hurried to the stables and put down a deposit. The moment he returned from the stables, the Rebbe asked him if he had secured donkeys, and he answered in the affirmative.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 16:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/16:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/16:4)


אָמַר לוֹ רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: "תֵּכֶף וּמִיָּד תֵּלֵךְ וְתִקַּח מֵהֶם הָאַדְרוּף, וְאִם לֹא יִרְצוּ לְהַחֲזִיר לְךָ, אֲזַי תַּנִּיחַ לְאִבּוּד הָאַדְרוּף, כִּי אֵינִי רוֹצֶה לֵילֵךְ לְשָׁם".


Then the Rebbe said, “Go this minute! Get the deposit back! If they won’t return it, forget about it. But I will not leave now!”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 16:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/16:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/16:5)


וְאַחַר שְׁתֵּי שָׁעוֹת אוֹ שָׁלֹשׁ, נָפַל הָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ בְּחֻלְשָׁה גְּדוֹלָה רַחֲמָנָא לִצְלָן. וְאָז נָתַן שֶׁבַח וְהוֹדָאָה לה' יִתְבָּרַךְ. אֲשֶׁר מְנָעוֹ לָלֶכֶת עִם הַשַּׁיָּרָה הַנַּ"ל.


Two or three hours later, the Rebbe’s attendant became extremely ill. Then he thanked God that he had not traveled with the caravan.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:1)


אַךְ תֵּכֶף כַּאֲשֶׁר נִשְׁמַע לְאַנְשֵׁי אֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל הַיּוֹשְׁבִים בִּצְפָת וּבִטְבֶרְיָה שֶׁרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל הוּא בְּחֵיפָה, שָׁלְחוּ הַגְּדוֹלִים וְהַצַּדִּיקִים אֲשֶׁר שָׁם לְבַקֵּשׁ אֶת רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל שֶׁיִּסַּע אֲלֵיהֶם.


The people in Safed and Tiberias soon heard that the Rebbe had arrived in Haifa. Their leaders immediately sent word, asking him to visit their communities.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:2)


וּבָאוּ אַנְשֵׁי טְבֶרְיָה עַל יוֹם כִּפּוּר אֵלָיו, וְהֵבִיאוּ לוֹ אִגְּרוֹת מִגְּדוֹלֵי הַצַּדִּיקִים, שֶׁכֻּלָּם מְבַקְּשִׁים אוֹתוֹ לָבוֹא לִטְבֶרְיָה עַל חַג הַסֻּכּוֹת. וְהוּא לֹא הִשְׁגִּיחַ עַל כָּל זֶה. וְאָמַר לְהָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ, לִקְנוֹת לוֹ אֶתְרוֹגִים. וְהָלַךְ אֶל הֶחָכָם הַחוֹנֶה שָׁם, וְנָתַן לוֹ עֶשְׂרִים פָּארֶעס וְהָלַךְ אֶל שְׂדֵה יִשְׁמָעֵאל, וְהֵבִיא לוֹ שְׁלשָׁה אֶתְרוֹגִים מֻבְחָרִים וְיָפִים מְאֹד.


Emissaries from Tiberias arrived and spent Yom Kippur with the Rebbe. They had letters from all the great tzaddikim living there, requesting the Rebbe to spend Sukkot in Tiberias. The Rebbe, however, ignored the request completely. He told his attendant to buy him *etrogim* for the Sukkot festival. The attendant went to the *chakham* of Haifa and gave him twenty paras³⁴ for *etrogim*. The *chakham* went to an Arab field and brought him three beautiful, choice *etrogim*.

footnotes:
³⁴ A unit of Turkish money.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 17:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:3)


בְּתוֹךְ כָּךְ שֶׁהָיָה יוֹשֵׁב שָׁם בְּחֵיפָה, בָּא יִשְׁמָעֵאל אֶחָד רַךְ בַּשָּׁנִים וְיָשַׁב אֶצְלוֹ וְדִבֵּר אֵלָיו דִּבּוּרִים הַרְבֵּה וְלֹא יָדַע מַה הוּא אוֹמֵר. וְכֵן הָיָה אֵצֶל כָּל סְעֻדָּה וּסְעֻדָּה, בַּיּוֹם וּבַלַּיְלָה, וְהָיָה מְחַבֵּב אוֹתוֹ מְאֹד.


While the Rebbe was in Haifa, a mysterious event took place. A young Turk came to his lodgings and sat there, speaking to the Rebbe at length. Since he spoke in Turkish, the Rebbe could not understand a word he was saying. The Arab was there for each meal, day and night. From his actions, it seemed that he was very fond of the Rebbe.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 17:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:4)


וּפַעַם אַחַת בָּא אֵלָיו בִּכְלִי זַיִן בְּכַעַס גָּדוֹל וְהִתְחִיל לִזְעֹק וְלִצְעֹק עָלָיו. וְהוּא לֹא יָדַע מַה הוּא מְדַבֵּר. אַךְ אִשָּׁה אַחַת מִמְּדִינַת וָאלִיחַאי הָיְתָה שָׁם, וְתֵכֶף כְּשֶׁהָלַךְ הַיִּשְׁמָעֵאל אָמְרָה אֶל רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: "לְמַעַן הַשֵּׁם, שֶׁיִּבְרַח מִבַּיִת זֶה, כִּי זֶה הַיִּשְׁמָעֵאל אוֹמֵר שֶׁהוּא ז"ל יֵלֵךְ עִמּוֹ לִלְחֹם" שֶׁקּוֹרִין 'פֶעכְטִין'.


One day, the Turk came to the Rebbe fully armed and in a state of rage. He began to scream at the Rebbe in Turkish. The Rebbe sat there dumbfounded, not understanding a word he was saying. But a woman from Walachia was in the house and she understood Turkish. As soon as the Turk left, she told the Rebbe, “For God’s sake, flee from this house! This Turk has just challenged you to a duel!”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 17:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:5)


וְתֵכֶף בָּרַח הוּא ז"ל לְבֵית הֶחָסִיד הַמְפֻרְסָם מוֹרֵנוּ הָרַב זְאֵב מִטְשָׁארְנִי אוֹסְטְרְהָא הַנַּ"ל וְהִצְנִיעוּ אוֹתוֹ בְּחַדְרֵי חֲדָרִים.


The Rebbe flew from the house and hid in the home of Rabbi Zev of Charni. They concealed him in an innermost chamber.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 17:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:6)


וְאַחַר־כָּךְ בָּא הַיִּשְׁמָעֵאל הַנַּ"ל עוֹד הַפַּעַם לְבֵית אַכְסַנְיָא שֶׁל רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, וְאָמַר: "הֵיכָן הָאִישׁ הַלָּז? יְדִיעַ לֶהֱוֵי לֵהּ שֶׁאֲנִי אוֹהֵב אוֹתוֹ מְאֹד, אֲנִי אֶתֵּן לוֹ הַחֲמוֹרִים וְגַם הַסּוּס שֶׁלִּי שֶׁיֵּלֵךְ עִם הַשַּׁיָּרָה לִטְבֶרְיָה וְשׂוּב אֵין לוֹ לְהִתְפַּחֵד מִמֶּנִּי כְּלָל מֵעַתָּה".


The Turk soon returned to the Rebbe’s quarters, looking for him. He said, “Where is the man who was here? Tell him that I am very fond of him. If he wants to go with the caravans to Tiberias, I will give him donkeys. I will even give him my own horse! From now on, he has nothing to fear from me!”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 17:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:7)


וְכֵן הָיָה, שֶׁבָּא רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל אֶל אַכְסַנְיָא שֶׁלּוֹ, וּבָא הַיִּשְׁמָעֵאל וְלֹא דִּבֵּר לוֹ כְּלוּם רַק שָׁתַק וְשָׂחַק. וְאַחַר־כָּךְ חִבֵּב אוֹתוֹ זֶה הַיִּשְׁמָעֵאל מְאֹד בְּאַהֲבָה יְתֵרָה.


So it was. The Rebbe returned to his quarters and the Turk came and sat there, not saying a word. He just sat quietly and smiled. Then he began to act friendly to the Rebbe, displaying an unusual degree of affection.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 17:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:8)


וְעִנְיָן זֶה שֶׁל הַיִּשְׁמָעֵאל, הָיָה פְּלִיאָה גְּדוֹלָה. וְאָמַר רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: שֶׁהָיָה לוֹ יִסּוּרִים מֵהָאַהֲבָה שֶׁל הַיִּשְׁמָעֵאל יוֹתֵר מִן הַשִּׂנְאָה וְהַכַּעַס שֶׁלּוֹ. וּכְפִי הַנִּשְׁמַע מִפִּיו הַקָּדוֹשׁ, שֶׁהָיָה בְּעִנְיַן מַעֲשֶׂה זוֹ שֶׁהָיָה לוֹ עִם הַיִּשְׁמָעֵאל סַכָּנָה גְּדוֹלָה, וְכִמְדֻמֶּה שֶׁנִּשְׁמַע מִפִּיו הַקָּדוֹשׁ שֶׁאָמַר שֶׁזֶּה הַיִּשְׁמָעֵאל הָיָה הַס"מ בְּעַצְמוֹ.


The entire episode was extremely mysterious. The Rebbe said, “I suffered even more from that Turk’s love than from his hatred and anger.” From the Rebbe’s holy words, it appeared that his dealings with that Turk had placed him in great peril. It also seems that we heard the Rebbe say this Turk was the Evil One himself.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 17:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/17:9)


וּבָרוּךְ ה' שֶׁנִּצַּל מִמֶּנּוּ בְּשָׁלוֹם בְּחַסְדֵּי ה'.


Whatever the case, God had mercy and protected the Rebbe. He was saved [and left] in peace, thanks to God’s kindness.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:1)


וּבְחֹל־הַמּוֹעֵד סֻכּוֹת הָלְכוּ כָּל הָעוֹלָם, וְגַם רַבֵּנוּ הָלַךְ עִמָּהֶם, לַמְּעָרָה שֶׁל אֵלִיָּהוּ הַנָּבִיא. וְשָׁם עָשׂוּ כָּל הָעוֹלָם שִׂמְחָה גְּדוֹלָה וְרִקּוּדִין וּמְחוֹלוֹת. וְהוּא לֹא הָיָה שָׂמֵחַ כְּלָל. רַק יָשַׁב שָׁם בְּהַכְנָעָה גְּדוֹלָה וּבְלֵב נִשְׁבָּר.


During *Chol HaMoed* Sukkot, the entire community went to the Cave of Elijah, where they celebrated with singing and dancing. The Rebbe went along but did not take part in the festivity. He merely sat there with a broken heart, appearing to be in a very dejected state.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:2)


וְשָׁאַל הָרַב רַבִּי זְאֵב הַנַּ"ל אֶת הָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ: "מַה זֶּה וְעַל מַה זֶּה, שֶׁהוּא בְּעַצְבוּת מִן רֹאשׁ־הַשָּׁנָה עַד עַתָּה? ה' יוֹדֵעַ אִם טוֹב הַדָּבָר".


Rabbi Zev went to the Rebbe’s attendant and asked him, “What is this all about? Your master has been depressed since Rosh HaShanah. God only knows if this is good.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:3)


וְגַם בְּשִׂמְחַת תּוֹרָה עָשׂוּ הַקָּפוֹת בְּרִקּוּדִין וְשִׂמְחָה כַּנָּהוּג, בִּפְרָט שֶׁהָיוּ שָׁם אֲנָשִׁים חֲסִידִים.


On Simchat Torah, the congregation made the customary rounds in the synagogue, dancing joyfully with the Torah scrolls. The mood was especially jubilant because of the many chassidim there.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:4)


וְהוּא ז"ל הָיָה גַּם־כֵּן בְּבֵית־הַכְּנֶסֶת, וְלֹא רָצָה לַעֲשׂוֹת שׁוּם הַקָּפָה, רַק יָשַׁב שָׁם בְּהַכְנָעָה וּבִכְפִיפַת רֹאשׁ.


The Rebbe was in the synagogue, but would not even carry a Torah. He just sat there with his head bent, looking utterly dejected.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:5)


(כַּאֲשֶׁר דַּרְכּוֹ הָיָה כֵּן עַל־פִּי הָרֹב קֹדֶם שֶׁזָּכָה לִפְעֹל מַה שֶּׁהָיָה רוֹצֶה בְּכָל־עֵת וּבְכָל שָׁעָה, שֶׁהָיָה לוֹ לֵב נִשְׁבָּר מְאֹד, בְּכָל פַּעַם וְכוּ' כַּאֲשֶׁר יְבֹאַר לְקַמָּן).


[This was always the Rebbe’s way. Each time he wanted to accomplish something important, it was preceded by an extremely brokenhearted mood. This happened very often (cf. “*His Praises*” #6).]


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:6)


וְאַחַר שִׂמְחַת תּוֹרָה אָמַר לְהָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ: "בָּרוּךְ ה' פָּעַלְתִּי מַה שֶּׁרָצִיתִי עַל צַד הַיּוֹתֵר טוֹב וְנָכוֹן, וְאַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן הָיָה בְּדַעְתִּי לְהִתְמַהְמֵהּ כָּאן מֵחֲמַת חִבּוּב אֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל, וְעַכְשָׁו בִּרְצוֹנִי לִנְהֹג נְסִיעוֹת לְבָתֵּינוּ לְחוּץ לָאָרֶץ. עַל־כֵּן לֵךְ וּשְׂכֹר הַסְּפִינָה לִסְטַנְבּוּל".


After Simchat Torah, the Rebbe told his attendant, “Thank God, I have attained my goal. Everything has been accomplished with the greatest degree of perfection. I was going to stay a while because of my love of the Land, but now I have changed my mind. Go book passage for us back to Istanbul. I am ready to return home today!”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:7)


וְלֹא אָבָה הָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ לִשְׁמֹעַ אֵלָיו, וּמֵאֵן לִנְסֹעַ לְבֵיתוֹ, כִּי אָמַר שֶׁרְצוֹנוֹ לִהְיוֹת בִּטְבֶרְיָה וְכוּ'.


The Rebbe’s attendant refused to listen. He said, “I am not yet ready to return. I still want to see Tiberias and the other holy places.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:8)


וְהֵשִׁיב לוֹ רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: "מֵאַחַר שֶׁאַתָּה מִתְאַוֶּה לִהְיוֹת בִּטְבֶרְיָה, לֵךְ וּשְׂכֹר חֲמוֹרִים לְשָׁם". כִּי דַּרְכּוֹ הָיָה לִבְלִי לְהִתְעַקֵּשׁ הַרְבֵּה עַל שׁוּם דָּבָר וְכוּ'.


The Rebbe replied, “If you really must see Tiberias, then hire donkeys and we will go.” This was always the Rebbe’s way; he was never stubborn about anything.³⁵ #119, #430-435; *Yemey Moharnat* #56; *Avenehah Barzel*, p. 18, #15.

footnotes:
³⁵ Cf. Tzaddik


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:9)


וְשָׂכַר חֲמוֹרִים לְעִיר־הַקֹּדֶשׁ טְבֶרְיָה, וּבָאוּ לְשָׁם לְעֵת עֶרֶב. וְכָל הַלַּיְלָה הָלְכוּ אַנְשֵׁי טְבֶרְיָה לְקַבֵּל פָּנָיו, זֶה נִכְנָס וְזֶה יוֹצֵא. וְהַרְבֵּה הִלְבִּישׁוּ עַצְמָם בְּבִגְדֵי שַׁבָּת לִכְבוֹדוֹ וְלֹא הָיָה יָשֵׁן כָּל הַלַּיְלָה מֵחֲמַת זֶה.


They got the donkeys and traveled to Tiberias, arriving there in the evening. All that night, the people of Tiberias went to meet the Rebbe. As soon as one would leave, another would come. Many dressed in their Shabbat garments in honor of the Rebbe. Because of this, he did not sleep all that night.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:10)


וּבִתְחִלָּה נִתְאַכְסֵן בְּבֵית שְׁאֵר־בְּשָׂרוֹ, נִין וָנֶכֶד שֶׁל הָרַב הַקָּדוֹשׁ מוֹרֵנוּ הָרַב נַחְמָן מֵהָרִידֶענְקֶא ז"ל. אַךְ רַבִּים אָמְרוּ שֶׁצְּרִיכִין לִרְאוֹת בִּשְׁבִילוֹ בַּיִת גָּדוֹל שֶׁיִּהְיֶה לוֹ הַרְחָבַת הַדַּעַת. וּמִיָּד הִסְכִּימוּ שֶׁיִּתְאַכְסֵן בְּבֵית הָרַבָּנִי הַמְפֻרְסָם הַוָּתִיק וְהֶחָסִיד מוֹרֵנוּ הָרַב צְבִי הַרְקֶער, וְנִכְנַס לְשָׁם.


When the Rebbe first came to Tiberias, he stayed with a cousin who was also a grandson of Rabbi Nachman Horodenker. However, the community felt that a larger house would provide him with more peace of mind. It was agreed that the Rebbe should stay in the home of the saintly Rabbi Zvi Harker.³⁶ (Kehot, N.Y., 1971), p. 229, 303 (note 13).

footnotes:
³⁶ Rabbi Zvi Hirsch ben Avraham of Horky (or Gorky). One of the leaders of the Tiberias community, he was later involved in the dispute between Rabbi Shneur Zalman of Liadi and Rabbi Abraham of Kalisk. See Nissan Mindel, Rabbi Shneur Zalman of Liadi


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:11)


וְשָׁלַח אֵלָיו הָרַב הַקָּדוֹשׁ הַמְפֻרְסָם מוֹרֵנוּ הָרַב אַבְרָהָם קַלִּיסְקֶר ז"ל: בַּאֲשֶׁר שֶׁיּוֹם הֲקָזָה הוּא אֶצְלוֹ, עַל־כֵּן הוּא מִן הַנִּמְנָע שֶׁיֵּלֵךְ אֶצְלוֹ לִתֵּן לוֹ שָׁלוֹם. וְהֵשִׁיב רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: שֶׁבְּלֹא זֶה הָיָה בְּדַעְתּוֹ לֵילֵךְ אֵלָיו. וּמִיָּד הָלַךְ לְהַצַּדִּיק הַנַּ"ל, וְקִבְּלוֹ בְּכָבוֹד גָּדוֹל מְאֹד מְאֹד וּבְאַהֲבָה יְתֵרָה וּבְחִבָּה גְּדוֹלָה מְאֹד מְאֹד. וְגֹדֶל הָאַהֲבָה וְהַחִבּוּב שֶׁהָיָה לְהָרַב הַקָּדוֹשׁ מוֹרֵנוּ הָרַב אַבְרָהָם הַנַּ"ל עִם כְּבוֹד רַבֵּנוּ הַקָּדוֹשׁ, ז"ל, אִי־אֶפְשָׁר לְבָאֵר וּלְסַפֵּר.


Rabbi Abraham Kalisker, head of the Chassidic community in the Holy Land, lived in Tiberias at the time. He sent a message to the Rebbe saying that he had just received a bloodletting treatment and could not come to greet him. The Rebbe answered that he had already intended to go to Rabbi Abraham. He went immediately and Rabbi Abraham greeted him with great affection and respect. The bond of friendship that was forged between the two is beyond all description.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:12)


וְשִׁחֵר פָּנָיו שֶׁיִּתְאַכְסֵן אֶצְלוֹ, וְהֵשִׁיב רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: שֶׁזֶּה אִי־אֶפְשָׁר שֶׁיַּעֲמֹד בִּקְבִיעוּת אֶצְלוֹ. אַךְ עַל שַׁבָּת אַחַת יִהְיֶה אֶצְלוֹ. וּבְיוֹם מָחָר שָׁלַח בִּשְׁבִילוֹ. וְקִבְּלוֹ עַל שַׁבָּת הָרִאשׁוֹן פָּרָשַׁת נֹחַ.


Rabbi Abraham pleaded with the Rebbe to stay with him. The Rebbe answered that he could not move in permanently, but would spend one Shabbat there. The very next day, Rabbi Abraham sent a message inviting the Rebbe for the next Shabbat, the Shabbat of Noah.³⁷

footnotes:
³⁷ The Torah reading that recounts the story of Noah and the flood (Genesis 6:9-11:32). This was 3 Cheshvan (October 13, 1798).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:13)


וּבְלֵיל שַׁבָּת כָּפַף רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל אֶת ראֹשׁוֹ שֶׁיְּבָרֵךְ אוֹתוֹ, וְקָפַץ אַרְבַּע אַמּוֹת מִמֶּנּוּ לַאֲחוֹרָיו בִּרְעָדָה גְּדוֹלָה. וְדִבֵּר בְּהִתְלַהֲבוּת גָּדוֹל וְלֹא הָיָה אֶפְשָׁר לְהָבִין מַה שֶּׁאָמַר. אַךְ בְּסוֹף דְּבָרָיו שָׁמְעוּ שֶׁאָמַר: "אֵיךְ אָנוּ מִתְבַּיְּשִׁים בִּפְנֵי זֶרַע הַבַּעַל־שֵׁם־טוֹב ז"ל".


On Friday night, the Rebbe bowed his head for Rabbi Abraham to bless him. Rabbi Abraham jumped back, trembling violently. He began to speak with such great excitement that it was impossible to understand him. His final words, however, were clear: “How abashed we are before a descendant of the holy Baal Shem Tov.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:14)


וְלֹא רָצָה לְבָרֵךְ אוֹתוֹ. אַךְ אֶת הָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִם רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל בֵּרַךְ מִיָּד כְּשֶׁכָּפַף ראֹשׁוֹ.


Rabbi Abraham absolutely refused to bestow his blessing on the Rebbe. However, as soon as the Rebbe’s attendant bowed his head, Rabbi Abraham gave him the blessing.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:15

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:15](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:15)


וְהָיָה שָׁם בִּשְׁעַת הַסְּעֻדָּה שִׂמְחָה גְּדוֹלָה וּבִקֵּשׁ הַצַּדִּיק הַנַּ"ל אֶת רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל שֶׁיֹּאמַר תּוֹרָה. וְלֹא רָצָה בְּשׁוּם אֹפֶן, וְאָמַר הַצַּדִּיק הַנַּ"ל בְּעַצְמוֹ תּוֹרָה. וְכֵן הָיָה בִּסְעֻדָּה שְׁנִיָּה וּשְׁלִישִׁית.


The meal was the scene of great rejoicing. Rabbi Abraham asked the Rebbe to deliver the Torah lesson, but he absolutely refused. Rabbi Abraham then said the lesson himself. The same thing took place at the Shabbat morning meal and again at *seudah shelishit*.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:16

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:16](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:16)


וַאֲמִירַת הַתּוֹרָה שֶׁל הַצַּדִּיק מוֹרֵנוּ הָרַב אַבְרָהָם הַנַּ"ל הָיָה בְּהִתְלַהֲבוּת גָּדוֹל וּבִצְעָקָה גְּדוֹלָה וְלֹא נִשְׁמַע שׁוּם דִּבּוּר. רַק לְבַסּוֹף סִיֵּם: "כָּךְ הוּא עִקַּר עֲבוֹדַת הַבּוֹרֵא יִתְבָּרַךְ". וְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל הָיָה מְשַׁבֵּחַ וּמְפָאֵר הַתּוֹרָה שֶׁלּוֹ בְּלִי עֵרֶךְ, כִּמְעַט שֶׁאֵין דֻּגְמָתוֹ.


Rabbi Abraham delivered the lesson with fiery enthusiasm, shouting so loudly that it was impossible to understand his words. Again, only his last words could be heard: “This is the root of devotion to God.” The Rebbe praised his lesson beyond all measure, saying that it was second to none.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 18:17

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:17](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/18:17)


גַּם אָנֹכִי שָׁמַעְתִּי מִפִּיו הַקָּדוֹשׁ שֶׁאָמַר: "שֶׁשְּׁלֵמוּת הָיָה רַק לְהַצַּדִּיק מוֹרֵנוּ הָרַב אַבְרָהָם" הַנַּ"ל. וְאָמַר לִי: "הֲלֹא רָאִיתִי כַּמָּה צַדִּיקִים, אֲבָל שְׁלֵמוּת הוּא רַק אֵצֶל הַצַּדִּיק הַקָּדוֹשׁ הַנַּ"ל".


We also heard this from the Rebbe’s holy lips. He said, “Perfect sincerity only exists in the tzaddik, Rabbi Abraham.” Another time, he said, “I have seen many tzaddikim. But I have seen perfection only in this holy man” (cf. *Tzaddik* #553).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:1)


וְאַחַר שַׁבָּת, בְּיוֹם רִאשׁוֹן, בָּא הַצַּדִּיק מוֹרֵנוּ הָרַב אַבְרָהָם הַנַּ"ל אֶל רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל לְאַכְסַנְיָא שֶׁלּוֹ, וְדִבְּרוּ בְּיִחוּד שְׁנֵיהֶם.


That Sunday, Rabbi Abraham went to visit the Rebbe. The two conversed for a long time.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:2)


וְאַחַר יוֹם אוֹ יוֹמַיִם נָפַל רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל עַל עֶרֶשׂ דְּוַי רַחֲמָנָא לִצְלָן, וְשָׁלַח עַל פִּדְיוֹן לְהַצַּדִּיק הַנַּ"ל. וּבְכָל יוֹם וָיוֹם הָיָה הוֹלֵךְ הָרַב מוֹרֵנוּ הָרַב אַבְרָהָם הַנַּ"ל לְבַקְּרוֹ.


After a day or two, the Rebbe became extremely ill. He sent a *pidyon*³⁸" #175. to Rabbi Abraham, asking that he pray for him. As long as the Rebbe was sick, Rabbi Abraham visited him daily.

footnotes:
³⁸ Literally, "redemption." A small sum of money given as symbolic payment for a prayer. See "His Wisdom


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:3)


אַחַר־כָּךְ הָיָה שָׁם הַמַּעֲשֶׂה הַמְפֻרְסָם שֶׁל הַמָּסוֹר שֶׁהָיָה שָׁם.


The Rebbe’s illness caused him to be directly involved in an episode with an informer.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:4)
