וְהַמָּסוֹר הָיָה אֶחָד מֵאַנְשֵׁי חוּץ־לָאָרֶץ שֶׁבָּאוּ לְשָׁם וְנַעֲשָׂה מָסוֹר אֵצֶל הַפֶּעשָׁא וְעָשָׂה רָעוֹת גְּדוֹלוֹת לְאַנְשֵׁי טְבֶרְיָה וְתָפַס אֶת גְּדוֹלֵי הָעִיר בַּשִּׁבְיָה, וְתָפַס אוֹתָם בַּבּוֹר תִּשְׁעָה שָׁבוּעוֹת. וְאַחַר־כָּךְ נָסְעוּ אַנְשֵׁי פְרֶענְקֶען (הַסְּפָרַדִּים) אֶל הַפֶּעשָׁא וְנָתְנוּ לוֹ הוֹן רַב וּפָטַר אוֹתָם, וְהָלְכוּ, וְתָפְסוּ אֶת הַמָּסוֹר, וְרָצוּ לַחֲנֹק אוֹתוֹ, וְעָשָׂה עַצְמוֹ כְּאִלּוּ כְּבַר נִגְוַע וָמֵת.


This individual had recently arrived in the Holy Land and had become an informer for the pasha, causing much hardship for the Tiberias community. He had had the community leaders arrested and held in prison for nine weeks. Some Sephardic Jews went to the pasha and paid a large ransom to free these prisoners. They captured the informer and attempted to strangle him. He was able to escape only by feigning death.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:5)


וְאַחַר־כָּךְ נִתְעוֹרֵר וְהָלַךְ וּמָסַר אוֹתָם, עַד שֶׁנָּתַן לוֹ הַפֶּעשָׁא רְשׁוּת שֶׁיַּעֲשֶׂה עִם כָּל הָעִיר כִּרְצוֹנוֹ. וְעָשָׂה אוֹתוֹ לְפָקִיד. וּבָא אֶל הָעִיר בְּכָבוֹד גָּדוֹל עִם אַנְשֵׁי חַיִל יִשְׁמְעֵאלִים בִּגְדֻלָּה וּבְכָבוֹד, וַאֲזַי תֵּכֶף וּמִיָּד בָּרְחוּ כָּל הָעִיר וְלא נִשְׁאַר כִּי־אִם נָשִׁים וָטַף וְהָיָה בְּכִיּוֹת גְּדוֹלוֹת בְּכָל הָעִיר, וְהָיְתָה צְעָקָה גְּדוֹלָה וּבְכִיּוֹת רַבּוֹת בְּכָל בַּיִת וּבַיִת.


He complained to the pasha and was appointed as overseer of the city, to do as he pleased. Accompanied by a regiment of Turkish troops, he returned to Tiberias with great fanfare. When the Jewish community saw the troops, they fled the city. They were so terrorized that they did not even wait for the women and children, leaving them crying bitterly in the city. Every house was filled with great weeping.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:6)


וְגַם רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל רָצָה לִבְרֹחַ, אַךְ לֹא הָיָה יָכֹל מֵחֲמַת חֻלְשָׁתוֹ, וּכְבָר הִתְחִיל לָצֵאת מִן הָעִיר, אַךְ חָזַר אַחַר־כָּךְ. וּמֵאֵת ה' הָיְתָה זֹאת, כִּי עַל־יְדֵי רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל חָזְרוּ כֻּלָּם לְתוֹךְ הָעִיר, כִּי הַמָּסוֹר הִכְנִיעַ עַצְמוֹ מְאֹד תַּחַת רַגְלֵי רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל. וְהָלַךְ לְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל וְהִכְנִיעַ עַצְמוֹ מְאֹד תַּחְתָּיו.


The Rebbe wanted to flee with them, but was prevented by his infirmity. He made an attempt to leave the city, but had to return. This was all God’s doing (Psalms 118:23), for it resulted in the entire community being able to return. It turned out that the Rebbe was able to control this informer completely. When the informer went to the Rebbe, he literally humbled himself before the Rebbe’s feet.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:7)


וְעִנְיַן הַהַנְהָגָה, שֶׁנָּהַג עִם הַמָּסוֹר הַנַּ"ל, תִּקְצַר הַיְרִיעָה לְסַפֵּר, כִּי נָהַג עִמּוֹ בְּחָכְמָה נִפְלָאָה עַד שֶׁהַמָּסוֹר נִתְבַּטֵּל נֶגְדּוֹ. וְצִוָּה לְהָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִם רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל שֶׁיַּעֲשֶׂה כְּרוּז שֶׁיַּחַזְרוּ כָּל הַבּוֹרְחִים, וְחָזְרוּ כֻּלָּם עַל־יְדֵי־זֶה לְתוֹךְ הָעִיר.


The entire story would consume many volumes. In short, the Rebbe made use of his great intelligence, totally subjugating the informer. By the time he was finished with him, he was able to tell his attendant to announce that all who had fled could return. In this way, the Rebbe’s illness resulted in everyone returning to the city.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:8)


וְרַבֵּנוּ דִבֵּר עִמּוֹ בְּחָכְמָה, וְאָמַר לוֹ: שֶׁנָּאֶה וְיָפֶה לְפָקִיד שֶׁיִּתְפַּלֵּל עַל צָרַת יִשְׂרָאֵל, וְשֶׁיִּהְיֶה חָסִיד וְעָנָו. כִּי הַמָּסוּר הַנַּ"ל הָיָה צָבוּעַ, וְדִמָּה עַצְמוֹ כְּאִלּוּ הוּא חָסִיד. וְהִתְחִיל לְדַבֵּר עִם רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל עִנְיְנֵי חֲסִידוּת וְהוּא ז"ל עָשָׂה עַצְמוֹ כְּלֹא יָדַע, עַד שֶׁנִּדְמָה לְהַמָּסוֹר כְּאִלּוּ הוּא ז"ל מַחֲזִיק אוֹתוֹ גַּם־כֵּן לְחָסִיד.


The people later found out what had happened. The informer was a complete hypocrite, acting as if he were most pious. As soon as he met the Rebbe, he began to speak about religious matters. The Rebbe pretended that he knew no better, so that it was actually he who tricked the informer. The informer was greatly impressed with the Rebbe’s wisdom. The Rebbe then told him, “A good overseer should pray to God, asking him to help his Jewish brethren. He should be a pious, humble person.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:9)


וְדִבֵּר עִמּוֹ בְּחָכְמָה וְעָרְמָה, וְדִבֵּר עַל לִבּוֹ שֶׁיַּרְגִּיל אֶת עַצְמוֹ לוֹמַר תְּהִלִּים, עַד שֶׁאָמַר לוֹ רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: "בְּזֹאת אֵדַע שֶׁאֵין בְּךָ גֵּאוּת, אִם תּוּכַל לוֹמַר תְּהִלִּים בְּבֵיתִי לִפְנֵי בְּנֵי הַנְּעוּרִים בִּבְכִיָּה גְּדוֹלָה".


The Rebbe continued to impress the informer with his wisdom and cleverness. He began to speak about prayer, convincing the informer that he should make a habit of chanting the Psalms with great humility. The informer assured the Rebbe that he would heed this advice, but the Rebbe acted very skeptical. Finally, he said, “If you want to convince me that you are truly a humble and pious man, this is what you must do: Gather the young boys in my house, and in their presence, chant the Psalms with great weeping.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:10)


וְכֵן הָיָה, וְהִתְחִיל מִיָּד לוֹמַר תְּהִלִּים בִּבְכִיָּה גְּדוֹלָה וְדִמְעָתוֹ עַל לֶחְיוֹ. וְאַחַר־כָּךְ נָפַל הַמָּסוֹר לְחֻלְשָׁה.


This was soon accomplished, and the informer began to chant the Psalms with tears literally rolling down his cheeks (cf. Lamentations 1:2). Suddenly he felt faint, and became extremely ill.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:11)


וְאַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן הָיוּ שׁוֹמְרִים עַל כָּל הַשְּׁעָרִים לִשְׁמֹר אִם יָבוֹאוּ מְשֻׁלָּחִים. כִּי זֶה הָיָה מְסִירָתוֹ, שֶׁאָמַר: שֶׁבָּא הוֹן רַב מִכָּאן לְהָתָם. אַךְ ה' הָיָה בְּעֶזְרָם וְהֶעֱלִימוּ הַדָּבָר מֵהַמָּסוֹר הַנַּ"ל וְשָׁלְחוּ אֶת הָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִם רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל לְחֵיפָה, וְקִבֵּל הוּא אֶת הַמָּעוֹת מֵהַמְשֻׁלָּחִים וְהוֹלִיכָם לִטְבֶרְיָה וּנְתָנָם לְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, וְהוּא נָתַן לָהֶם, וְחִלְּקוּ.


Now, this informer had told the pasha that the Jews were bringing a large sum of money from Europe. The Turks had posted special watchmen at all the city gates, waiting for these emissaries. It was only with God’s help that they were prevented from learning the details of the transaction. The Rebbe’s attendant was later sent to Haifa, where he collected the funds from the emissaries and brought the money to Tiberias. The money was then delivered to the Rebbe, who gave it to the community leaders for distribution.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:12)


וְכַאֲשֶׁר עָמַד הַמָּסוֹר מֵחֻלְשָׁתוֹ, הִתְפָּאֵר אֶת עַצְמוֹ שֶׁיַּעֲשֶׂה לָהֶם עוֹד רָעוֹת, וְהָיָה סוֹפוֹ שֶׁנָּפַל עוֹד הַפַּעַם לְחֻלְשָׁה וָמֵת.


When the informer later recovered, he boasted that he would cause the community even more evil than before. He suddenly fell ill again, but this time he did not recover. He died soon after.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:13)


וְהָיָה לְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל שִׂמְחָה גְדוֹלָה, עַל שֶׁנִּתְגַּלְגֵּל עַל יָדוֹ זְכוּת כָּזֶה, כִּי עַל יָדוֹ חָזְרוּ כֻּלָּם לְתוֹךְ הָעִיר. וּבַחֲזִירָתָם לְשָׁם שָׂמְחוּ עִמּוֹ כֻּלָּם בְּיַחַד.


When the people returned to Tiberias, they all came to the Rebbe and made a great celebration. The community was safe again, and the Rebbe was very happy that he had had the merit to bring this about.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:14)


וְהָלַךְ רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, שָׁם עַל קֶבֶר זְקֵנוֹ רַבִּי נַחְמָן ז"ל. וְגַם שָׂכְרוּ חֲמוֹרִים וְנָסְעוּ עַל כָּל הַמְּעָרוֹת.


The Rebbe then went to visit the grave of his grandfather, Rabbi Nachman Horodenker, who is buried in Tiberias.³⁹ 71b, p. 35), but others give it as 5520/1760, right after the Baal Shem Tov’s passing. It is known that Rabbi Nachman was with the Baal Shem at the time of his passing (*Shevachey HaBaal Shem Tov*, p. 160). Rabbi Nachman had visited Israel one time before, and left his son Shimshon there when he returned to Europe (ibid., p. 126). His tombstone cites the anniversary of his passing as 2 Tamuz, but no year is given. There is a tradition that he passed away on a Shabbat soon after returning to Israel the second time (ibid., p. 110). We also know that our Rebbe Nachman was named after him, so he must have passed away before the latter's birth in 5532/1772. The only year that 2 Tamuz fell on a Shabbat was 5521/1761, so this must be given as the year of his passing. He obtained donkeys and visited the other holy tombs there.

footnotes:
³⁹ Rabbi Nachman Horodenker immigrated to Israel together with Rabbi Menachem Mendel of Premishlan. One source gives the date of this pilgrimage as 5525/1765 (Tevor HaAretz


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:15

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:15](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:15)


וּבְבוֹאָם לִמְעָרַת רַבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן בֶּן יוֹחַאי, אֲזַי בְּנֵי הַנְּעוּרִים עָסְקוּ בִּתְפִלָּה וּבְלִמּוּד זֹהַר כַּאֲשֶׁר פָּקַד עֲלֵיהֶם הוּא ז"ל. וּמִמֶּנּוּ לֹא רָאוּ שׁוּם דָּבָר, רַק הָיָה שָׂמֵחַ עַד מְאֹד. וּבְכָל־עֵת בָּא אֵצֶל הָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ וְאָמַר לוֹ: "אַשְׁרֶיךָ" וְכוּ'.


He traveled to Meron and visited the cave where Rabbi Shimon bar Yochai, author of the *Zohar*, is buried. The Rebbe told all the young men accompanying him to engage in prayer and study of the *Zohar*, which they did with great enthusiasm. But the Rebbe himself appeared to be doing nothing, just standing in the cave, glowing with joy. Every few minutes, he went over to his attendant and said, “Fortunate are you.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:16

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:16](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:16)


וּבַלַּיְלָה הָלַךְ מֵחֶדֶר לְחֶדֶר, וְהִזְהִיר אוֹתָם לוֹמַר זֹהַר וְכוּ' וְכַיּוֹצֵא. וְהוּא בְּעַצְמוֹ לֹא אָמַר כְּלוּם, רַק הָיָה הוֹלֵךְ וּמְנַגֵּן בְּעַצְמוֹ וְהָיָה בְּשִׂמְחָה גְּדוֹלָה עַד אוֹר הַיּוֹם. וּכְשֶׁהֵאִיר הַיּוֹם עָמַד בְּטַלִּית וּתְפִלִּין וְהִתְפַּלֵּל כַּמָּה שָׁעוֹת.


That night, they went from room to room in the cave. Again the Rebbe cautioned those who were with him to recite passages from the *Zohar*. However, the Rebbe himself did not say a thing. He merely walked around very happily, humming to himself until daybreak. Putting on his *talit* and *tefilin*, he then stood and prayed for several hours.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:17

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:17](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:17)


וּמִשָּׁם נָסַע עִמָּהֶם לִמְעָרַת הִלֵּל וְשָׁם אָמַר גַּם הוּא קַפִּיטְל ל"ג ול"ד. וְשָׁם גַּם כֵּן הָיָה בְּשִׂמְחָה גְּדוֹלָה.


From there, they all went to the cave where Hillel⁴⁰ (Pairs) that presided over the Sanhedrin during the late Second Temple era. Hillel was the *Nasi* (Prince) who headed the Sanhedrin, while Shammai was the *Av Bet Din* (head of the rabbinical court). Hillel was noted for his kindness and Shammai for his strictness (see *Shabbat* 31a). is buried. The Rebbe displayed great joy, chanting Psalms 33 and 34⁴¹ along with the others.

footnotes:
⁴⁰ Hillel and Shammai (mentioned in the next paragraph) were leaders of the Jewish people in the first century B.C.E. They were the last of the Zugot
⁴¹ These two Psalms are customarily recited at Hillel’s grave. Hillel’s school was humble and righteous, the theme of Psalm 33. The initial letters of each verse of Psalm 34 follow the Hebrew alphabet, signifying that Hillel taught every letter of the Torah.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:18

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:18](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:18)


וּמִשָּׁם הָלְכוּ לְקֶבֶר שַׁמַּאי, שָׁם נָפַל עָלָיו עַצְבוּת. וְאָמַר: שֶׁתְּמִיהָה גְּדוֹלָה אֶצְלוֹ זֹאת. וְאַחַר־כָּךְ אָמַר שֶׁיֵּשׁ תֵּרוּץ עַל זֶה, וְלֹא גִּלָּה הַתֵּרוּץ.


Then they went to the grave of Shammai, where the Rebbe began to act very depressed. At first, he was very surprised at his mood, but later said that he understood its reason. The reason, however, was never revealed.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:19

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:19](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:19)


וּמִשָּׁם נָסְעוּ לִשְׁאָר מְעָרוֹת. וּבְבוֹאָם לָהָר הַגָּדוֹל, שֶׁיֵּשׁ שָׁם עַל רֹאשׁ הָהָר מְעָרַת הַתַּנָּא רַבִּי כְּרוֹסְפַּדַּאי, וְלֹא הָיָה בְּאֶפְשָׁרִי לֵילֵךְ עִם הַחֲמוֹר לְשָׁם, וְקָפַץ מֵעַל הַחֲמוֹר וְהָלַךְ הוּא בְּעַצְמוֹ עִם הָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ. וְהָלַךְ כִּמְעַט עַל יָדָיו וְעַל רַגְלָיו עַד בּוֹאוֹ לִמְעָרָה זוֹ.


They also traveled to the other tombs. Approaching a very high mountain, they were informed that there was a cave near its peak where the *Tanna*, Rabbi Kruspedai,⁴² #37, the grave of Rabbi Kruspedai is described as being on the top of a mountain, south of the road running east of Safed. This is the Rabbi Kruspedai mentioned in the *Zohar* III, 164b, and *Tikkuney Zohar* #70, 136a. *Seder HaDorot* (p. 86b) identifies him with Rabbi Kruspedai, the son of Rabbi Shabsai, mentioned in *Niddah* 46a. He is not to be confused with the disciple of Rabbi Yochanan having the same name, who is mentioned in *Rosh HaShanah* 16b and *Keritot* 24a. is buried. They attempted the climb, but it was too steep for the donkeys. The Rebbe jumped down from his animal and practically climbed on his hands and knees to the top. His attendant followed close behind, and the two finally reached the cave.

footnotes:
⁴² In Sha'ar HaGilgulim


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:20

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:20](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:20)


וְשָׁם נִשְׁתַּהָה קְצָת וְחָזְרוּ מִשָּׁם לִמְעָרָה אַחֶרֶת. וְהָיוּ עַל מְעָרָה שֶׁל יַנּוּקָא אֶחָד.


After staying there a while, they went down the mountain. Then the group went to another cave, where a holy child was buried.⁴³. Rabbi Chaim ben Atar, author of *Or HaChaim*, identifies him with the child of Rabbi Hamnuna the Elder mentioned in the *Zohar* III, 186 (Rabbi Avraham Yishmael Chai Sangotini, *Ageres Masa* [1742], published by Benjamin Klar in *Eretz HaChaim* [Vienna, 1933], pp. 20-27; Zev Vilnai, *Matzevot Kodesh BeEretz Yisrael* [Jerusalem, 1963], p. 410). This cave is on the way to that of the Prophet Hosea (*Yemey Moharnat* II, #142).

footnotes:
⁴³ A holy child mentioned in the Zohar


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:21

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:21](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:21)


וּבְאוֹתָהּ הַמְּעָרָה הָיוּ הַכֹּל יְרֵאִים לִכְנֹס, כִּי אָמְרוּ שֶׁנָּחָשׁ כָּרוּךְ שָׁם בְּאוֹתָהּ הַמְּעָרָה. גַּם אִילָן גָּדוֹל הָיָה עַל הַמְּעָרָה זוֹ. וְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל הָיָה הָרִאשׁוֹן שֶׁנִּכְנַס לְשָׁם, וְלֹא הָיָה שָׁם נָחָשׁ כְּלָל. וּמֵאָז וָהָלְאָה הִתְחִילוּ כֻּלָּם לֵילֵךְ בְּאוֹתָהּ הַמְּעָרָה.


The cave of the holy child was like a deep pit in the ground. There was a rumor that a snake lived in the cave, and people were afraid to enter it. A large tree grew next to the mouth of the cave, and the Rebbe climbed down by one of its roots, ignoring all warnings (cf. *Tzaddik* #35). He was the first one ever to enter the cave, and didn't find any snake at all inside. From that time on, people were no longer afraid to go into the cave and it was visited by all.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:22

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:22](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:22)


וְאַחַר־כָּךְ הָלְכוּ עַל שְׁאָר מְעָרוֹת עַד שֶׁחָזְרוּ לִטְבֶרְיָה.


From there, they visited some other tombs in Meron and then returned to Tiberias.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:23

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:23](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:23)


וְאָז צִוָּה רַבֵּנוּ לְהָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ: שֶׁיִּסַּע לְחֵיפָה לִשְׂכֹּר סְפִינָה לְחוּץ לָאָרֶץ. וּבְבוֹאוֹ לְשָׁם לִשְׂכֹּר, בְּתוֹךְ כָּךְ בָּא סְפִינָה עִם הָרַב הַגָּאוֹן הֶחָסִיד הַמְפֻרְסָם דִּקְהִלַּת שְׁפִּיטוּבְקֶע וְגַם שְׁלוּחֵי דְּרַבָּנָן.


Soon after they returned, the Rebbe told his attendant to go to Haifa and obtain passage on a ship heading home. The attendant went to do his bidding, but as soon as he came to Haifa, a ship arrived carrying the renowned Rabbi of Shepetovka,⁴⁴ along with a group of emissaries returning from Europe.

footnotes:
⁴⁴ Rabbi Shimshon Yaakov of Shepetovka, a leading disciple of Rabbi Dov Ber, the Maggid of Mezeritch, and a follower of Rabbi Pinchas of Koretz and Rabbi Baruch of Medzeboz. He settled in Tiberias and passed away there on 3 Nisan 5561 (March 17, 1801).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:24

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:24](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:24)


וְאָז הָיְתָה הַמַּעֲשֶׂה שֶׁהָיוּ יְרֵאִים לְקַבֵּל בְּעַצְמָם הַמָּעוֹת מֵחֲמַת הַמָּסוֹר הנ"ל, וְהֻכְרְחוּ לִמְסֹר הַמָּעוֹת הַכֹּל לְיַד הָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִם רַבֵּנוּ וְכוּ' כַּנַּ"ל. אֲזַי הֻכְרַח הָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל לַחֲזֹר לִטְבֶרְיָה עִם הַמָּעוֹת.


It is at this time that the episode mentioned above occurred. The emissaries heard that the informer had told the pasha about them, and that troops were on the lookout for foreign Jews carrying money. Meeting the Rebbe’s attendant, they decided it would be best to send the money with him. Because of this, the Rebbe’s attendant had to return to Tiberias without securing passage.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:25

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:25](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:25)


וּבְבוֹאוֹ לִטְבֶרְיָה אָמַר רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: "מִן הַסְּתָם רְצוֹן הַשֵּׁם שֶׁנִּהְיֶה בְּאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל עוֹד אֵיזֶה זְמַן".


When his attendant returned, the Rebbe said simply, “It is probably God’s will that we remain in the Holy Land a while longer.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:26

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:26](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:26)


וְאָמַר רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: שֶׁיִּשְׂכְּרוּ לוֹ חֲמוֹרִים לֵילֵךְ לִירוּשָׁלַיִם עִיר־הַקֹּדֶשׁ. וּכְשֶׁשָּׁמַע זֹאת הַצַּדִּיק הַקַּלִּיסְקֶר בִּקֵּשׁ מֵאִתּוֹ שֶׁלֹּא יֵלֵךְ.


The Rebbe instructed his attendant to obtain donkeys for a journey to Jerusalem. When Rabbi Abraham heard that he was leaving, he asked the Rebbe to remain in Tiberias a while longer.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:27

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:27](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:27)


וְכַאֲשֶׁר בָּא הַצַּדִּיק הַשְּׁפִּיטוּבְקֶער לִטְבֶרְיָה עָשָׂה סְעֻדָּה גְּדוֹלָה בַּלַּיְלָה עֲבוּר רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, וְחִבֵּב אוֹתוֹ מְאֹד. וְדִבְּרוּ בְּיִחוּד, וְלֹא שָׁמַע שׁוּם אָדָם מַה שֶּׁדִּבְּרוּ יַחַד. וְעָשָׂה רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל שָׁלוֹם בֵּין הָרַב הַקָּדוֹשׁ דִּשְׁפִּיטוּבְקֶע וּבֵין הָרַב הַקָּדוֹשׁ הַקַּלִּיסְקֶר.


The Rabbi of Shepetovka soon arrived in Tiberias. One of the first things he did was to make a large feast in honor of the Rebbe. He acted very friendly toward him and they conversed for a long time. Their conversation was absolutely private, and no one knows what went on between them. But soon after that, the Rebbe made peace between the Rabbi of Shepetovka and Rabbi Abraham Kalisker.⁴⁵

footnotes:
⁴⁵ The Rabbi of Shepetovka had been sent by Rabbi Baruch to look after the charity monies dispatched to the Holy Land. Some aspersions had been cast on Rabbi Abraham’s handling of these funds (see above, note 46), so he understandably resented the newcomer.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:28

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:28](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:28)


וְאַחַר זְמַן קָצָר נָפַל שָׁם דֶּבֶר בָּעִיר, וְהֻכְרַח לִבְרֹחַ מֵאַכְסַנְיָא שֶׁלּוֹ לְאַכְסַנְיָא אַחֶרֶת. וְאַחַר־כָּךְ נָפַל שָׁם הַדֶּבֶר גַּם־כֵּן, וְהֻכְרַח לִבְרֹחַ מִטְּבֶרְיָה.


A short time later, an epidemic broke out in Tiberias, forcing the Rebbe to flee from his quarters. As soon as he secured lodgings in a new area, the plague broke out there too. Then the Rebbe had to flee from Tiberias altogether.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:29

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:29](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:29)


וּבְרִיחָתוֹ מִטְּבֶרְיָה בְּעֵת הַדָּבָר רַחֲמָנָא לִצְלָן הָיָה בְּסַכָּנָה גְּדוֹלָה מְאֹד, כִּי הַשַּׁעַר שֶׁל הַחוֹמָה הָיָה סָגוּר, וְהָיָה מֻכְרָח לִבְרֹחַ דֶּרֶךְ הַמְּעָרָה עַד שֶׁבָּא אֶל הַחוֹמָה אֵצֶל שְׂפַת כִּנֶּרֶת. וְרָצָה לַעֲלוֹת עַל הַחוֹמָה וְלֵירֵד מִשָּׁם כְּדֵי לָצֵאת עַל־יְדֵי־זֶה מִן הָעִיר.


The flight from Tiberias involved tremendous peril. The city gate was sealed because of the plague and the only exit was through a cave. After crawling through this cave, the Rebbe found his way blocked by a high wall.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:30

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:30](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:30)


וַיְהִי כַּאֲשֶׁר עָלָה עַל הַחוֹמָה וְעָבַר וּבָא עַל צַד הַשֵּׁנִי כְּלַפֵּי חוּץ, הִסְתַּכֵּל וְהִנֵּה הַיָּם תַּחְתָּיו וְהוּא הָיָה תָּלוּי וְעוֹמֵד עַל יָדָיו וְעַל רַגְלָיו עַל הַחוֹמָה לְמַעְלָה וְתַחְתָּיו הָיָה הַיָּם, וְנָשָׂא לִבּוֹ לה' יִתְבָּרַךְ, וְהִצִּילוֹ בְּרַחֲמָיו.


The Rebbe climbed the wall and slid over it, thinking he had escaped. But when he looked down, he saw water beneath him. There he was, hanging by his hands and feet with nothing but the sea below him. He had only the most meager grip and was sure that he would fall and drown. At this moment, he lifted his heart to God, praying with all his might. In His mercy, God rescued him (see “*His Wisdom*” #117).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 19:31

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:31](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/19:31)


וְיָצָא מִשָּׁם בְּשָׁלוֹם עַד שֶׁבָּא לָעִיר הַקְּדוֹשָׁה צְפָת, וְיָשְׁבוּ שָׁם יָמִים אֲחָדִים עַד שֶׁנִּשְׁמַע שֶׁהַצָּרְפַת יָבוֹא בְּקָרוֹב לְעַכּוֹ.


Finally, the Rebbe left Tiberias and reached Safed. He was there for just a few days when news arrived that the French were about to invade Acre.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:1)


וּכְשֶׁשָּׁמַע זֹאת, שָׁלַח שָׁלִיחַ מְיֻחָד לְעַכּוֹ לִשְׂכֹּר הַסְּפִינָה מֵרֶגַאז שֶׁהִיא בְּשָׁלוֹם עִם כָּל הָאֻמּוֹת. הַיְנוּ כִּי יֵשׁ מְדִינַת רֶגַאז וְיֵשׁ לָהֶם שָׁלוֹם עִם כָּל הַמְּלָכִים, וּכְשֶׁאוֹתָהּ הַסְּפִינָה מוֹצִיאָה דֶּגֶל שֶׁלָּהּ אֲזַי אֵין לוֹקְחִין לְתוֹךְ הַפְּלֶען. כִּי דֶּרֶךְ הַסְּפִינוֹת שֶׁל מִלְחָמָה שֶׁחוֹטְפִין עַל הַיָּם סְפִינָה עִם הָאֲנָשִׁים שֶׁבָּהּ לְתוֹךְ הַפְּלֶען דְּהַיְנוּ בְּשִׁבְיָה, אֲבָל כְּשֶׁהַסְּפִינָה הַנַּ"ל מִמְּדִינַת רֶגַאז מוֹצִיאָה דֶּגֶל שֶׁלָּהּ, אֲזַי אֵין לוֹקְחִין מֵהֶם לְתוֹךְ הַפְּלֶען.


As soon as the Rebbe heard this, he sent someone to Acre to obtain passage on a ship flying the flag of Ragusa.⁴⁶ in the Hebrew. Ragusa was a community in southeast Sicily with a flourishing maritime trade. Its flag was respected by the British, who were notorious for their acts of impressment both during the Napoleonic wars and before the War of 1812. It is reported that on February 5, 1799, after being cut off from Europe for five months, Napoleon finally received a letter on a Ragusan wine ship that had slipped through the British blockade (see J.M. Thomson, *Napoleon Bonaparte* (Oxford, N.Y., 1952), p. 138. This may have led to the rumor that these ships were safe. There are also indications that *Regez* was Dubrovnik, a city-state in Yugoslavia (see *Until the Mashiach*, p. 45). He knew that Ragusa was not involved in the war between the French and the Turks, and as a neutral nation, ships flying its flag were not subject to impressment. The Rebbe was quite aware of the practice of impressment, in which passengers on enemy ships were captured and forced into military service.

footnotes:
⁴⁶ Regez

וְכֵן הָיָה שֶׁשָּׂכְרוּ לוֹ סְפִינָה כָּזוֹ. וּכְשֶׁשָּׁמַע הָרַב הַצַּדִּיק מוֹרֵנוּ הָרַב אַבְרָהָם הַנַּ"ל שֶׁרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל רוֹצֶה לִנְהֹג נְסִיעוֹת לְבֵיתוֹ, שָׁלַח שָׁלִיחַ מְיֻחָד אֵלָיו עִם אִגַּרְתּוֹ וְגַם אִגֶּרֶת הַכּוֹלְלוֹ. וְהוּא ז"ל זֵרַז אֶת עַצְמוֹ מְאֹד וְלֹא רָצָה לְהַמְתִּין עַל אִגֶּרֶת שֶׁל הָרַב הַגָּאוֹן דִּקְהִלַּת שְׁפִּיטוּבְקֶע.


The Rebbe’s envoy was successful in obtaining passage on a ship from Ragusa. News that the Rebbe was attempting a voyage home soon reached Tiberias. Rabbi Abraham immediately sent a special messenger to Safed with his own personal letter, as well as one from the community as a whole. A letter was also dispatched from the Rabbi of Shepetovka, but the Rebbe was in too great a hurry to wait for it.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:3

וַיֵּצֵא מִשָּׁם בְּעֶרֶב־שַׁבַּת־קֹדֶשׁ פָּרָשַׁת זָכוֹר. וְהָלְכוּ כָּל הַלַּיְלָה עַד שָׁלֹשׁ שָׁעוֹת קֹדֶם קַבָּלַת שַׁבָּת. אָז בָּאוּ לַסְּפָר שֶׁל עַכּוֹ.


On Thursday evening before *Shabbat Zakhor*,⁴⁷ – Remember what Amalek did to you" (Deuteronomy 25:17) is read. The practice of "remembering Amalek" is one of the 613 *mitzvot* of the Torah. In the year of Rebbe Nachman's pilgrimage, *Shabbat Zakhor* fell on 9 Adar II (March 16, 1799). Napoleon had captured Jaffa on March 7 and began marching on Acre on March 14. He arrived there on March 20, just a day after Rebbe Nachman had left. the Rebbe left Safed with his attendant. They traveled all that night, arriving in Acre just three hours before Shabbat began.

They found their agent and sent him to make arrangements for them to board the ship on which they had booked passage. He returned with the news that their ship was already filled beyond capacity and it would be impossible for them to board.

וְהֻכְרְחוּ רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל עִם הָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ לִכְנֹס לְתוֹךְ הָעִיר עַכּוֹ לִשְׁבֹּת שָׁם פָּרָשַׁת זָכוֹר. וְהָלְכוּ לְהַגְבִּיר שֶׁהָיָה לָהֶם אִגֶּרֶת אֵלָיו מֵהָרַב הַקַּלִּיסְקֶר הַנַּ"ל, וְנִתְאָרְחוּ אֶצְלוֹ. וְשָׁם נָפַל עֲלֵיהֶם פַּחַד גָּדוֹל מְאֹד מְאֹד מֵחֲמַת שֶׁהָיָה שָׁם בְּעֶרֶךְ חֲמִשָּׁה־עָשָׂר אֶלֶף אַנְשֵׁי חַיִל יִשְׁמְעֵאלִים וְיוֹתֵר עִם כָּל הַשַּׁיָּךְ לָהֶם לְתַכְסִיסֵי מִלְחָמָה, וְסָגְרוּ הַדֶּלֶת שֶׁל חוֹמַת הָעִיר.


The Rebbe and his attendant realized they would have to spend *Shabbat Zakhor* in Acre. They had a letter of introduction from Rabbi Abraham to a wealthy man who lived there, and were able to spend Shabbat with him. When they heard the news that over fifteen thousand Turkish troops were stationed in Acre, they became quite alarmed. Later, they learned that the gates of the city had been sealed, which filled them with great apprehension.

In the middle of the Shabbat morning prayer service, the agent burst into the synagogue with the news that it was impossible to find another Ragusan ship, only Turkish merchant ships carrying cargo to Istanbul. War would break out in another two or three days. Remaining in the city involved great peril, for many people were sure to be killed in the battle.

The agent said to the Rebbe, “It is better to risk the danger at sea than to remain here. Why risk your life? Permit me to obtain passage on a ship for you right now! Where life is in danger, Shabbat may be ignored” (*Shabbat* 132a; *Pesachim* 83a).

The Rebbe agreed, and the agent paid a gold dinar as deposit for their passage on a Turkish trading vessel. Meanwhile, many British troopships arrived in Acre.⁴⁸ and *Tigre*, and the frigate, *Alliance*. Commander Smith led the defense of Acre and eventually forced Napoleon to retreat on May 17. The city became crowded with troops, and the terror of the civilians knew no bounds.

That Shabbat was one of fear and apprehension. The entire household spent the day praying, pouring out their hearts to God. The Rebbe and his attendant felt some degree of security because they had obtained passage to leave the city. But they were also distressed, since events had proceeded so rapidly that they had not had time to secure food and provisions for their voyage.

They spoke about their previous journey, when they had had everything they needed on the ship. At that time, they had carried the customary provisions – enough for four or five months at sea. How different would it be now, when they would have to set forth without a crumb!

On Sunday morning, the Rebbe sent his attendant to the agent, asking that he attempt to secure the customary provisions for the voyage. The agent and the attendant went to the market, but not a thing was to be bought.

וְחָזַר לָאַכְסַנְיָא. וְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל עָמַד אָז מְעֻטָּף בְּטַלִּית וּתְפִלִּין וְלֹא דִּבֵּר אִתּוֹ מְאוּמָה. וְהָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל נָפַל וְנִרְדַּם מִגֹּדֶל הַצַּעַר וְהָאֵימָה, וְרַבֵּנוּ לֹא יָדַע שֶׁהוּא בַּבַּיִת.


The attendant returned to the Rebbe and found him wearing his *talit* and *tefilin*, totally engrossed in prayer. The attendant was so upset and frightened that he collapsed and fell unconscious. The Rebbe did not even know that he had returned.

בְּתוֹךְ כָּךְ נַעֲשָׂה הָרַעַשׁ הַגָּדוֹל שָׁם, כִּי בָּא הַפְּקֻדָּה מֵהַפֶּעשָׁא שֶׁהוּא נוֹתֵן אַרְכָּא רַק שְׁתֵּי שָׁעוֹת, מִי שֶׁאֵינוֹ יוֹדֵעַ תַּכְסִיסֵי מִלְחָמָה יִרְאֶה לִבְרֹחַ עַל הַיָּם, כִּי אֵין שׁוּם שַׁעַר פָּתוּחַ לָצֵאת מִן הָעִיר כִּי־אִם דֶּרֶךְ הַיָּם. וּמִי שֶׁיִּמָּצֵא תּוֹךְ הָעִיר אַחַר שְׁתֵּי שָׁעוֹת יִשְׁחֲטוּ אוֹתוֹ שָׁם.


Meanwhile, a great cry arose in the city. An order had come from the pasha giving all civilians a two-hour time limit to leave the city. The city gates would remain closed; all civilians were to attempt to leave by sea. Anyone found in the city after the two-hour limit would be killed.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:14)


כִּי הַיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים שֶׁל הָעִיר הָיוּ רוֹצִים לַעֲשׂוֹת הָרְחָבָה בְּתוֹךְ הָעִיר שֶׁלֹּא יִהְיֶה לָהֶם דֹּחַק כָּל־כָּךְ מֵאֲנָשִׁים הַרְבֵּה, עַל־כֵּן רָצוּ לִשְׁחֹט אֶת הַנִּמְצָאִים שָׁם לְפַנּוֹת לָהֶם מָקוֹם שֶׁלֹּא יִהְיֶה לָהֶם דֹּחַק כָּל־כָּךְ.


The Rebbe soon learned the reason for this. The city was very overcrowded, and the Turkish troops needed room to maneuver. In order to make room, they were prepared to slaughter everyone in the city.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:15

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:15](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:15)


וְהָיָה שָׁם רַעַשׁ גָּדוֹל וּצְעָקָה גְּדוֹלָה בָּעִיר, וּפַחַד וְאֵימָה גְּדוֹלָה מְאֹד, וּבִשְׁעַת הָרַעַשׁ הַגָּדוֹל בָּרָח רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל מִשָּׁם עַל הַיָּם, וְלֹא הִסְתַּכֵּל כְּלָל עַל הָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ וְהוּא הָיָה נִרְדָּם כַּנַּ"ל.


The city became a seething mass of terror. Screams and wails filled the streets. In the midst of all this commotion, the Rebbe made his way to the sea. He was not even aware of his attendant, who was still lying unconscious.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:16

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:16](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:16)


אַחַר־כָּךְ נִתְעוֹרֵר הָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל וְרָאָה שֶׁרַבֵּנוּ אֵינוֹ בַּבַּיִת וְהִמְתִּין עַד בּוֹשׁ, וְאַחַר־כָּךְ הָלַךְ לֶחָצֵר, וְרָאָה וְשָׁמַע שֶׁאֲנָשִׁים וְנָשִׁים וָטַף צוֹעֲקִים וּבוֹכִים בִּמְרִירוּת גָּדוֹל מְאֹד וַתַּעַל שַׁוְעַת הָעִיר הַשָּׁמַיִם.


When the attendant regained consciousness, he realized that the Rebbe was no longer in the house. He waited as long as he could and then went out to the courtyard. He saw men, women and children in the streets, screaming, weeping and wailing with great bitterness. The cry of the city seemed to rise to the very heavens (cf. I Samuel 5:12).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:17

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:17](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:17)


וְהַגְּבִירָה שֶׁל הַבַּיִת כְּשֶׁרָאֲתָה אוֹתוֹ אֶת הָאִישׁ שֶׁל רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל בָּאת אֶצְלוֹ וְאָמְרָה לוֹ: "אוֹי וַאֲבוֹי, חָס אֲנִי עַל נְעוּרֵיכֶם שֶׁאַתֶּם תִּהְיוּ נֶהֱרָגִים פֹּה, אֲנַחְנוּ קִבַּלְנוּ עַל־כָּל־פָּנִים טוֹבָה כָּאן, עַכְשָׁו אָנוּ צְרִיכִים לְקַבֵּל גַּם־כֵּן מַה שֶּׁנִּגְזַר עַל הַמָּקוֹם הַזֶּה, אֲבָל עֲלֵיכֶם רַחֲמָנוּת גָּדוֹל כִּי תֹּאבְדוּ פֹּה מִמִּשְׁפְּחוֹתֵיכֶם" וְכוּ' וְכַיּוֹצֵא בַּדְּבָרִים הָאֵלֶּה.


Their host’s wife saw him and ran over to him, screaming, “Woe! A pity on your youth! You will be killed here! At least we had some good here. Now we must accept the bad with the good. But all the more pity on you. You have had nothing here, and now you will be lost to your family!”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:18

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:18](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:18)


וְנָפַל עָלָיו חֲרָדָה גְּדוֹלָה עַד שֶׁלֹּא הָיָה לוֹ כֹּחַ לְמַשְׁמֵשׁ בְּאֶצְבְּעוֹתָיו. וְשָׁאַל אוֹתָהּ עַל רַבֵּנוּ, וְהֵשִׁיבָה: "כִּמְדֻמֶּה לִי שֶׁהוּא בָּרַח עַל הַיָּם".


As she continued to wail in this manner, the attendant became more and more terrified. He was numb with fear and could not move a finger. Finally, he summoned enough presence of mind to ask her about the Rebbe. She replied, “I imagine he has fled to the sea.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:19

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:19](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:19)


וְהוּא כְּשֶׁשָּׁמַע זֹאת, רְצוֹנוֹ הָיָה לִקַּח הַתֵּבָה עִם הַמָּעוֹת וְהַחֲפָצִים שֶׁלָּהֶם, כִּי רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל בָּרַח שָׁם לְבַדּוֹ בְּלִי שׁוּם דָּבָר. וְלֹא יָכֹל הָאִישׁ לְבַדּוֹ לָשֵׂאת אֶת הַתֵּבָה.


As soon as he heard this, the attendant realized the Rebbe must have fled empty-handed, since their chest was still in the house. That chest contained all their money and possessions, and they could not leave without it. He tried to lift it, but it was obvious he could not carry it by himself.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:20

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:20](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:20)


וְהִזְמִין לוֹ הַשֵּׁם אֵיזֶה פְרֶנְק וְנָתַן לוֹ הַתֵּבָה עִם הַחֲפָצִים וּבָרַח עִמּוֹ לַיָּם. וּמֵחֲמַת הַמְּהוּמָה הַגְּדוֹלָה שֶׁהָיְתָה בָּעִיר כִּמְעַט שֶׁאֶחָד תָּקַע סַכִּינוֹ בַּחֲבֵרוֹ, לֹא הָיָה בְּאֶפְשָׁרִי לֵילֵךְ בָּעִיר.


Suddenly a Jew appeared and offered to carry the chest. Together, they fled toward the sea. But soon they found it impossible to move through the city because of the great confusion. Troops were surging through the city, ready to stab anyone who got in their way.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:21

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:21](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:21)


אַךְ בְּחֶמְלַת הַשֵּׁם הָיְתָה סִבָּה מֵאֵת הַשֵּׁם, שֶׁבְּאוֹתָהּ הַשָּׁעָה עָמְדוּ יִשְׁמְעֵאלִים עַל גַּג הַחוֹמָה וְהִכְרִיזוּ עַל אֵיזֶה בְּשׂוֹרָה טוֹבָה. וְנִתְקַבְּצוּ אֲלֵיהֶם הַרְבֵּה אֲנָשִׁים. וּמֵחֲמַת זֶה הָיָה לוֹ דֶּרֶךְ לַעֲבֹר בָּעִיר לֵילֵךְ אֶל הַיָּם.


But here again God had mercy and arranged things for the best. Suddenly the Turks standing on top of the wall announced that they had a good report. The people pressed around the wall to listen, and a lane opened to get through the city to the sea.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:22

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:22](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:22)


וְכַאֲשֶׁר בָּא הָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל אֶל הַיָּם רָאָה הַרְבֵּה סְפִינוֹת וְלֹא רָאָה אֶת רַבֵּנוּ שָׁם. וְהָיָה מְבַקֵּשׁ וּמְחַפֵּשׂ מְאֹד בְּחִפּוּשׂ אַחַר חִפּוּשׂ עַד שֶׁמָּצָא אֶת רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל שֶׁיּוֹשֵׁב בִּסְפִינָה וְהַסְּפִינָה מְלֵאָה מִיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים וְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל יָשַׁב בָּאֶמְצַע. וּכְשֶׁנִּתְוַעֲדוּ יַחַד רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, עִם הָאִישׁ שֶׁלּוֹ הַנַּ"ל, הָיָה בֵּינֵיהֶם שִׂמְחָה גְּדוֹלָה. כִּי מִתְּחִלָּה הָיָה לָהֶם צַעַר גָּדוֹל מְאֹד מְאֹד מֵחֲמַת שֶׁלֹּא יָדְעוּ זֶה מִזֶּה.


When the attendant arrived at the docks, he saw a confusion of ships and could not find the Rebbe. He looked up and down, searching one place after another. Finally, he came to a small sailboat filled with Turks. When he looked more closely, he saw that there, sitting in their midst, was the Rebbe. The Rebbe and his attendant were overjoyed to be together again. They had both experienced considerable anguish, each not knowing where to find the other.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:23

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:23](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:23)


וְהָיוּ יוֹשְׁבִים תְּחִלָּה בַּסְּפִינָה הַקְּטַנָּה הַנִּקְרָא 'בַּרְקֶס'. אַחַר־כָּךְ הָלְכוּ עִם סְפִינָה זוֹ עַד שֶׁבָּאוּ אֶל הַסְּפִינָה הַגְּדוֹלָה הַנִּקְרֵאת 'אָקְרֶענְט' שֶׁהָיְתָה עוֹמֶדֶת סָמוּךְ לְחֵיפָה. וְרָאוּ שֶׁהַסְּפִינָה מְסֻבֶּבֶת עִם הוֹרְמַאטֶעס וְהָיָה לָהֶם לְפֶלֶא.


The small boat eventually left the port and brought them to a large cruiser anchored in the harbor near Haifa. At first, they were very surprised to see their ship bristling with cannon. They discussed this and came to the conclusion that, because of the war, even the merchant ships were carrying weapons.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:24

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:24](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:24)


אַךְ אָמְרוּ בְּלִבָּם: 'שֶׁמֵּחֲמַת שֶׁעֵת מִלְחָמָה הוּא, עַל־כֵּן גַּם הַסְּפִינָה שֶׁל הַסּוֹחֲרִים הוֹלֶכֶת גַּם־כֵּן עִם הוֹרְמַאטֶעס'. וּבֶאֱמֶת סְפִינָה זוֹ הָיְתָה סְפִינָה שֶׁל מִלְחָמָה, עִם אַנְשֵׁי חַיִל וּכְלֵי זַיִן.


There had been so much confusion when the Rebbe was fleeing that he did not have time to get further information from the agent. He had no idea where his ship was, and somehow was led to believe that this small boat would bring him to it. Little did he know that he was being brought to a Turkish warship!


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:25

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:25](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:25)


וּבְוַדַּאי הוּא סַכָּנָה גְּדוֹלָה וַעֲצוּמָה מְאֹד לְהִתְוַעֵד עִמָּהֶם יַחַד, בִּפְרָט עִם אַנְשֵׁי חַיִל יִשְׁמְעֵאלִים שֶׁהֵם פֶּרֶא אָדָם וּבִפְרָט לִפְרֹשׁ עִמָּהֶם עַל הַיָּם. וְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל בִּשְׁעַת בְּרִיחָתוֹ בְּעֹצֶם הָרַעַשׁ לֹא כִּוֵּן לִכְנֹס בַּסְּפִינָה שֶׁשָּׂכְרוּ לוֹ, כִּי לֹא יָדַע הֵיכָן הוּא, וְנִכְנַס בִּשְׁעַת בֶּהָלָה בִּסְפִינָה זוֹ. וְלֹא יָדַע כְּלָל שֶׁהִיא סְפִינָה שֶׁל מִלְחָמָה.


Even as they boarded, they had no idea of their great peril. They were actually walking onto a warship loaded with troops and weapons. The Turkish troops were unpredictable enough on land. To travel with them by sea was to take one’s life into one's hands.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:26

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:26](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:26)


וְלָקְחוּ אוֹתָם הַיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים, וְהִכְנִיסוּ אוֹתָם אֶל הַסְּפִינָה הַגְּדוֹלָה וְנָתְנוּ לָהֶם חֶדֶר מְיֻחָד. וְגַם נָתְנוּ לְהָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל שֶׁהָיָה עִם רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל קְנֵה שְׂרֵפָה טְעוּנָה, וְלֹא הָיוּ יְכוֹלִים לִשְׁאֹל אוֹתָם דָּבָר כִּי לֹא יָדְעוּ הַלָּשׁוֹן.


The Turks helped them on board and gave them a private room. Not knowing the language, they had no way of communicating with the Turks. But they certainly realized that something was wrong when the Rebbe’s attendant was handed a loaded gun.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:27

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:27](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:27)


וְלֹא הָיָה לָהֶם שׁוּם מַאֲכָל וּמִשְׁתֶּה בַּסְּפִינָה. וְאַחַר שְׁתַּיִם אוֹ שָׁלֹשׁ שָׁעוֹת בַּלַּיְלָה סָגְרוּ הַדֶּלֶת מֵהַחֶדֶר וְשָׁכְבוּ לִישֹׁן. וְלֹא אָכְלוּ מְאוּמָה כִּי לֹא הָיָה לָהֶם מַה לֶּאֱכֹל כַּנַּ"ל.


They sat in their room without a thing to eat or drink. After two or three hours, they closed their door and tried to get some sleep.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:28

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:28](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:28)


וְאַחַר שָׁעָה אוֹ שְׁתַּיִם, פָּתַח אֶחָד אֶת הַדֶּלֶת וְאָמַר לָהֶם בִּלְשׁוֹן נָכְרִים שֶׁל מְדִינָתֵנוּ: "מָה אַתֶּם עוֹשִׂים כָּאן?! הֲלֹא זֹאת הִיא סְפִינָה שֶׁל מִלְחָמָה וְאַתֶּם תֹּאבְדוּ פֹּה ח"ו. הֲלֹא אַתֶּם רוֹאִים שֶׁהַסְּפִינָה מְלֵאָה סָבִיב עִם הוֹרְמַאטֶעס טְעוּנִים".


Another hour or two passed, when suddenly their door was opened and a voice said to them in Russian, “What are you doing here? Don’t you realize this is a warship and you can get killed here? Didn’t you see the loaded cannon all around?”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:29

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:29](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:29)


וְנִתְעוֹרְרוּ מִשְּׁנָתָם וְחָטְפוּ אוֹתוֹ בְּבִגְדוֹ וְהָיָה הַבֶּגֶד מִגִּיוַואנְט (בַּד פָּשׁוּט) אַךְ צוּרָתוֹ לֹא רָאוּ.


They were thoroughly aroused from their sleep, and grabbed the stranger by his sleeve. They could not see his face in the dark, but felt that his jacket was made of linen.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:30

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:30](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:30)


וּבִקְּשׁוּ אוֹתוֹ שֶׁיַּעֲזֹר לָהֶם שֶׁיַּנִּיחוּ אוֹתָם לָצֵאת מֵהַסְּפִינָה עַל הַסְפָר שֶׁל אֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל, כִּי כְּבָר רָאוּ שֶׁהֵם בְּסַכָּנָה קְרוֹבִים לְמִיתָה מֵחַיִּים. כִּי רָאוּ שֶׁפָּרְחוּ הַקּוֹילִין (כַּדּוּרִים) סָמוּךְ לַסְּפִינָה, עַל כֵּן אָמְרוּ: 'טוֹב יוֹתֵר לִשָּׁאֵר בָּאָרֶץ הַקְּדוֹשָׁה שֶׁיִּהְיוּ עַצְמוֹתֵיהֶם נִשְׁאָרִים שָׁם בָּאָרֶץ הַקְּדוֹשָׁה', כִּי רָאוּ שֶׁקָּשֶׁה לְהִמָּלֵט מִן הַהֲרֵגָה.


While they had slept, a battle had begun. They came out of their room and saw cannonballs flying toward the ship. They realized they were in mortal danger, and decided it would be better to return to the Holy Land to die than to be killed at sea.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:31

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:31](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:31)


וְאָמַר: שֶׁמֻּכְרָחִים לִתֵּן לוֹ אֵיזֶה סַךְ טָאלֶער בִּשְׁבִיל זֶה, וְאָמַר: שֶׁיֵּלֵךְ לְהַקַּפִּיטָן.


They begged the stranger to help them get off the ship and return to shore. He replied that he would need a considerable sum of money to try to bribe the captain.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:32

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:32](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:32)


וְהָלַךְ הָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל לִפְתֹּחַ הַתֵּבָה לִקַּח הַטָּאלֶער, וְנִתְמַהְמַהּ קְצָת, וְהָלַךְ לְהַקַּפִּיטָן, וּפָשַׁט יָדוֹ לִתֵּן הַמָּעוֹת לְהַקַּפִּיטָן, כִּי סָבַר שֶׁזֶּה שֶׁדִּבֵּר בִּלְשׁוֹן נָכְרִים אֲלֵיהֶם כְּבָר בִּקֵּשׁ עֲבוּרָם אֶת הַקַּפִּיטָן עֲבוּר זֶה שֶׁיַּנִּיחַ אוֹתָם. וְהַקַּפִּיטָן צָעַק עָלָיו צְעָקָה גְּדוֹלָה וְלֹא שָׁמַע אֵלָיו כְּלָל.


The Rebbe’s attendant went to their chest to get the money. He waited a while, thinking that the stranger would speak to the captain in the meantime. After a short time had passed, he went to the captain, holding out his hand with the money. The captain began to scream at him in Turkish, but the attendant could not understand a word.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:33

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:33](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:33)


וְאַחַר־כָּךְ הָיוּ מְחַפְּשִׂים אַחַר זֶה שֶׁדִּבֵּר אֲלֵיהֶם בִּלְשׁוֹן נָכְרִים וְחִפְּשׂוּ בְּחִפּוּשׂ אַחַר חִפּוּשׂ וְלֹא מָצְאוּ שָׁם שׁוּם בֶּן אָדָם שֶׁיּוּכַל לְדַבֵּר בִּלְשׁוֹן רוּסְיָא, רַק הַכֹּל יִשְׁמְעֵאלִים וְעַרְבִיִּים.


They had no other choice but to find the stranger who had spoken to them in Russian. They searched the ship over and over, but all they found were Turks and Arabs. There was not a single person who spoke Russian.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:34

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:34](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:34)


וְאָז נָפַל עֲלֵיהֶם פַּחַד גָּדוֹל וַאֲחָזָתַם רְעָדָה גְּדוֹלָה עַד שֶׁלֹּא הָיוּ יְכוֹלִים לָנוּחַ עַל הַמִּשְׁכָּב מֵחֲמַת רְעִידַת הַגּוּף מִגֹּדֶל הַפַּחַד, כִּי אָז הֵבִינוּ שֶׁהֵם בִּסְפִינָה שֶׁל מִלְחָמָה שֶׁקּוֹרִין 'קְרִיג שִׁיף' וְהוּא סַכָּנָה גְּדוֹלָה בְּלִי עֵרֶךְ.


Having lost their only means of communication, their apprehension grew by the minute. They literally began to tremble with fear. They tried to get some rest during the night, but could not even lie on their cots, so great was their trembling.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 20:35

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:35](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/20:35)


וַעֲדַיִן אֵין יוֹדְעִים מִי הָיָה זֶה שֶׁדִּבֵּר לָהֶם בִּלְשׁוֹן רוּסְיָא וְהוֹדִיעַ לָהֶם שֶׁהִיא סְפִינָה שֶׁל מִלְחָמָה.


More and more, they began to understand the grave danger they were in. Their only chance lay with the stranger who had spoken to them during the night, but they had no idea who he was. The only thing they knew about him was that he spoke Russian.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:1)


וּבַבֹּקֶר בָּאוּ אַל הַסְּפִינָה אַנְשֵׁי חַיִל שֶׁקּוֹרִין 'בְּרַנְדּ וַאךְ' (כְּמוֹ מִשְׁמַר הַגְּבוּל). וְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל עִם הָאִישׁ שֶׁלּוֹ הַנַּ"ל רָצוּ עוֹד לָבוֹא לְהִתְנַפֵּל לִפְנֵי רַגְלֵי הַקַּפִּיטָן וּלְהִתְחַנֵּן לוֹ שֶׁיַּנִּיחַ אוֹתָם לָשׁוּב אֶל הַסְּפָר [החוף]. אַךְ בְּתוֹךְ כָּךְ עָקְרוּ הָאַנְקֶרְס (עוֹגְנֵי הַסְּפִינָה) וּבָרְחוּ לְצַד שֶׁהָרוּח נְשָׂאָם, מֵחֲמַת פַּחַד, כִּי הַמִּלְחָמָה נִתְעוֹרְרָה מִיָּד.


The next morning, the harbor patrol⁴⁹, from the German for "fire watch" (*Yemey Moharnat* II, #80). It appears that this was a form of harbor patrol. boarded the ship. The Rebbe and his attendant searched for the captain, ready to fall before his feet if necessary and beg for permission to return with the patrol. But before they could find him, the ship lifted anchor and the wind carried them out of the harbor. The sounds of battle were coming closer and the sailors were very frightened, wanting to leave as quickly as possible.

footnotes:
⁴⁹ In Hebrew, brand wach


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:2)


וְנִשְׁמַע קוֹלָם מְאֹד, וְשָׁמְעוּ שָׁם עַל הַסְּפִינָה קוֹל גָּדוֹל מְאֹד שֶׁל הַהוֹרְמַאטֶעס וְהַבָּאמְבֶּעס (פִּצּוּצִים) וּשְׁאָר קוֹלוֹת כָּאֵלּוּ מֵעִנְיְנֵי מִלְחָמָה, כִּי קוֹלָם נִשְׁמַע לְמֵרָחוֹק מְאֹד. וְגֹדֶל הַסַּכָּנָה וְהָאֵימָה וְהַפַּחַד שֶׁהָיָה לָהֶם אָז – אֵין לְשַׁעֵר.


From where the Rebbe and his attendant stood on the ship, they could hear the sound of the bombs and cannon, even though they were already very far away. The very thought of their great peril drained them of all strength and they both fell into their cots, thoroughly exhausted.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:3)


וְנָפְלוּ לְמִשְׁכָּב שְׁנֵיהֶם יַחַד. וְלֹא הָיָה לָהֶם אֲפִלּוּ מַיִם לִשְׁתּוֹת, כִּי לֹא הֵכִינוּ לָהֶם כְּלוּם כַּנַּ"ל. וַה' בְּרֹב רַחֲמָיו וַחֲסָדָיו נָתַן לָהֶם חֵן בְּעֵינֵי יִשְׁמָעֵאל אֶחָד, שֶׁהוּא הָיָה קֶעכֶער (טַבָּח) אֵצֶל הַקַּפִּיטָן, וְנָתַן לָהֶם בִּגְנֵבָה אֶחָד 'שָׁאל קַאוֶוע שְׁחוֹרָה' (סֵפֶל קָפֶה) לְכָל אֶחָד וְאֶחָד בַּבֹּקֶר וּבָעֶרֶב.


Even as they lay there, they became aware of a new crisis. They had not brought any supplies and now found themselves without even water to drink. But here again, God was with them. The ship’s cook learned of their plight and had pity on them. Every morning and evening, he would sneak a cup of black coffee to each of them. This sustained them, but their strength was rapidly draining away.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:4)


וּמֵחֲמַת הַחֻלְשָׁה שֶׁלָּהֶם, הָיוּ מֻטָּלִים עַל עֶרֶשׂ דְּוַי רַחֲמָנָא לִצְלָן, וְלֹא יָדְעוּ בְּעַצְמָם הֵיכָן הֵם, כִּי הָיוּ סְגוּרִים בַּחֶדֶר שֶׁלָּהֶם נוֹפְלִים לְמִשְׁכָּב. אַךְ זֹאת שָׁמְעוּ: שֶׁהָיָה רוּחַ סְעָרָה וְנָשָׂא אֶת הַסְּפִינָה וְלֹא יָדְעוּ לְאֵיזֶה מָקוֹם נְשָׂאָהּ.


They soon reached the point that they did not have enough energy to leave their cots. Closed up in their cabin, they had no idea at all where the ship was headed. For a while, they heard a storm raging outside and felt the ship being carried rapidly along. But all the while, they did not know where they were going.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:5)


כְּשֶׁשָּׁמְעוּ שֶׁהִגִּיעַ הַסְּפִינָה לְאֵיזֶה סְפָר הָלְכוּ מֵהַחֶדֶר לַחוּץ לִרְאוֹת הַמָּקוֹם שֶׁהִגִּיעוּ לְשָׁם, וְרָאוּ שֶׁאֵין זֶה סְטַנְבּוּל. וְשָׁאֲלוּ אֶת הַיִּשְׁמָעֵאל הַנַּ"ל וְאָמַר: שֶׁהַמְּדִינָה הַזֹּאת נִקְרֵאת אַדַּל.


Eventually they heard the sounds of their ship being tied up at a port. They crawled out of their cabin to see where they had landed. Realizing immediately that it was not Istanbul, they asked the Turkish cook where they were. He replied that this was the land of Adal.⁵⁰

footnotes:
⁵⁰ Most probably Antalya, Turkey, formerly known as Adalia.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:6)


וְהֵם שָׁמְעוּ כְּבָר הַנְהָגָתָם: כְּשֶׁבָּא יְהוּדִי לְיָדָם – מִצְוָה רִאשׁוֹנָה אֶצְלָם לְהַקְרִיבוֹ לְקָרְבָּן, וְכַאֲשֶׁר שָׁמְעוּ שֶׁזֶּה אַדַּל הָלְכוּ מִיָּד לַחֶדֶר שֶׁלָּהֶם, וְסָגְרוּ הַדֶּלֶת בַּעֲדָם, וְהֶחְבִּיאוּ עַצְמָם שָׁם. וְהָיָה עֲלֵיהֶם פַּחַד גָּדוֹל. וְעָמְדָה הַסְּפִינָה שָׁם שְׁלֹשָׁה יָמִים, וְאֶפְשָׁר הָיָה בְּדַעַת הַקַּפִּיטָן לַעֲמֹד שָׁם עוֹד.


They had already heard of Adal. The people there had a rule that any Jew who fell into their hands should be killed as a human sacrifice. When the Rebbe and his attendant understood that this was where they were, they fled to their cabin and locked themselves inside. For three days they hid there, utterly terrified. They knew they could not remain concealed much longer, and had no idea how long the captain would stay there.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:7)


אַךְ שֶׁפִּתְאֹם עָמַד רוּחַ סְעָרָה וְנִתְּקוּ הַחֲבָלִים שֶׁל הָאַנְקֶר וְנִשְׁאַר הָאַנְקֶר בַּיָּם וְהָלְכָה סְפִינָה מֵעַצְמָהּ מִשָּׁם. וְהָיָה שָׁם צְעָקָה גְּדוֹלָה בֵּין הַיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים "הַמַן הַמַן".


Suddenly a storm arose, snapping the anchor ropes. The sailors had no chance to raise sail, and without an anchor, the ship was totally out of control. There was a great cry on board, for even the Turks were terrified. From where they were huddled, the Rebbe and his attendant could hear their cries: “*Homan*! *Homan*! Woe! Woe!”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:8)


וְהָיְתָה הַסְּפִינָה מְטֹרֶפֶת בַּיָּם כָּל הַלַּיְלָה עַד אוֹר הַבֹּקֶר, אָז נִשְׁקַט הָרוּחַ סְעָרָה וַיִּשְׂמְחוּ כִּי יִשְׁתֹּקוּ. אַךְ לֹא הִנְחָם לִמְחוֹז חֶפְצָם, כִּי עַל יְדֵי הָרוּחַ סְעָרָה חָזְרוּ וּבָאוּ סָמוּךְ לְעַכּוֹ בְּעֵרֶךְ מַהֲלַךְ יוֹם.


The ship tossed at sea all that night. In the morning, the wind subsided and they were glad because the seas were quiet (cf. Psalms 107:30). But they had not been led to their desired haven (ibid.), for the storm had carried them back to within a day’s journey of Acre.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:9)


וְאַחַר־כָּךְ, אַחַר חֲצִי הַיּוֹם, נִתְעוֹרֵר עוֹד הַפַּעַם רוּחַ סְעָרָה גְּדוֹלָה, וְנָשָׂא אֶת הַסְּפִינָה. וְהָיְתָה הַסְּפִינָה מְטֹרֶפֶת וּמְבֻלְבֶּלֶת כַּמָּה יָמִים וְלֵילוֹת רְצוּפִים, יַעֲלוּ שָׁמַיִם יֵרְדוּ תְּהוֹמוֹת, וְלֹא הָיָה לְבַעֲלֵי הַסְּפִינָה שׁוּם עֵצָה אֵיךְ לְהִמָּלֵט. וַיָּחֹגּוּ וַיָּנוּעוּ כַּשִּׁכּוֹר כָּל חָכְמָתָם תִּתְבַּלָּע.


Another storm broke out that afternoon, sweeping the ship along. For several days and nights, the ship was tossed about at sea. They mounted to the heavens and plunged to the depths (ibid., 107:26). The captain and his mates had no idea how to control the vessel. They reeled and staggered like drunkards, and all their wisdom was in vain (ibid., 107:27).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:10)


וּבְיוֹם חֲמִישִׁי, שָׁעָה שְׁתֵּים־עֶשְׂרֵה, הִתְחִילוּ לְהוֹצִיא הַמַּיִם מֵהַסְּפִינָה מֵהַמָּדוֹר הַשְּׁלִישִׁי עַל־יְדֵי הַפְּלוֹמְפִּין (הַמַּשְׁאֵבוֹת) כְּדֶרֶךְ בַּעֲלֵי הַסְּפִינָה. וְדַרְכָּם הָיָה בְּכָל מֵעֵת־לְעֵת, שֶׁנִּשְׁתַּהוּ בְּהוֹצָאַת הַמַּיִם רְבִיעִית שָׁעָה. וְעַכְשָׁו כְּשֶׁהִתְחִילוּ לִשְׁאֹב, לֹא הִסְפִּיקוּ לִשְׁאֹב עַד אֲשֶׁר הִגִּיעַ עֵת הַלַּיְלָה.


That Thursday at noon, they began pumping the third compartment as usual. It usually took about fifteen minutes to pump out all the water that accumulated in the bilge each day. This time, they pumped all day and were still drawing water by nightfall.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:11)


וְנִתְרַבּוּ הַמַּיִם הַרְבֵּה עַל הַסְּפִינָה מֵחֲמַת הַשְּׁאִיבָה, וְהָלְכוּ הַמַּיִם גַּם לַחֶדֶר שֶׁלָּהֶם, שֶׁל רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, וְלֹא יָכְלוּ לִשְׁכַּב עַל הַמִּשְׁכָּב שָׁם, כִּי גָּאוּ הַמַּיִם שָׁם, וְהֻכְרַח רַבֵּנוּ לַעֲלוֹת עַל אֵיזֶה מָקוֹם גָּבוֹהַּ לִשְׁכַּב, וְהָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ עָלָה לְמָקוֹם אַחֵר, כִּי לֹא רָצוּ לָצֵאת לְהִתְרָאוֹת בִּפְנֵי הַיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים.


Soon the entire upper deck was awash with water pumped up from the bilges. The water seeped into the Rebbe’s compartment, making it impossible for him and his attendant to sleep on their cots. The Rebbe climbed to a higher place in the compartment and lay down there; his attendant found a similar place. They remained inside, not wanting to be seen by the Turks.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:12)


וְהַמַּיִם שֶׁבַּסְּפִינָה הָלְכוּ וְנִתְרַבּוּ בְּכָל פַּעַם יוֹתֵר וְיוֹתֵר, עַד שֶׁהֻכְרְחוּ הַיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים לַעֲקֹר הַפְּלוֹמְפִּין, וְעָשׂוּ גַּלְגַּלִּים, וְשָׁאֲבוּ עַל־יָדָם מַיִם עִם כֵּלִים גְּדוֹלִים. וְהָרוּחַ סְעָרָה הוֹלֵךְ וְחָזֵק מְאֹד. וּמִגֹּדֶל הִתְגַּבְּרוּת הָרוּחַ סְעָרָה עָלוּ הַגַּלִּים כִּמְעַט עַד לֵב הַשָּׁמַיִם וְהָיוּ כְּמוֹ הָרִים גְּבוֹהִים וּבְקָעוֹת גְּדוֹלוֹת. וְהָיָה כָּךְ כָּל הַלַּיְלָה גַּם־כֵּן.


The water continued to rise until the pumps could no longer function. The sailors had to tear out the pumps and install pulleys in an attempt to remove the water with large barrels. Meanwhile, the storm continued to rage, whipping up mountains of water. The waves seemed to rise to the very height of the heavens. This continued all through the night.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:13)


וּכְשֶׁהֵאִיר הַיּוֹם, בָּא הָאִישׁ שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ ז"ל, וְאָמַר לוֹ רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: שֶׁיֵּשׁ לוֹ חֲלִישׁוּת הַלֵּב מְאֹד מִגֹּדֶל הַפַּחַד, "כִּי אֲנִי רוֹאֶה בְּלִבִּי שֶׁאֲנִי עַכְשָׁו בְּסַכָּנָה גְּדוֹלָה כִּי אֵין זֶה דָּבָר פָּשׁוּט, אַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁאֵין אֲנִי רוֹאֶה מַה הֵם עוֹשִׂים אַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן הַלֵּב רוֹאֶה".


When his attendant approached him the next morning, the Rebbe told him, “My heart is faint with terror. Deep down, I know that we are in great danger, and this is by no means a simple matter. I do not know what they are doing, but the heart still sees.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:14)


וְהָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל כִּחֵשׁ לוֹ וְאָמַר לוֹ: "אַל תְּפַחֲדוּ כִּי בָּרוּךְ הַשֵּׁם הַכֹּל עַל נָכוֹן".


His attendant comforted him, “Don’t be afraid. Thank God, everything is fine.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:15

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:15](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:15)


וְהָלַךְ הָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל לְהִסְתַּכֵּל בַּהַמְבֶּר בַּמָּקוֹם שֶׁהֵם שׁוֹאֲבִין הַמַּיִם, וְרָאָה שֶׁכְּבָר יֵשׁ שָׁם כְּמוֹ גַּלִּים קְטַנִּים כְּמוֹ עַל הַנְּהָרוֹת שֶׁלָּנוּ, כִּי הַסְּפִינָה נַעֲשָׂה טְעוּנָה מִמַּיִם לְבַד. כִּי דֶּרֶךְ הַסְּפִינָה לִהְיוֹת טְעוּנָה עִם מַשּׂוֹי שְׁנֵי חֲלָקִים, וּכְבָר הִשְׁלִיכוּ הַיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים כָּל הַמַּשָּׂאוֹת וְנִשְׁאֲרָה הַסְּפִינָה טְעוּנָה עִם מַיִם לְבַד. וּמַה שֶּׁהָיָה שָׁם עוֹד יוֹתֵר מַיִם מִמִּשְׁקַל הַמַּשּׂוֹי הָרָאוּי לִהְיוֹת בַּסְּפִינָה, אֵלּוּ הַמַּיִם שָׁאֲבוּ לַחוּץ, וְגַם עַל זֶה לֹא הָיוּ מַסְפִּיקִים לִשְׁאֹב.


The attendant then went to look into the hold where they were drawing out water. He saw nothing but water rippling, just like in a river. Cargo normally took up all but a third of the ship’s capacity, but it had been thrown overboard. Only water was left in the hold. Still, the vessel was riding very low and it was obvious that the water’s weight far exceeded its capacity. The sailors were trying feverishly to pump out the water, but still it continued to rise.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:16

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:16](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:16)


וְאָז רָאָה הָאִישׁ שֶׁאִי־אֶפְשָׁר עוֹד עַל־פִּי דֶּרֶךְ הַטֶּבַע לְהִנָּצֵל. וּבָא אֶל הַחֶדֶר שֶׁלָּהֶם, וּמֵחֲמַת גֹּדֶל הַצַּעַר וְהַפַּחַד לֹא הָיָה יָכוֹל לְדַבֵּר כִּמְעַט.


Realizing that nothing less than a miracle could save them, the Rebbe’s attendant returned to their compartment. He was trembling with anguish and terror, and could not get a word out of his mouth.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:17

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:17](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:17)


וְאָמַר לוֹ רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: "מַה זֹּאת וְאַיֵּה פִּיךָ אֲשֶׁר אָמַרְתָּ שֶׁאֵין זֶה כְּלוּם וְעַכְשָׁו אַתָּה בְּעַצְמְךָ מִתְפַּחֵד כָּל־כָּךְ?!"


The Rebbe saw him and said, “Where is your tongue? You yourself said that nothing was wrong. Why are you so afraid?”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:18

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:18](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:18)


וְהֵשִׁיב לוֹ: שֶׁשּׁוּב אֵין סְבָרָא עַל־פִּי דֶּרֶךְ הַטֶּבַע לְהִנָּצֵל, כִּי הַמַּיִם מִתְרַבִּים וְהוֹלְכִים בַּסְּפִינָה, וְאֵין בִּיכָלְתָּם לִשְׁאֹב הַכֹּל, כִּי כָּשַׁל כֹּחַ הַסַּבָּל. כִּי זֶה קָרוֹב לְמֵעֵת־לְעֵת שֶׁלֹּא אָכְלוּ וְלֹא שָׁתוּ וְלֹא רָאוּ שֵׁנָה בְּעֵינֵיהֶם.


The attendant replied that there was no logical way they could be saved without a miracle. The water was now rising faster than the men could remove it, and the laborers’ strength was failing (cf. Nehemiah 4:4). The sailors had gone without food, water and sleep for almost twenty-four hours.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:19

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:19](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:19)


וְאָמַר לוֹ הָאִישׁ: שֶׁעֲדַיִן לֹא הִתְפַּלֵּל. הֵשִׁיב לוֹ רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: אֵין אַתָּה צָרִיךְ לְהִתְפַּלֵּל עַכְשָׁו, רַק תְּקַבֵּל עֹל מַלְכוּת שָׁמַיִם בְּפָסוּק רִאשׁוֹן, וְתֹאמַר שָׁלֹשׁ רִאשׁוֹנוֹת וְשָׁלֹשׁ אַחֲרוֹנוֹת וְכוּ'.


The attendant then told the Rebbe that he had not yet said his morning prayers. The Rebbe told him, “You need not recite the entire service now. Accept the yoke of the Kingdom of Heaven in the first verse of the *Shma*,⁵¹," the declaration of faith in God's oneness, whose recital is termed "accepting the yoke of the Kingdom of Heaven" in *Berakhot* 13a. For the ruling mentioned here about not reciting the entire service, see ibid., 16a. and say the first and last three blessings of the *Amidah* (cf. *Orach Chaim* 110:1). That is enough at a time like this” (see *Berakhot* 4:5).

footnotes:
⁵¹ "Shma Yisrael


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:20

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:20](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:20)


וְאָמַר לוֹ: קַח כָּל הַמָּעוֹת עַד פְּרוּטָה אַחֲרוֹנָה וּתְחַלֵּק לִשְׁנַיִם. מֶחֱצָה תִּקְשֹׁר אֶצְלְךָ עַל גּוּפְךָ, וּמֶחֱצָה אֶקְשֹׁר עַל גּוּפִי.


Then the Rebbe told him, “Take every penny that we have and divide it in half. Bind your half next to your body, and I will do the same with mine.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:21

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:21](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:21)


וּשְׁאָלוֹ הָאִישׁ: "לָמָּה זֹאת?, הֲלֹא הַדָּג שֶׁבַּיָּם יוּכַל לִבְלֹעַ אוֹתָנוּ בְּלֹא הַמָּעוֹת?"


His attendant was mystified. “Why bother?" he asked. "Can’t the fish swallow us without money?”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:22

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:22](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:22)


הֵשִׁיב לוֹ: "עֲשֵׂה כָּךְ כְּמוֹ שֶׁאֲנִי אוֹמֵר לְךָ. יִשְׂרָאֵל הָיוּ עַל הַיָּם וְלֹא טָבְעוּ, וַאֲנַחְנוּ עֲדַיִן בַּסְּפִינָה" וְכוּ'. וְעָשָׂה מַה שֶּׁעָשָׂה.


The Rebbe replied, “Do as I tell you. The Jews were inside the Red Sea and did not drown. At least we are still in a ship … .”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:23

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:23](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:23)


וְאָמַר לְהָאִישׁ: שֶׁיַּלְבִּישׁ אֶת עַצְמוֹ וְיַחְגֹּר בַּחֲגוֹרָה עַל הַטִּילוֹפּ (מְעִיל פַּרְוָה) וְגַם הוּא עָשָׂה כָּךְ, כְּמוֹ אֶחָד שֶׁמּוּכָן לֵילֵךְ בַּדֶּרֶךְ.


Having taken care of this, the Rebbe told his attendant to dress himself in his fur coat and bind his belt tightly around it. The Rebbe did the same, acting as if he were preparing to embark on a journey.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:24

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:24](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:24)


וְשָׁאַל אוֹתוֹ הָאִישׁ וְאָמַר לוֹ: "אֲנִי אֵינִי יוֹדֵעַ לְהִתְפַּלֵּל אֲפִלּוּ תְּפִלָּה הַסְּדוּרָה מֵאַנְשֵׁי כְּנֶסֶת־הַגְּדוֹלָה מִכָּל־שֶׁכֵּן עַכְשָׁו בְּעֵת צָרָה הַזֹּאת. אֲבָל כְּבוֹד־תּוֹרָתוֹ יוֹדֵעַ לְהִתְפַּלֵּל בְּעַד כְּלָלִיּוּת וּפְרָטִיּוּת, מִפְּנֵי מָה אֵין אַתֶּם מִתְפַּלְּלִין עַכְשָׁו?"


The Rebbe’s attendant asked him, “Why don’t you pray for us? I cannot even recite the prayers that I say every day, and certainly cannot open my mouth to God at a time like this. But you, Rebbe, know how to pray at all times, for the general and the specific.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:25

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:25](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:25)


וְהֵשִׁיב: "שֶׁעַכְשָׁו מֵחֲמַת הַמֹּחִין דְּקַטְנוּת אֲנִי מְרֻחָק מֵה' יִתְבָּרַךְ. אֲבָל עֲצָתִי אֱמוּנָה, מֵחֲמַת אֵין בְּרֵרָה מֵחֲמַת גֹּדֶל הַסַּכָּנָה שֶׁאֲנַחְנוּ עַכְשָׁו בְּסַכָּנָה נוֹרָאָה וַעֲצוּמָה כָּזוֹ, ה' יִתְבָּרַךְ יוֹדֵעַ שֶׁכָּל יְמֵי חַיַּי עֲדַיִן לֹא הָיִיתִי מִשְׁתַּמֵּשׁ מֵעוֹלָם בִּזְכוּת אָבוֹת, רַק מֵחֲמַת הַהֶכְרֵחַ מֻכְרָח אֲנִי לְבַקֵּשׁ מֵה' יִתְבָּרַךְ שֶׁיַּעֲשֶׂה זֹאת לְמַעַן זְכוּת זְקֵנִי רַבִּי יִשְׂרָאֵל בַּעַל־שֵׁם־טוֹב ז"ל, וּזְכוּת זְקֶנְתִּי אָדְל, וּזְכוּת זְקֵנִי רַבִּי נַחְמָן מֵהָרִידֶנְקָא". וְיֶתֶר מִזֶּה לֹא הִזְכִּיר עוֹד.


The Rebbe replied, “I am now in a state of constricted consciousness.⁵², a Kabbalistic term referring to the immature state of the transcendental intellects. Cf. *Etz Chaim* 22. I am far from God. Still, my counsel is faithful. We are now in fierce danger and I have no other choice. God knows that I have never made use of the merit of my fathers, but now I am compelled. I pray that God help us for the sake of my grandfather, Rabbi Israel Baal Shem Tov, of blessed memory; for the sake of my grandmother, Adil; and for the sake of my grandfather, Rabbi Nachman Horodenker.”⁵³ #14. See *Tovot Zikhronot* 7, where it states that Rebbe Nachman used his ancestral merits threee times: Once, on his return from the Holy Land; the second time, to bring Rabbi Aharon to be the Rav in Breslov; the third time is unknown. The Rebbe said no more.

footnotes:
⁵² Mochin deKatnut
⁵³ Cf. Tzaddik


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:26

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:26](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:26)


וְאַחַר זֶה, רָאוּ מֵרָחוֹק כְּמוֹ עָנָן גָּדוֹל אָפֵל מְאֹד, וְנָפַל עֲלֵיהֶם עוֹד פַּחַד גָּדוֹל, מֵחֲמַת שֶׁלִּפְעָמִים כְּשֶׁהֶעָנָן מִתְאַסֵּף וּמִתְקַשֵּׁר לִשְׁאֹב הַמַּיִם מֵהַיָּם, נַעֲשֶׂה שָׁם כְּמוֹ בִּקְעָה, וּכְשֶׁהַסְּפִינָה יוֹרֶדֶת לְשָׁם אֲזַי נִטְבָּעִין שָׁם. וְהַתַּקָּנָה לָזֶה: לִירוֹת בִּקְנֵי־שְׂרֵפָה וְהוֹרְמַאטֶעס כְּדֶרֶךְ הַמִּלְחָמָה, כְּדֵי לְשַׁבֵּר הָעֲנָנִים. וְלָהֶם לֹא הָיָה פְּנַאי לָזֶה מֵחֲמַת שֶׁהָיוּ צְרִיכִין לִשְׁאֹב הַמַּיִם מֵהַסְּפִינָה כַּנַּ"ל, וְהָיוּ הַצָּרוֹת צְרוֹרוֹת זוֹ לָזוֹ כְּמוֹ זִבּוֹרָא וְעַקְרַבָּא.


They looked up and suddenly saw a thick, dark cloud in the distance. Their hearts filled with dread, since this was the type of cloud that usually creates a waterspout. If the ship were to be drawn into the vortex, it could easily be sunk. The only solution would be to fire at the cloud with cannon, but the sailors were too busy bailing out the ship and could not stop for an instant. It seemed as if one trouble was coming on top of another, like a wasp and a scorpion biting at the same time.⁵⁴ 5a.
