###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:27

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:27](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:27)


אַךְ בְּחֶמְלַת ה' מֵחֲמַת תֹּקֶף הָרוּחַ סְעָרָה הָיָה הֲלִיכַת הַסְּפִינָה בִּמְהִירוּת גָּדוֹל וּפָרְחָה הַסְּפִינָה כְּמוֹ חֵץ מִקֶּשֶׁת מַמָּשׁ, וּבָאָה הַסְּפִינָה לַמָּקוֹם הַנַּ"ל שֶׁהֶעָנָן שׁוֹתֶה מִשָּׁם. וְהָיָה שָׁם כְּמוֹ שַׁעַר וְהָרִים סָבִיב לָהּ, וְעָבְרָה הַסְּפִינָה מִשָּׁם בְּשָׁלוֹם.


Again God came to their rescue and a very strong wind began to blow. The ship began to skim across the waves like an arrow shot from a bow. When it approached the waterspout, the cloud opened up like a mountain pass and the ship crossed through safely.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:28

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:28](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:28)


וּבְתוֹךְ כָּךְ הֵאִיר ה' עֵינֵיהֶם, וּמָצְאוּ הַחוֹר שֶׁדֶּרֶךְ שָׁם נִכְנָסִין הַמַּיִם בַּסְּפִינָה, וְשָׁחֲטוּ תַּיִשׁ, וְלָקְחוּ הָעוֹר שֶׁלּוֹ וְתִקְּנוּ הַסְּפִינָה וְנִצְּלוּ בְּשָׁלוֹם מִסַּכָּנוֹת גְּדוֹלוֹת וְנוֹרָאוֹת הַלָּלוּ.


God also enlightened the sailors’ eyes and they discovered the place where the ship’s hull was leaking. The Turks slaughtered a ram and used its skin to repair the leak.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:29

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:29](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:29)


וְהָיָה זֹאת בְּעֶרֶב־שַׁבַּת־קֹדֶשׁ. וְהָיָה לָהֶם שִׂמְחָה גְדוֹלָה. וְאָמַר רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל אָז "הוֹדוּ לַהשם" בְּשִׂמְחָה גְּדוֹלָה.


They had been in fearsome peril, but had managed to come through safely. It was a Friday, and everybody on the ship rejoiced greatly. In the afternoon prayers before Shabbat, the Rebbe chanted Psalm 107 (“Give thanks to God”) with great joy.⁵⁵" #270.

footnotes:
⁵⁵ This is a regular part of the Chassidic rite. See "His Wisdom


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:30

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:30](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:30)


מִשָּׁם הָלְכוּ דֶּרֶךְ מְקוֹמוֹת הַרְבֵּה מְאֹד. וַאֲכִילָה שֶׁהָיָה לָהֶם עַל הַסְּפִינָה הָיָה רַק מַה שֶּׁנָּתַן לָהֶם הַקֶּכֶער [טבח] הַנַּ"ל, מִצַּד רַחֲמָנוּת מֵהַמַּגִּיזַאן (מַחְסָן) שֶׁלָּהֶם, סוּחֲרֶעס (צְנִימִים) וְתוֹלָעִים הָיוּ מְרַחֲשִׁים עֲלֵיהֶם. וְהָיוּ הוֹלְכִים וּמְבַקְּשִׁים וּפוֹרְשִׂים יְדֵיהֶם בְּהַכְנָעָה בְּכָל יוֹם כְּעָנִי בַּפֶּתַח, וְהָיָה נוֹתֵן לָהֶם מֵאֵלּוּ הַסּוּחֲרֶעס וְאָכְלוּ זֹאת לְבַד.


They continued on their voyage, passing many places. During the entire voyage, the only thing the Rebbe and his attendant had to eat was the dried bread that the cook gave them from the ship’s stores. Their meager rations were often putrid and crawling with maggots. Even for these morsels, they had to plead with the cook like beggars until he had pity on them.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:31

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:31](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:31)


וַיְהִי בַּעֲלוֹת עַל לִבָּם שֶׁהוּא סָמוּךְ לַפֶּסַח, הָיָה לָהֶם צַעַר גָּדוֹל, כִּי הָיוּ דּוֹאֲגִים: מַה יַּעֲשׂוּ בַּפֶּסַח? כִּי אִי־אֶפְשָׁר לְהִתְעַנּוֹת כָּל יְמוֹת הַפֶּסַח.


As the days passed, they realized that Pesach was rapidly approaching. One day, they discussed this and were very concerned. The food from the ship’s stores could not be eaten during the festival, and it would be impossible to fast for the entire week.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:32

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:32](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:32)


וְאַחַר שָׁעָה אוֹ שְׁתַּיִם בָּאוּ אֶל כְּרָךְ גָּדוֹל (אחד מאיי הַדּוֹרְקֶנִיז) הָעוֹמֶדֶת עַל הָהָר שֶׁל אֲבָנִים בְּאֶמְצַע הַיָּם. וְשָׁם קָנוּ הַיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים פֵּרוֹת הַרְבֵּה, וְלָהֶם נָתְנוּ בְּעַד טָאלֶער אֶחָד חָרוּבִים (שֶׁקּוֹרִין בָּאקְסֶערְן) הַרְבֵּה, בְּעֵרֶךְ טְשֶׁעטְוִוירְט.


After an hour or two, they came to a large city perched on a rocky mountain in the middle of the sea.⁵⁶ The Turks bought a considerable amount of fruit there. For a thaler, the Rebbe and his attendant were able to buy a bushel of carobs – what we call *buxer*.

footnotes:
⁵⁶ One of the Dodecanese islands in the Aegean Sea, off the southwest coast of Turkey. The description most closely fits Lindos on the island of Rhodes or Voladha on the island of Karpathos.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 21:33

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:33](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/21:33)


וָאָמַר רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: "לְעֵת עַתָּה גַּם זֶה טוֹב מְאֹד, כִּי יְכוֹלִין לִחְיוֹת שְׁמוֹנַת יְמֵי הַפֶּסַח עִם חָרוּבִין לְבַד, אַךְ מִי יִתֵּן שֶׁיְּזַכֶּה אוֹתָנוּ ה' יִתְבָּרַךְ לְקַיֵּם מִצְוַת אֲכִילַת מַצָּה וְאַרְבַּע כּוֹסוֹת".


The Rebbe said, “This is also very good right now. If necessary, we can subsist on these carobs alone during the eight days of Pesach. Now, if God helps us, we will also have *matzah* and the Four Cups of wine for the Seder.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/22:1)


וְגֹדֶל עֹצֶם הַסַּכָּנוֹת שֶׁהָיָה לָהֶם בַּחֲזִירָתָם, אִי־אֶפְשָׁר לְסַפֵּר. כִּי הָיוּ עַל הַסְּפִינָה הַזּוֹ שֶׁל מִלְחָמָה שֶׁהָיְתָה מְלֵאָה יִשְׁמְעֵאלִים. וְהֵם הָיוּ רַק שְׁנֵי יְהוּדִים לְבַד. וְדֶרֶךְ הַיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים, בִּפְרָט אַנְשֵׁי מִלְחָמָה, לִתְפֹּס יְהוּדִים וּלְמָכְרָם בְּמֶרְחַקִּים לַעֲבָדִים. וְהָיָה לְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל פַּחַד גָּדוֹל מִזֶּה.


Even after overcoming these threats, the Rebbe and his attendant were still in indescribable danger. They were two Jews alone on a warship filled with Turkish troops. These Turks would think nothing of selling two Jews as slaves in some faraway place. The very thought filled the Rebbe’s heart with dread.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/22:2)


וְאָמַר: שֶׁהִתְחִיל לַחֲשֹׁב בְּעַצְמוֹ; מַה יַּעֲשֶׂה אִם יוֹלִיכוּ אוֹתוֹ לְאֵיזֶה מָקוֹם עַל הַיָּם שֶׁאֵין שָׁם יְהוּדִי וְיִמְכְּרוּ אוֹתוֹ שָׁם?, וּמִי יֵדַע מִזֶּה? וְהָיָה לוֹ צַעַר גָּדוֹל, אֵיךְ יוּכַל לְקַיֵּם שָׁם אֶת מִצְווֹת הַתּוֹרָה?


He began to think about it. What would he do if he were sold as a slave in a faraway place where there were no Jews? What if nobody knew about it? How would he keep the commandments of the Torah? This particular worry bothered him the most.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 22:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/22:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/22:3)


וְהִתְחִיל לַחֲשֹׁב בְּדַעְתּוֹ בְּעִנְיָן זֶה, עַד שֶׁזָּכָה שֶׁבָּא עַל הַשָּׂגָה: שֶׁיּוּכַל לַעֲבֹד אֶת ה' יִתְבָּרַךְ אֲפִלּוּ כְּשֶׁלֹּא יוּכַל ח"ו לְקַיֵּם הַמִּצְווֹת. כִּי הִשִּׂיג אֶת הָעֲבוֹדָה שֶׁל אֲבוֹת הָעוֹלָם שֶׁהָיָה לָהֶם קֹדֶם מַתַּן תּוֹרָה, שֶׁקִּיְּמוּ כָּל הַמִּצְווֹת אַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁלֹּא עָשׂוּ אֶת הַמִּצְווֹת כִּפְשׁוּטָן. כְּמוֹ יַעֲקֹב אָבִינוּ שֶׁקִּיֵּם מִצְוַת תְּפִלִּין עַל־יְדֵי הַמַּקְלוֹת אֲשֶׁר פִּצֵּל כַּיָּדוּעַ, וְכַיּוֹצֵא בָּזֶה, עַד שֶׁהִשִּׂיג אֵיךְ לְקַיֵּם אֶת כָּל הַמִּצְווֹת בְּדֶרֶךְ זֶה כְּשֶׁיִּהְיֶה אָנוּס שָׁם בְּמָקוֹם שֶׁיִּמְכְּרוּ אוֹתוֹ ח"ו.


The Rebbe pondered the possibilities again and again. Finally, he was worthy of realizing that he could serve God even if he were unable to actually keep the commandments. He comprehended the devotion of the Patriarchs who lived before God even gave the Torah. They also kept all the commandments, even though they often did so only in a symbolic manner. Thus Jacob fulfilled the commandment of *tefilin* through the sticks he peeled while watching Laban’s sheep.⁵⁷ (*Sisrei Torah*) I, 162b.

The Rebbe thought along these lines until he understood how he could keep all the commandments in this manner. He could continue to serve God even if he were sold as a slave in the most distant land.⁵⁸ #39; *Parparaot LeChokhmah* II, 78; *Be’ibey HaNachal* II*,* 78. Also see *Likutey Moharan* I, 54.

footnotes:
⁵⁷ Genesis 30:37. See Zohar
⁵⁸ Cf. Tzaddik


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 22:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/22:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/22:4)


וְתֵכֶף כְּשֶׁזָּכָה לְהַשָּׂגָה זוֹ, עֲזָרוֹ ה' יִתְבָּרַךְ וּבָאָה הַסְּפִינָה וְהִגִּיעָה אוֹר לְאַרְבָּעָה עָשָׂר בְּנִיסָן לִכְרָךְ גָּדוֹל שֶׁעוֹמֵד עַל אִי הַיָּם וְשָׁם עִיר גְּדוֹלָה לֵאלקִים 'וּשְָׂהּ רַאדִישׁ' (רוּדוּס), וְהִכִּירוּ שֶׁזֶּה עִיר שֶׁל יִשְׂרָאֵל. וְהָיָה לָהֶם שִׂמְחָה גְּדוֹלָה, כִּי יוּכְלוּ לִקְנוֹת כָּאן מַצּוֹת לְפֶסַח וְאַרְבַּע כּוֹסוֹת.


No sooner had the Rebbe arrived at this conclusion than they sighted land. It was the night before Pesach when they arrived at the island city of Rhodes, a great city unto God. They knew that this city had a thriving Jewish community. Here they would be able to buy *matzot* for Pesach and wine for the Four Cups.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 22:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/22:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/22:5)


אַךְ זֶּה לֹא עָלָה עַל דַּעְתָּם שֶׁהַקַּפִּיטָן וְהַיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים יַנִּיחוּ אוֹתָם לִכָּנֵס לָעִיר הַזֹּאת, כִּי רָאוּ וְהֵבִינוּ שֶׁהַיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים רוֹצִים לְתָפְסָם וּלְגָזְלָם וְאֶפְשָׁר יִמְכְּרוּ אוֹתָם בְּעַצְמָם וְכַּנַּ"ל.


The Rebbe and his attendant were overjoyed by this prospect alone. They never imagined that the captain or the sailors would actually let them go into the city. Everything seemed to indicate that the Turks were going to imprison them, rob them of all their possessions, and perhaps even sell them as slaves.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:1)


וּבַבֹּקֶר, שֶׁהוּא עֶרֶב פֶּסַח, נָסַע הַקַּפִּיטָן עִם כַּמָּה יִשְׁמְעֵאלִים עַל הַסְּפִינָה קְטַנָּה לְתוֹךְ הָעִיר. וּבָאוּ לְפָנָיו וּבִקְּשׁוּ מִמֶּנּוּ לֶחֶם לֶאֱכֹל, וְדִבְּרוּ עִמּוֹ בִּרְמִיזָה. וְהֵשִׁיב לָהֶם בִּרְמִיזָה וְהוֹדִיעָם: 'שֶׁיֵּשׁ כָּאן יְהוּדִים' 'וִיכוֹלִים אַתֶּם לִקְנוֹת לָכֶם לֶחֶם'.


It was the morning before Pesach. The captain and a number of sailors were preparing to take their small boat and go to the city. The Rebbe and his attendant came to the captain and, using gestures, communicated that they needed some kind of bread. The captain told them in sign language, “There are Jews in the city. You can have bread brought for you.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:2)


וה' יִתְבָּרַךְ בְּרַחֲמָיו אֲשֶׁר בְּיָדוֹ לֵב מְלָכִים וְשָׂרִים, הֵסִיר לִבּוֹ וּבִלְבֵּל דַּעְתּוֹ עַד שֶׁיָּצָא מִפִּיו בְּלִי יִשּׁוּב־הַדַּעַת, וְאָמַר שֶׁיִּקַּח אֶחָד מֵהֶם כְּדֵי לִקְנוֹת לֶחֶם עֲבוּרָם.


The hearts of kings and princes are in the hand of God. In His mercy, He turned the captain’s heart and confounded his thoughts. Without thinking, the captain blurted out that he would take one of them along to buy their bread.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:3)


וְנָסַע מִיָּד עִמּוֹ הָאִישׁ שֶׁל רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל. וּבָא אֶל הָעִיר, וְהָלַךְ אַחֲרָיו אֶחָד מֵהַיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים שֶׁלּוֹ לְשָׁמְרוֹ שֶׁלֹּא יִבְרַח. וְדֶרֶךְ הֲלִיכָתוֹ לָעִיר הָיָה גַּם־כֵּן סַכָּנָה גְּדוֹלָה, כִּי אָז הָיָה שְׁעַת מִלְחָמָה גְּדוֹלָה, וּבְקַל הָיוּ יְכוֹלִים לְתָפְסוֹ שֶׁהוּא אֶחָד מֵהַמְרַגְּלִים, כִּי נִכָּר בּוֹ שֶׁאֵינוֹ מִמְּדִינָתָם כִּי־אִם מִמְּדִינָה רְחוֹקָה מְאֹד. אַךְ בְּחַסְדֵּי ה' עָבַר עַל כָּל שׁוֹמְרֵי הַשְּׁעָרִים, וְלֹא עָשׂוּ לוֹ מְאוּמָה.


The Rebbe’s attendant went with the crew. He was allowed to go to the city alone, with just one sailor following him to make sure he didn't escape. But even the way to the city involved great peril. It was a time of war and it was obvious that he was from a distant land. He could easily have been imprisoned as a spy with nobody to defend him. But again God protected him, and he passed through all the gates without being stopped.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:4)


עַד שֶׁבָּא אֶל הֶחָכָם הַכּוֹלֵל וְסִפֵּר לוֹ מַה שֶּׁאֵרַע לָהֶם, וְשֶׁהֵם נִלְכְּדוּ בִּסְפִינָה שֶׁל מִלְחָמָה. אַךְ הוּא בְּעַצְמוֹ לֹא יָדַע מַה לְּסַפֵּר, כִּי הֵם בְּעַצְמָן לֹא יָדְעוּ כַּוָּנַת הַקַּפִּיטָן מַה הוּא רוֹצֶה לַעֲשׂוֹת עִמָּהֶם. אַךְ בְּדֶרֶךְ כְּלָל סִפֵּר לוֹ שֶׁהֵם כִּמְעַט בְּשִׁבְיָה ח"ו וְהֵם בְּסַכָּנָה.


He finally came to the chief rabbi and told him the whole story. They were captives aboard a warship, and they themselves did not know what the captain intended to do with them. He could not supply the details, but this he knew: They were prisoners and in great danger.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:5)


וְאֶצְלוֹ הָיְתָה הַשָּׁעָה הַזֹּאת גְּדוֹלָה כְּמוֹ כַּמָּה שָׁעוֹת. וְהֶחָכָם הָיָה מִתְאַוֶּה לִשְׁמֹעַ סִפּוּרֵי מַעֲשִׂיּוֹת מֵהַמִּלְחָמוֹת וְכַיּוֹצֵא, וְאָמַר לוֹ בְּכָל פַּעַם: "לֹא תִּפְחַד", וְהָיָה לוֹ צַעַר גָּדוֹל.


It only took a few minutes to tell the story, but it seemed like hours. The rabbi wanted to hear more and more about their adventures. It was obvious that the attendant was suffering greatly, but the rabbi kept telling him not to worry.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:6)


וְהָיָה בְּדַעְתּוֹ לִקְנוֹת מַצּוֹת וָיַיִן וְלַחֲזֹר אֶת עַצְמוֹ לְהַסְּפִינָה. אַךְ ה' יִתְבָּרַךְ שָׁלַח לוֹ בְּמַחֲשַׁבְתּוֹ: הֱיוֹת שֶׁבִּהְיוֹתָם בָּאַכְסַנְיָא בִּטְבֶרְיָה שָׁמְעוּ מֵרַבִּי צְבִי הָארְקֶער, שֶׁאִשְׁתּוֹ מִמִּשְׁפַּחַת פְרֶענְקֶען (ספרדים) וְשֶׁהִיא מְיֻחֶסֶת גְּדוֹלָה, וְאָמַר שֶׁיֵּשׁ לָהּ אָח בְּרַאדִישׁ חָכָם גָּדוֹל וְצַדִּיק. וְעָלָה זֹאת עַל דַּעְתּוֹ, אַךְ לֹא יָדַע שְׁמוֹ. רַק שְׁמָהּ שֶׁל אֵשֶׁת רַבִּי צְבִי הַנַּ"ל יָדַע.


The attendant felt pressed because he wanted to buy *matzot* and wine and return to the ship before sundown. Suddenly God sent him another thought. While in Tiberias, he had heard Rabbi Zvi Harker mention that his wife came from a renowned Sephardic family. She had a brother in Rhodes who was a great sage and tzaddik. He could not remember the brother’s name, but he did recall the name of Rabbi Zvi’s wife.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:7)


וְתֵכֶף וּמִיָּד שָׁאַל עַל זֶה הֶחָכָם שֶׁיֵּשׁ לוֹ אָחוֹת בִּטְבֶרְיָה. וּמִיָּד שָׁלְחוּ אֶל הֶחָכָם הַנַּ"ל. וּבָא אֶצְלוֹ הָאִישׁ שֶׁל רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל. וּכְבָר סִפְּרוּ לְהֶחָכָם כָּל מַה שֶּׁשָּׁמְעוּ מִפִּי הָאִישׁ מִבֵּית הֶחָכָם הָרִאשׁוֹן הַנַּ"ל.


He asked the rabbi if he knew a sage who had a sister in Tiberias, and the rabbi said he did. He notified this sage and told the attendant to hurry to his house. By the time the attendant arrived, the sage had already been apprised of everything the attendant had told the chief rabbi.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:8)


וְתֵכֶף בְּבוֹאוֹ אֵצֶל הֶחָכָם הַזֶּה אָמַר לוֹ: "אֵין שׁוּם פַּחַד בָּזֶה, וְסִפּוּר מַעֲשִׂיּוֹת תְּסַפֵּר לִי אִם יִרְצֶה הַשֵּׁם עַל הַסֵּדֶר". וְהָיָה בְּלִבּוֹ תְּמִיהָה גְּדוֹלָה, כִּי לֹא עָלְתָה עַל דַּעְתּוֹ שֶׁתִּהְיֶה הַיְּשׁוּעָה כָּל־כָּךְ בִּמְהֵרָה עַד שֶׁיִּזְכֶּה לִהְיוֹת כָּאן עַל הַסֵּדֶר.


As soon as the attendant walked in, the sage told him, “There is nothing to be afraid of. You will tell me the whole story at the Seder.” The attendant could hardly believe his ears. He trusted in God, but never imagined that salvation would come so quickly. Never in his wildest dreams did he imagine that he would spend the Pesach Seder in a Jewish house.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:9)


וְאָמַר לוֹ הֶחָכָם הַזֶּה: "תֵּדַע, שֶׁזֶּה הַקַּפִּיטָן כְּבָר עִקַּלְנוּ אוֹתוֹ עִם הַסְּפִינָה, עַד שֶׁיַּנִּיחַ אֶתְכֶם לָצֵאת מִסְּפִינָתוֹ, וּמָעוֹת צְרִיכִים לִתֵּן לוֹ כַּמָּה שֶׁיִּרְצֶה, וְטוֹב לְהוֹדוֹת לַה' אֲשֶׁר הִצִּיל אֶתְכֶם וְהִפְלִיא עִמָּכֶם נִסִּים וְנִפְלָאוֹת שֶׁזְּכִיתֶם לָבוֹא לְכָאן, וְשֶׁהַקַּפִּיטָן יְבֻלְבַּל דַּעְתּוֹ וְיַנִּיחַ אוֹתְךָ לִכְנֹס אֶל הָעִיר. כִּי זֶה הַקַּפִּיטָן הוּא גַּזְלָן מְפֻרְסָם וְהוּא מֻשְׁרָשׁ בְּרִשְׁעָתוֹ, כִּי יִחוּסוֹ הוּא מֵחֲמֵשֶׁת סַרְנֵי פְּלִשְׁתִּים כִּי כָּךְ הוּא חוֹתֵם עַצְמוֹ.


The sage said, “We have already placed a warrant of seizure on the captain and the ship, and he will allow you to leave the ship. Of course, he will first have to be given whatever sum he requests. It is good to give thanks to God (Psalms 92:2), Who saved you in such a miraculous manner.

“The fact that you got to the city was a miracle in itself. I cannot imagine how the captain became so confused that he let you come here. He is a known thief and his wickedness has roots going back countless generations. He is a descendant of the Five Lords of the Philistines,⁵⁹ which is how he signs his name.

footnotes:
⁵⁹ Joshua 13:3; Judges 3:3; I Samuel 6:16.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:10)


וְעַכְשָׁו אֵין פְּנַאי לִשְׁמֹעַ סִפּוּרֵי מַעֲשִׂיּוֹת. לֵךְ עִמִּי לְרוֹפֵא לְגַלֵּחַ אוֹתְךָ לִכְבוֹד יוֹם־טוֹב". וְכָךְ הָיָה, הוֹלִיכוּהוּ מִיָּד וְגִלְּחוּ אוֹתוֹ וְרָחֲצוּ אוֹתוֹ וְנָתַן לוֹ קַאוֶוע לִשְׁתּוֹת.


“But now there is no time to listen to stories. Let us go to the barber and get you a haircut for the festival.” Immediately they went to the barber and the attendant had his hair cut. After he had bathed, they conversed over a cup of coffee.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:11)


וְשָׁאַל אוֹתוֹ: "מִי הוּא הַשֵּׁנִי?"


In the course of the conversation, the sage said, “We heard that you are traveling with another man. Who is he?”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:12)


וְאָמַר לוֹ: "שְׁמַעתֶּם מֵהַבַּעַל־שֵׁם־טוֹב?"


“Have you ever heard of the Baal Shem Tov?” the attendant asked.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:13)


וְהֵשִׁיב: "הֵן", כִּי הַסְּפָרִים שֶׁל הַגָּאוֹן הֶחָסִיד אַב־בֵּית־דִּין דִּקְהִלַּת קֹדֶשׁ פּוּלְנָאָה נִמְצָאִים שָׁם.


“Of course," the sage replied. "We are quite familiar with the works of the renowned chassid of Polonnoye.”⁶⁰, the first book discussing the Baal Shem Tov’s teachings, was published in 1780.

footnotes:
⁶⁰ Rabbi Jacob Joseph of Polonnoye (1740-1810), one of the chief disciples of the Baal Shem Tov. His Toldos Yaakov Yosef


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:14)


וְאָמַר לוֹ: שֶׁהוּא נֶכְדּוֹ, וְגַם הוּא נֶכֶד לְרַבִּי נַחְמָן הָרִידֶנְקֶער, "וּבְעַצְמוֹ הוּא חָכָם כַּאֲשֶׁר תִּרְאוּ בְּעֵינֵיכֶם".


The attendant then told him, “The man traveling with me is a great-grandson of the Baal Shem Tov. He is also a grandson of Rabbi Nachman Horodenker. And as you shall soon see, he is also a sage in his own right.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:15

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:15](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:15)


וְהָיָה לְזֶה הֶחָכָם שִׂמְחָה כְּפוּלָה, כִּי אָמַר: "אֲפִלּוּ לִפְדּוֹת אֲנָשִׁים פְּשׁוּטִים הוּא מִצְוָה רַבָּה וּבִפְרָט שֶׁהִזְמִין לוֹ ה' לִפְדּוֹת אָדָם גָּדוֹל כָּזֶה וּמְיֻחָס מִגֶּזַע קְדוֹשִׁים כָּאֵלֶּה".


The sage was overjoyed. “To rescue even a simple person is a very great mitzvah," he said. "But now God has given me the opportunity to redeem a man who descends from such holy roots!”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:16

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:16](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:16)


וְזֵרַז אֶת עַצְמוֹ עִם עוֹד אֲנָשִׁים לִנְסֹעַ אֵלָיו. אַךְ בְּתוֹךְ־כָּךְ, נִזְדַּמֵּן שֶׁפָּגְעוּ יִשְׁמְעֵאלִים זֶה בָּזֶה וְנִתְקוֹטְטוּ אֶחָד עִם חֲבֵרוֹ עַד שֶׁנֶּהֶרְגוּ בֵּינֵיהֶם כַּמָּה יִשְׁמְעֵאלִים בַּשּׁוּק. וְתֵכֶף כְּשֶׁשָּׁמְעוּ זֹאת בָּרַח הֶחָכָם הַנַּ"ל עִם כָּל הָאֲנָשִׁים לַחֲצֵרוֹ, וְגַם אֶת הָאִישׁ שֶׁל רַבֵּנוּ הַנַּ"ל לָקְחוּ עִמָּהֶם גַּם־כֵּן וְסָגְרוּ אֶת עַצְמָם סָגוֹר וּמְסֻגָּר וְלֹא יָצְאוּ הַחוּצָה בְּעֶרֶךְ כַּמָּה שָׁעוֹת. וְלֹא יָכְלוּ לִנְסֹעַ אֵלָיו, כִּי כְּבָר פָּנָה הַיּוֹם.


The sage hurried with a small group of men to fetch the Rebbe. Meanwhile, two groups of Turks clashed with each other, resulting in a fight in which several people were killed in the main thoroughfare. When they saw what was happening, the sage and his companions took the Rebbe’s attendant and fled to the sage’s courtyard. They locked themselves in and were afraid to leave for several hours. By the time it was safe to leave, the sun was beginning to set and it was too close to the festival to go and get the Rebbe.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:17

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:17](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:17)


וְעָנָה הֶחָכָם וְאָמַר לוֹ: "תֵּדַע שֶׁאֲנִי מִנְּעוּרַי עוֹסֵק בְּתוֹרָה וַעֲבוֹדָה וּבְמִצְווֹת וּמַעֲשִׂים טוֹבִים, וּמִן הַסְּתָם יֵשׁ לִי שָׂכָר בִּשְׁבִיל זֶה חוּץ חֵלֶק עוֹלָם הַבָּא שֶׁיֵּשׁ לִי בִּכְלַל יִשְׂרָאֵל. זֹאת לָכֶם בְּמַשְׁכּוֹן: בְּאִם ח"ו לֹא אֶשְׁתַּדֵּל וְאֶתְאַמֵּץ אֶת עַצְמִי לְהוֹצִיא אֶתְכֶם, יֹאבַד הַכֹּל כַּאֲשֶׁר לַכֹּל.


The sage then spoke up and said to the attendant, “From the time I was a child I have devoted my life to Torah, prayer and good deeds. I am sure that God will reward me for this. Besides this, I also have a portion in the World to Come along with every other Jew.⁶¹ 11:1. The present interpretation, that this is in addition to the reward for one's deeds, is a novel one. I am putting all this up as security. If, Heaven forbid, I do not exert myself to do everything in my power to free you, let me lose it all!

footnotes:
⁶¹ Sanhedrin


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:18

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:18](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:18)


וְאֵין כַּוָּנָתִי לְהִתְפָּאֵר לְפָנֶיךָ, רַק כְּדֵי שֶׁתַּאֲמִין לִי שֶׁאֵין אֲנִי מַפְקִיר אֶתְכֶם ח"ו, אַךְ אֵין פְּנַאי לַעֲסֹק בָּזֶה הַיּוֹם.


“My intention is not to boast, but to assure you that I am not abandoning you, Heaven forbid. There is simply no time to complete the task today.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:19

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:19](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:19)


בְּכֵן תִּקַּח עִמְּךָ מַצּוֹת, הֵן מַצָּה שֶׁמוּרָה הֵן שְׁאָר מַצּוֹת אִם תִּרְצוּ לֶאֱכֹל, וְיַיִן טוֹב מֵהַמֻּבְחָר, וִירָקוֹת וּשְׁאָר דְּבָרִים הַשַּׁיָּכִים לְהַסֵּדֶר".


“Now, hurry! Take all the matzah that you need for the Seder. I have *shemurah* *matzah*⁶² 40a; *Orach Chaim* 453:4. as well as regular matzah if you want it. Take some of my best wine and greens, and everything else you will need tonight. Take whatever you need.”

footnotes:
⁶² “Watched matzah,” which is prepared from wheat that is safeguarded from dampness from the time of its harvest; see Pesachim


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:20

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:20](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:20)


וְנָסַע בְּעַצְמוֹ הָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל וְחָזַר אַל הַסְּפִינָה הַגְּדוֹלָה שֶׁהָיָה שָׁם רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל לְבַדּוֹ. וּבְּבוֹאוֹ לְשָׁם אֶל הַסְּפִינָה הַגְּדוֹלָה שֶׁהָיְתָה עוֹמֶדֶת רָחוֹק מִן הָעִיר, תֵּכֶף כְּשֶׁשָּׁמַע רַבֵּנוּ אֶת קוֹלוֹ, שָׂמַח מְאֹד שִׂמְחָה גְּדוֹלָה, וְרָץ לְנֶגְדּוֹ, וְאָמַר לוֹ: "בָּרוּךְ ה' שֶׁאֲנִי רוֹאֶה אוֹתְךָ בַּחַיִּים! כִּי אָמַרְתִּי אוּלַי טִבְּעוּ אוֹתְךָ בַּיָּם וְגַם אוֹתִי יְטַבְּעוּ בַּיָּם. וְעַכְשָׁו בָּרוּךְ ה' הַנּוֹתֵן לַיָּעֵף כֹּחַ, כִּי לֹא הָיָה לִי עוֹד כֹּחַ לִסְבֹּל הַיִּסּוּרִים עַד בּוֹאֲךָ כִּי אָמַרְתִּי אָמוּת עַד בּוֹאֲךָ", כִּי הָיָה לְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל כִּלְיוֹן עֵינַיִם מְאֹד, בְּכָל אוֹתָן הַשָּׁעוֹת שֶׁנִּפְרַד הָאִישׁ מִמֶּנּוּ וְנִשְׁאַר לְבַדּוֹ עַל הַסְּפִינָה, וְהָיָה עוֹמֵד וּמְצַפֶּה וְעֵינָיו כָּלוֹת כָּל הַיּוֹם עַל בִּיאָתוֹ.


The attendant returned to his ship, which was moored far from the city. As soon as he boarded the ship, he called out. When the Rebbe heard his voice, he was overjoyed and ran to greet him. The Rebbe had spent all that day waiting with yearning eyes for a sign of his companion. As long as the other was gone, he stood there, his eyes growing weary.

The Rebbe said, “Thank God, you’re still alive! I was sure that they had drowned you and would soon do the same to me. But now, thank God Who gives strength to the weary.⁶³ You have no idea how much I suffered while you were gone. I no longer had the strength to bear it and was sure that I would die!”

footnotes:
⁶³ Morning Blessings, from Isaiah 40:29.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:21

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:21](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:21)


וְהִתְפַּלְּלוּ, וְסִדְּרוּ אֶת הַסֵּדֶר כָּרָאוּי, וְאָכְלוּ וְשָׁתוּ. וְסִפֵּר לוֹ הָאִישׁ כָּל הַדְּבָרִים הַנַּ"ל וְשָׂמַח שִׂמְחָה גְּדוֹלָה.


They prayed, and then were able to have a proper Seder. It was a most happy meal, and as they ate, the attendant told the Rebbe everything that had happened.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:22

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:22](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:22)


וּבְיוֹם רִאשׁוֹן שֶׁל פֶּסַח, בָּאָה הַפְּקֻדָּה שֶׁיּוֹלִיכוּ אֶת הַסְּפִינָה אֶל הָעִיר וְלִקְשֹׁר אוֹתָהּ אֶל הַסְּפָר. וְהָיוּ שָׁם גַּם־כֵּן לַיְלָה שְׁנִיָּה בַּסְּפִינָה.


On the first day of Pesach, the order came to bring the ship to the city and tie it up at the dock. The Rebbe and his attendant remained on the ship for the second night of Pesach.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:23

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:23](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:23)


וּבַיּוֹם הַשֵּׁנִי הָלַךְ הָאִישׁ שֶׁל רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל אֶל הָעִיר לְבֵית־הַכְּנֶסֶת לְהִתְפַּלֵּל, וְאַחַר יְצִיאָתוֹ מִבֵּית־הַכְּנֶסֶת הָלַךְ עִם הֶחָכָם לְבֵיתוֹ לֶאֱכֹל סְעֻדַּת יוֹם־טוֹב, וְאָכְלוּ וְשָׁתוּ. וּבִשְׁעַת הַסְּעֻדָּה אָמַר הֶחָכָם תּוֹרָה מַה שֶּׁחִדֵּשׁ בְּעַצְמוֹ, וְגַם בְּשֵׁם אֲחֵרִים, וְגַם מִצַּדִּיקֵי דּוֹרֵנוּ שֶׁבִּמְדִינָתֵנוּ. וּבִקֵּשׁ מֵהָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל שֶׁיֹּאמַר גַּם־כֵּן תּוֹרָה מֵרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל. וְאָמַר לוֹ אֵיזֶה דָּבָר בִּשְׁמוֹ, וְהָיָה לוֹ נַחַת גָּדוֹל מְאֹד, וְאָמַר: שִׂישׂוּ בְּנֵי מֵעַי וְכוּ'.


The next day, the Rebbe’s attendant went into the city to pray in the synagogue. After the service, he was invited to the sage’s house for the festive meal. During the meal, the sage delivered an original Torah lesson. Then he repeated some thoughts from contemporary tzaddikim, including those of Europe. He invited his guest to discourse on the Torah, and the attendant repeated some of the Rebbe’s lessons. The sage enjoyed them immensely, saying, “Such words make me happy deep inside” (*Bava Metzia* 83b).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:24

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:24](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:24)


וְאַחַר הַסְּעֻדָּה, חָזַר אֶל הַסְּפִינָה וְהַגַּבַּאי הָלַךְ אַחֲרָיו וְנָשָׂא מַאֲכָלִים טוֹבִים לְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל. וּבְבוֹא הָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל עַל הַסְּפִינָה הָיָה נִרְאֶה פָּנָיו כְּמוֹ מִי שֶׁהוּא בְּעַצְבוּת וּמָרָה שְׁחוֹרָה. וְכִרְאוֹת אוֹתוֹ רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל נִדְמָה לוֹ שֶׁמֻּכְרָח לִהְיוֹת שֶׁנִּתְהַפֵּךְ הַדָּבָר וּמִי יוֹדֵעַ מַה נּוֹלַד הַיּוֹם וְלֹא יָכוֹל לֶאֱכֹל מֵחֲמַת צַעַר.


After the meal, the attendant returned to the ship, followed by the attendant of the sage carrying choice food for the Rebbe. But the Rebbe saw that his attendant's face looked utterly depressed. The Rebbe was sure that things had taken a turn for the worse, for who knows what had happened that day? (*Yebamot* 63b). He was so distressed about this that he could not eat.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:25

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:25](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:25)


וּבֶאֱמֶת, הָיָה מֵחֲמַת שֶׁהֶחָכָם אָנַס אוֹתוֹ שָׁם לִשְׁתּוֹת יַיִן הַרְבֵּה, וְנִשְׁתַּכֵּר, וּמֵחֲמַת זֶה הָיוּ נִרְאִין פָּנָיו זוֹעֲפוֹת כְּשֶׁבָּא אֶל רַבֵּנוּ. וְרַבֵּנוּ שָׁאַל אוֹתוֹ וְהוּא לֹא הֵשִׁיב כְּלוּם.


What the Rebbe did not know was that the sage had pressed his attendant to drink so much wine that he had become drunk. This was why he looked so depressed. The Rebbe tried to question him, but he was in too much of a stupor to respond.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:26

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:26](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:26)


רַק אַחַר־כָּךְ הִנִּיחַ עַצְמוֹ הָאִישׁ לִישֹׁן אֵיזֶה שָׁעוֹת. וְאַחַר־כָּךְ קָם מֵהַשֵּׁנָה בִּשְׂחוֹק וְאָמַר לְרַבֵּנוּ: "לֹא יְדַעְתֶּם שֶׁהֵם שִׁכְּרוּ אוֹתִי כִּי אֲנָסוּנִי לִשְׁתּוֹת יוֹתֵר מִדַּי מֵחֲמַת שִׂמְחָתוֹ".


The Rebbe's attendant went to sleep for a few hours. It was only when he woke up that he realized what had happened. He laughed and told the Rebbe, “Didn’t you know that I was drunk? The sage was so happy that he kept asking me to drink. Before I knew it, I had taken too much wine.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 23:27

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:27](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/23:27)


וְסִפֵּר לוֹ כָּל הַדְּבָרִים, אֵיךְ שֶׁהָיָה נוֹהֵג עִמּוֹ הֶחָכָם, וְהָיָה לוֹ נַחַת וְשִׂמְחָה. וְאָז אָכַל סְעֻדָּתוֹ.


He told the Rebbe everything that had happened at the sage’s house. Fully reassured, the Rebbe ate his festive meal joyfully.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/24:1)


וּבְיוֹם רִאשׁוֹן דְּחֹל הַמּוֹעֵד הָלַךְ הָאִישׁ לְהֶחָכָם, וְצִוָּה לוֹ הֶחָכָם: לִקַּח בִּגְנֵבָה כָּל הַמָּעוֹת שֶׁלָּהֶם וְלַהֲבִיאוֹ הָעִיר, כְּדֵי שֶׁלֹּא יִגְזֹל מֵהֶם מְעוֹתֵיהֶם. וְעָשָׂה כָּךְ.


On the first day of *Chol HaMoed*, the Rebbe's attendant returned to the sage’s house. The sage advised him to smuggle all their money out of the ship so the captain would not be able to steal it.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/24:2)


אַחַר־כָּךְ הָלַךְ הֶחָכָם עִם שְׁנֵי גְּבִירִים וְאָמְרוּ לְהַקַּפִּיטָן: "תֵּן לָנוּ שְׁנֵי הַנְּפָשׁוֹת הַלָּלוּ".


This accomplished, the sage went to the captain together with two prominent members of the community. They said, “Give us the two Jews you are holding.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 24:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/24:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/24:3)


וְהֵשִׁיב לָהֶם: "מַה שַּׁיָּכוּת יֵשׁ לָכֶם לַנְּפָשׁוֹת הָאֵלֶּה? אֲנִי מֵהֶפְקֵר זָכִיתִי בָּהֶם!".


“What business do you have with them?" the captain replied. "When they came on board, they belonged to no one!”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 24:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/24:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/24:4)


וְסִפֵּר לָהֶם אֶת כָּל הַתְּלָאוֹת אֲשֶׁר מְצָאָתַם בַּדֶּרֶךְ. וְאָמַר: "וְלֹא זֹאת בִּלְבַד מַה שֶּׁאֲנִי מְסַפֵּר לָכֶם, אֶלָּא שֶׁכִּמְעַט לֹא הָיָה רֶגַע בְּלֹא פֶּגַע. וְאֵלּוּ הַנְּפָשׁוֹת כְּבָר הָיִינוּ יְכוֹלִים לְטָבְעָם בַּיָּם אוֹ לְמָכְרָם לַיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים וְכָל הַמָּעוֹת וְהַחֲפָצִים שֶׁלָּהֶם הָיוּ הַכֹּל שֶׁלָּנוּ בְּאֵין פּוֹצֶה פֶּה וּמְצַפְצֵף כְּלָל.


The captain proceeded to tell them about all the travail that had befallen them on the way (Exodus 18:8). He added, “What I told you is nothing! During the entire voyage there was not an instant without distress.⁶⁴; *Shelah* 2:138a, quoted in "*His Wisdom*" #308. We could have drowned these two or sold them to the Arabs long ago, and all their money and possessions would have been ours. No one at all would have said a word or complained (cf. Isaiah 10:14).

footnotes:
⁶⁴ In Hebrew, ein rega belo pega


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 24:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/24:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/24:5)


אַךְ מַה לַּעֲשׂוֹת כִּי מַזָּלָם גָּדוֹל עַד לֵב הַשָּׁמַיִם, כִּי לֹא דַּי שֶׁה' יִתְבָּרַךְ עָשָׂה לָהֶם נֵס כָּזֶה שֶׁבְּמַזָּלָם הִגִּיעַ פִּתְאֹם הַסְּפִינָה לְפֹה, אַף גַּם זֹאת נַעֲשָׂה לָהֶם נֵס בְּתוֹךְ נֵס שֶׁה' יִתְבָּרַךְ הֵסִיר לִבִּי וְסִכֵּל אֶת דַּעְתִּי עַד שֶׁלָּקַחְתִּי אֶת אֶחָד מֵהֶם לְתוֹךְ הָעִיר הַזֹּאת.


“But now, what can I do? These two must have luck that reaches to the high heavens. First of all, God performed a miracle and the ship found its way here. This would have been enough! But then there was a miracle within a miracle (*Shabbat* 97a), and God made me crazy enough to actually bring one of them into the city.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 24:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/24:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/24:6)


וְעַכְשָׁו בְּוַדַּאי שׁוּב אֵינִי רַשַּׁאי אֲפִלּוּ לִקַּח מְעוֹתֵיהֶם, רַק כְּדֵי שֶׁלֹּא יִתְרַעֲמוּ הָעֲבָדִים שֶׁלִּי, תְּנוּ לִי מָאתַיִם טָאלֶער וּקְחוּ אוֹתָם מֵעַל הַסְּפִינָה".


“After all this, I really have no right to their money. So give me two hundred thaler to satisfy my crew, and take them!”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 24:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/24:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/24:7)


וְכָךְ הָיָה, שֶׁנָּתְנוּ לוֹ מִיָּד כְּמוֹ שֶׁאָמַר, וְהִצִּילוּ אוֹתָם מִיַּד הַגַּזְלָנִים, מִמָּוֶת לְחַיִּים, מִשִּׁעְבּוּד לִגְאֻלָּה.


They gave the captain the sum and the Rebbe and his attendant were saved. They were redeemed from death to life, from captivity to freedom.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:1)


וְאָז בָּאוּ שְׁנֵיהֶם אֶל הָעִיר, רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל עִם הָאִישׁ הַמְשַׁמֵּשׁ שֶׁלּוֹ הַנַּ"ל. וּבְבוֹאָם אֶל הָעִיר אָמְרוּ הַיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים שֶׁהֵם מְרַגְּלִים, וְנָפַל פַּחַד גָּדוֹל עַל הַסְּפָרַדִּים בְּעַצְמָם. וְתֵכֶף הֵבִיאוּ לָהֶם מַלְבּוּשִׁים שֶׁלָּהֶם, וְהֻכְרְחוּ לְהַלְבִּישׁ עַצְמָם בְּמַלְבּוּשִׁים שֶׁלָּהֶם כְּמִנְהָגָם.


The Rebbe and his attendant were brought to the city. As they walked through the streets, some Turks remarked that they looked like spies. The members of the community were highly alarmed. They immediately brought their guests their style of clothing and had them dress like the other Jews of Rhodes.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:2)


וְהָיָה רַבֵּנוּ בְּצַעַר גָּדוֹל מֵחֲמַת זֶה וְהָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל שָׂחַק. וְכָעַס עָלָיו רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל וְאָמַר לוֹ: "אֵין אַתָּה יוֹדֵעַ מֵהַקִּטְרוּג שֶׁיֵּשׁ עָלֵינוּ בָּעוֹלָם הָעֶלְיוֹן", וְאָמַר אָז דָּבָר נִפְלָא לְהָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל, וְאֵינוֹ רוֹצֶה לְגַלּוֹת.


The Rebbe's attendant took it as a joke, but the Rebbe was very troubled. He told his attendant, “Don’t you know that a great complaint is being directed against us on high?” He then told his attendant some wondrous secrets, but the latter would never reveal them.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 25:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:3)


וְנִתְקַבֵּל רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל לִפְנֵי הַחֲכָמִים, וְהוּטַב בְּעֵינֵיהֶם מְאֹד וְהֶחֱזִיקוּ אוֹתוֹ לְחִדּוּשׁ גָּדוֹל. וְאַחַר־כָּךְ בָּא רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, בְּעַצְמוֹ בְּשִׂמְחָה, וְאָמַר: "בָּרוּךְ ה' יִתְבָּרַךְ שֶׁזָּכִינוּ לְהַצָּלָה הַזֹּאת".


The Rebbe was received by the local rabbis and impressed them as being a very unique person. His mood became very joyful and he said, “Thank God, we were worthy of being rescued this way!”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 25:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:4)


וְהָיָה שָׁם בְּכָבוֹד גָּדוֹל מְאֹד עַד אַחַר הֶחָג. וְנָתְנוּ אַנְשֵׁי הָעִיר בְּעַצְמָם מִכִּיסָם הַמָאתַיִם טָאלֶער הַנַּ"ל, וְלֹא רָצוּ אַחַר־כָּךְ לַחֲזֹר לְקַבְּלָם מֵרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל שָׁם, אַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁהָיָה לוֹ מָעוֹת הַרְבֵּה שָׁם.


They stayed in Rhodes until after Pesach, and the Rebbe was treated with great honor. The community paid the two hundred thaler ransom out of their own pockets, and even though the Rebbe had sufficient funds, they would not let him repay them.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 25:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:5)


וְאַחַר הֶחָג, שָׂכְרוּ לָהֶם סְפִינָה לִסְטַנְבּוּל. וְנָתְנוּ לָהֶם אִגֶּרֶת – אוּלַי ח"ו יִזְדַּמֵּן לָהֶם עוֹד אֵיזֶה סִבָּה – שֶׁיַּעַמְדוּ בְּעֶזְרָם בְּכָל עִיר וָעִיר.


After the festival, the community booked them passage on a ship to Istanbul. It also provided them with a letter asking all communities along the way to help them if necessary.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 25:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:6)


וְהָלְכוּ עִם סְפִינָה זוֹ. וְשָׁם הָיוּ גְּרֶעקִין הַרְבֵּה וְהָיָה בָּהֶם עִפּוּשׁ. וְהֵם לֹא יָדְעוּ, אַךְ רָאוּ שֶׁמֵּתִים עַל הַסְּפִינָה. וּבָרוּךְ ה' שֶׁהָיָה רוּחַ סְעָרָה וְנָשָׂא הַסְּפִינָה בִּמְהִירוּת לִקְהִלַּת־קֹדֶשׁ סְטַנְבּוּל.


Many Greeks were on their ship, and a plague broke out among them at sea. The Rebbe and his attendant did not know the cause, but they saw people dying all around them. But God was with them, and a strong wind carried the ship rapidly to Istanbul. The high waves and great speed distressed them, but the fact that the ship traveled so quickly saved their lives.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 25:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:7)


וְאַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁהָיָה לָהֶם יִסּוּרִים מֵחֲמַת זַעֲפַת הַיָּם וּמֵחֲמַת שֶׁפָּרְחָה הַסְּפִינָה כָּל־כָּךְ בִּמְהִירוּת, אַךְ אַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן הָיָה לָהֶם לְטוֹבָה שֶׁעָבְרוּ בִּמְהִירוּת וּבָאוּ בַּיּוֹם הַשְּׁלִישִׁי לִקְהִלַּת־קֹדֶשׁ סְטַנְבּוּל. וְהָיוּ שָׁם עֲשָׂרָה יָמִים. וְגַם בִּסְטַנְבּוּל הָיָה לָהֶם סַכָּנָה, כִּי מֵחֲמַת שֶׁלֹּא הֶרְאוּ הַפְּרַשְׂפּוֹרְטִין (דַּרְכּוֹנִים) שָׁם בִּנְסִיעָתָם לְהָתָם, עַל־כֵּן לֹא רָצוּ עַכְשָׁו לְהַנִּיחָם מִשָּׁם. וְהֶעֱמִיסוּ עֲלֵיהֶם עֹל כָּבֵד לִתֵּן סַךְ עָצוּם מְאֹד בְּאֹפֶן שֶׁיַּשִּׂיגוּ הַפְּרַשְׂפּוֹרְטִין מֵהַתּוֹגָר. וְהַסַּךְ שֶׁצֻּוּוּ לִתֵּן הָיָה סַךְ עָצוּם מְאֹד שֶׁלֹּא הָיָה לָהֶם בִּיכָלְתָּם בְּשׁוּם אֹפֶן לְסַלְּקוֹ.


The ship arrived in Istanbul after only three days at sea, and they remained there another ten days. But even here, they were not out of danger. They had not shown their passports when they arrived, and now the officials would not let them leave. The only solution was to obtain a passport from the sultan, but the official demanded an exorbitant sum for such a document. The amount was so great, it was absolutely impossible for them to pay it.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 25:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:8)


וה' יִתְבָּרַךְ חָמַל עֲלֵיהֶם וְהִזְמִין לָהֶם אִישׁ א' שֶׁלֹּא יָדְעוּ אוֹתוֹ וְלֹא הִכִּירוּ אוֹתוֹ וְהָלַךְ לְאֵיזֶה שַׂר מֵהַיִּשְׁמְעֵאלִים וְגָנַב דַּעְתּוֹ וְאֵינָם יוֹדְעִים כְּלָל כַּמָּה נָתַן לוֹ. וְקִבֵּל קְוִויטְל (פֶּתֶק) לְהַנִּיחַ אוֹתָם מֵהַשַּׁעַר דִּקְהִלַּת קֹדֶשׁ הַנַּ"ל. וְהִזְהִיר אוֹתָם 'לְמַעַן הַשֵּׁם לְהַסְתִּיר הַדָּבָר מְאֹד מְאֹד'.


But God was with them, and they happened to meet a total stranger who agreed to help them. He went to a Turkish minister and wheedled what they needed from him. They had no idea how much he paid for it, but the stranger obtained a document allowing them to pass through the gate and leave the city. He cautioned them, “For God’s sake, don’t breathe a word about this.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 25:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:9)


וְעַל־יְדֵי־זֶה נִמְלְטוּ מִשָּׁם בְּשָׁלוֹם. וְהָלְכוּ עַל הַיָּם מִסְּטַנְבּוּל – לְגַלָּץ. וְהִגִּיעוּ לְאֵיזֶה עִיר עַל אִיֵּי הַיָּם, וְשָׁם לָקְחוּ אוֹתָם לְבֵית הָאֲסוּרִים וְהֻכְרְחוּ לִתֵּן אַרְבָּעָה אֲדֻמִּים בְּעַד כָּל נֶפֶשׁ.


They escaped Istanbul safely and traveled by sea to Galatz. Along the way, they landed on an island⁶⁵ and were held as prisoners. They were freed only after a fine of four rubles had been paid for each passenger.

footnotes:
⁶⁵ The only major island in the Black Sea is Serpent’s Island, opposite the mouth of the Danube. It is also called Fidonisi and Zmetnyj.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 25:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:10)


וּמִשָּׁם וָהָלְאָה לֹא אֵרַע לָהֶם שׁוּם דָּבָר, רַק זַעֲפַת הַיָּם הָיָה כַּמָּה פְּעָמִים. וּסְפִינָה אַחַת מְלֵאָה גְּרֶעקִין נִשְׁבָּרָה וְנִטְבְּעָה וְלֹא נִשְׁאֲרוּ מֵהֶם כִּי־אִם מְעַט דִּמְעַט.


The rest of their voyage was relatively peaceful except for a few storms at sea. They passed a ship filled with Greeks and saw it break up and sink; only a handful of passengers survived.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 25:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/25:11)


וַה' בְּרַחֲמָיו הִנְחָם לִמְחוֹז חֶפְצָם וְעָבְרוּ בְּשָׁלוֹם עַד שֶׁבָּאוּ לְגַלָּץ בְּשָׁלוֹם עַל חַג הַשָּׁבוּעוֹת.


God guided them toward their destination, and they arrived safely in Galatz for the festival of Shavuot.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/26:1)


וּבִנְסִיעָתָם מִגַּלָּץ לְבֵיתָם, נָסְעוּ דֶּרֶךְ קְהִלַּת־קֹדֶשׁ יַאס. וְהָיָה שָׁם עִפּוּשׁ גָּדוֹל וְהָיָה לָהֶם יִסּוּרִים גְּדוֹלִים בְּכָל עִיר וָעִיר וּבְכָל כְּפָר וּכְפָר. וְגַם הָיָה קָשֶׁה לַעֲבֹר הַגְּרֶענִיץ (הַגְּבוּל) מֵחֲמַת זֶה, כִּי אָז לֹא הוֹעִיל אֲפִלּוּ עֲמִידַת קַארָאן טָאן (מַחֲנֵה הֶסְגֵּר שֶׁלְתוֹכוֹ מְכַנְסִים הַבָּאִים מִחוּץ לָאָרֶץ לִמְנִיעַת מַחֲלוֹת) מֵחֲמַת זֶה מִמְּקוֹמוֹת כָּאֵלֶּה.


On the way home from Galatz, they passed through Iasi and encountered a great plague there. The epidemic had spread to all the surrounding towns, making travel most difficult. It also caused them considerable trouble when they tried to cross the border. Coming from a plague area, even remaining in quarantine was not sufficient to let them cross.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/26:2)


וה' יִתְבָּרַךְ הָיָה בְּעֶזְרָם וְנִצּוֹלוּ מֵהַכֹּל, מִדֶּבֶר וְחֶרֶב וְרָעָב וְצִמָּאוֹן וּשְׁבִי דְּכֻלְּהוּ הַווֹ בְּהוּ. וּבְחֶסֶד הַשֵּׁם נִמְלְטוּ מִכֻּלָּם וּבָאוּ לְבֵיתָם בְּשָׁלוֹם.


But God was with them, and they were led safely through it all. They had seen plague and sword and famine and thirst and captivity, but with God’s help, they returned home safely.⁶⁶ #42, #43, #46). Immediately upon his return home, he went to visit Rabbi Shneur Zalman (later of Liadi) in Liozna (*Avenehah Barzel*, p. 34, #46).

footnotes:
