###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 26:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/26:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/26:3)


שָׁלוֹם בְּגוּפוֹ שָׁלוֹם בְּמָמוֹנוֹ שָׁלוֹם בְּתוֹרָתוֹ' כִּי זָכָה לְהַשָּׂגָה נִפְלָאָה בְּאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל גְּבֹהָה וַעֲצוּמָה מְאֹד עַד אֵין סוֹף וְאֵין תַּכְלִית.


They returned complete and intact in body, possessions and Torah.⁶⁷ 33b on Genesis 33:18. Cf. *Rashi* ad loc. For in the Holy Land, the Rebbe was worthy of attaining a wondrously lofty perception, and his vision knew no bounds.

footnotes:
⁶⁷ Shabbat


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/27:1)


עַד כֹּה סִפַּרְנוּ קְצָת מְעַט דִּמְעַט כְּטִפָּה מִן הַיָּם מֵעֹצֶם הַתְּלָאוֹת וְהַהַרְפַּתְקָאוֹת וְהַסַּכָּנוֹת הָעֲצוּמוֹת וְהַיִּסּוּרִים הַקָּשִׁים וְהַפְּחָדִים הַגְּדוֹלִים שֶׁהָיָה לְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, בַּנְּסִיעָה לְאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל בַּהֲלִיכָה וּבַחֲזָרָה. וה' יִתְבָּרַךְ הָיָה בְּעֶזְרוֹ, וְעָשָׂה עִמּוֹ נִסִּים נִפְלָאִים וְנוֹרָאִים בְּכָל־עֵת וּבְכָל רֶגַע וְזָכָה לִגְמֹר בִּשְׁלֵמוּת מַה שֶּׁחָפֵץ, וְנִכְנַס בְּשָׁלוֹם וְיָצָא בְּשָׁלוֹם וּבָא לְבֵיתוֹ בְּשָׁלוֹם.


All that we have told is less than a drop in the sea of all the anguish and terror the Rebbe suffered during his pilgrimage. The great danger is beyond description, but God was with him, performing awesome miracles every step of the way. He was worthy of attaining his goal and returning home safely. He entered in peace, left in peace (*Chagigah* 15b), and returned home in peace.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/28:1)


וְתֹקֶף עֹצֶם הַהַשָּׂגָה שֶׁהִשִּׂיג בְּאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל אִי־אֶפְשָׁר לְבָאֵר וּלְסַפֵּר כְּלָל, מַמָּשׁ לֹא נִשְׁמַע וְלֹא נִרְאָה כָּזֹאת שֶׁיִּזְכֶּה יְלוּד אִשָּׁה לְהַשָּׂגָה כָּזוֹ עַל יְדֵי אֲוִירָא דְּאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל. וְאִי־אֶפְשָׁר לְדַבֵּר וּלְסַפֵּר בָּזֶה.


The great perception that the Rebbe attained in the Holy Land is utterly beyond description. Never had one born of an earthly mother (*Shabbat* 88b) been worthy of attaining what he did through the air of the Land of Israel (*Bava Batra* 158b). Words are completely inadequate to describe it.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 28:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/28:2)


וְהָיָה מִתְפָּאֵר עַצְמוֹ מְאֹד בַּמֶּה שֶּׁזָּכָה לִהְיוֹת בְּאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל. וְגַם בְּסוֹף יָמָיו אָמַר שֶׁהוּא מְחַיֶּה עַצְמוֹ מְאֹד בַּמֶּה שֶּׁזָּכָה לִהְיוֹת בְּאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל. וְאָמַר: זֶה אֲנִי יוֹדֵעַ מִכְּבָר, שֶׁכְּשֶׁאָדָם רוֹצֶה לַעֲשׂוֹת אֵיזֶה דָּבָר שֶׁבִּקְדֻשָּׁה וְיֵשׁ לוֹ מְנִיעוֹת עַל זֶה וְכוּ' (כַּמְבֹאָר כָּל זֶה לְקַמָּן).


The Rebbe took great pride in the fact that he had been in the Holy Land. Toward the end of his life, he said that he derived great inspiration just from this. He said, “For a long time, I have known that when a person wants to do something holy, he must face great barriers” (*Likutey Moharan* I, 66:4; see “*His Wisdom*” #11).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:1)


וְזֶה הָאִישׁ שֶׁבִּזָּה אוֹתוֹ מְאֹד בִּסְטַנְבּוּל נִפְטַר מִיָּד בְּבִיאָתוֹ לְבֵיתוֹ.


The man who insulted the Rebbe so much in Istanbul died immediately upon his return home.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 29:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:2)


וְשָׁאַל הָאִישׁ הַנַּ"ל שֶׁהָיָה עִמּוֹ אֶת רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: הֲלֹא כְּתִיב גַּם עָנוֹשׁ לַצַּדִּיק לֹא טוֹב?


The Rebbe’s attendant asked Rebbe Nachman, “Is it not written, 'For the righteous to punish is also not good'?” (Proverbs 17:26; cf. *Sanhedrin* 105b).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 29:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:3)


וְהֵשִׁיב לוֹ מָשָׁל עַל זֶה: שֶׁהָיָה מֶלֶךְ אֶחָד, וְאָהַב אֶחָד מִבְּנֵי הַשְּׂרָרוֹת. וְחִבֵּב אוֹתוֹ, וְקֵרְבוֹ בְּכָל מִינֵי אַהֲבָה וְחִבּוּב.


The Rebbe answered him with the following parable: Once there was a king who was very fond of one of the young princes. He showed the prince all sorts of affection, doing everything he could to attract him.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 29:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:4)


וְאַחַר־כָּךְ כְּשֶׁהִגְדִּיל, הֵעֵז נֶגֶד הַמֶּלֶךְ.


The child became a young man. One day, he did something highly disrespectful to the king.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 29:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:5)


וְאָמַר הַמֶּלֶךְ: "תֵּדַע, שֶׁאַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁאֲנִי אוֹהֵב אוֹתְךָ מְאֹד, אַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן אִי־אֶפְשָׁר לִי לַעֲבֹר עַל דַּת וּמִשְׁפַּט הַמַּלְכוּת. וּמִשְׁפָּטְךָ חָרוּץ לְמִיתָה". וְתֵכֶף צִוָּה הַמֶּלֶךְ לְסָגְרוֹ בְּכַבְלֵי בַּרְזֶל וּנְתָנוֹ בְּבֵית הַסֹּהַר.


The king told him, “No matter how much I love you, it is still impossible to go against the law of the kingdom. The judgment has been passed, and your sentence is death.” The king commanded that the prince be bound in chains and imprisoned.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 29:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:6)


וְהִתְחִיל זֶה הַבֶּן שְׂרָרָה לְצַיֵּר בְּדַעְתּוֹ הַיִּסּוּרִים שֶׁלּוֹ שֶׁיִּהְיֶה לוֹ מִן הַמִּיתָה, וּרְאֵה שֶׁצַּעֲרוֹ לֹא יֻמְשַׁךְ זְמַן הַרְבֵּה כִּי־אִם עַד שֶׁיֵּהָרֵג.


The young prince began to think about his imminent death. In his mind, he could already feel the great pain he would suffer at the moment he would be killed. But he realized that this agony would only last an instant, and then he would be dead.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 29:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:7)


אֲבָל כְּשֶׁהִתְחִיל לְצַיֵּר בְּדַעְתּוֹ צַעַר הַמֶּלֶךְ, רָאָה שֶׁצַּעַר הַמֶּלֶךְ יִגְדַּל מְאֹד כִּי צַעַר הַמֶּלֶךְ יִהְיֶה תָּמִיד. כִּי הוּא יוֹדֵעַ שֶׁהַמֶּלֶךְ אוֹהֵב אוֹתוֹ מְאֹד וְיִהְיֶה לוֹ גַּעְגּוּעִים גְּדוֹלִים אַחֲרָיו, וְצַעַר גָּדוֹל מְאֹד לְעוֹלָם. וְהָיָה לוֹ רַחֲמָנוּת עַל צַעַר שֶׁל הַמֶּלֶךְ יוֹתֵר מִצַּעֲרוֹ. וְחָשַׁב מַחֲשָׁבוֹת אֵיךְ לְהַצִּיל אֶת הַמֶּלֶךְ מִצַּעֲרוֹ. וְנִתְיַשֵּׁב כָּךְ: הֲלֹא בּוּשָׁה חֲשׁוּבָה כְּמוֹ מִיתָה.


Then he began to imagine the anguish of the king. He knew that the king loved him very much and still yearned for his presence. He understood how much pain his death would cause the king, and that this anguish would last forever. The prince worried more about the king’s suffering than his own. He thought and thought about a way to spare the king this pain. Finally, an idea came to him: Shame is considered equal to death (*Bava Metzia* 58b).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 29:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:8)


וְצִוָּה לְהַשַּׂר שֶׁל הַתְּפִיסָה לְהַכְנִיסוֹ לִפְנֵי הַמֶּלֶךְ. וְאָמַר אֶל הַמֶּלֶךְ: "הָאֱמֶת יָדַעְתִּי כִּי צַעַרְךָ גָּדוֹל מִצַּעֲרִי אַךְ אִי־אֶפְשָׁר לְךָ לַעֲבֹר עַל חָק וְדַת הַמַּלְכוּת. בְּכֵן זֹאת הָעֵצָה הַיְּעוּצָה: שֶׁתְּסַבֵּב תַּחְבּוּלוֹת, שֶׁיְּבַיֵּשׁ אֶחָד אוֹתִי בָּרַבִּים, וְהַבּוּשָׁה חֲשׁוּבָה כְּמוֹ מִיתָה.


He asked the jail keeper to bring him before the king. Standing before the king, he said, “Truthfully, I know that your suffering is greater than mine. I also realize that it is impossible for you to ignore the law of your kingdom. But I think I know a way out. If you can arrange for someone to publicly shame me, the sentence will be fulfilled, for shame is equal to death.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 29:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:9)


עַל־כֵּן תִּרְאֶה לְהוֹצִיא מֵהַשְּׁבוּיִים גַּזְלָן אֶחָד שֶׁדִּינוֹ לַהֲרֵגָה, וַאֲנִי אֶתְגָּרֶה בּוֹ עַד שֶׁיִּכְעַס עָלַי וִיבַזֶּה אוֹתִי וְיַכֶּה אוֹתִי בָּרַבִּים, וְיַגִּיעַ לִי מִזֶּה בּוּשָׁה גְּדוֹלָה, וְיִהְיֶה נֶחֱשָׁב כְּמוֹ מִיתָה.


“Bring me a prisoner who has already been condemned to death. I will provoke him until he becomes angry and publicly insults and beats me. I will be bitterly humiliated, and this will be the same as death.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 29:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:10)


וְאַחַר־כָּךְ יִקְחוּ אֶת הַגַּזְלָן הַחַיָּב מִיתָה בְּלֹא זֶה וִימִיתוּהוּ כְּדִינוֹ. וְהָעוֹלָם יִהְיוּ סוֹבְרִים שֶׁבִּשְׁבִיל שֶׁבִּזָּה עֶבֶד הַמֶּלֶךְ הֶחָבִיב נֶהֱרַג, וְלֹא יִתְחַלֵּל כְּבוֹד הַמַּלְכוּת וְהָעֶבֶד שֶׁלּוֹ".


“Then take this condemned criminal and execute him according to his sentence. The people will think that he was killed because he insulted one of the king’s favorite servants. In this way, neither the honor of the king nor his servants will be impinged.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 29:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:11)


כָּךְ לִפְעָמִים אֶחָד מְבַזֶּה אֶת הַצַּדִּיק. וּבֶאֱמֶת הוּא עוֹשֶׂה טוֹבָה גְּדוֹלָה לְהַצַּדִּיק, כִּי מְכַפֵּר לוֹ עַל זֶה מַה שֶּׁהָיָה חַיָּב לְהִסְתַּלֵּק, וְנִתְחַלֵּף עַל בּוּשָׁה זוֹ.


The same is true in this case, the Rebbe explained. A man might insult a tzaddik and without realizing it, be doing him a great service. The tzaddik may have been condemned to death for some misdeed. The shame he experiences might then take the place of his death sentence.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 29:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:12)


אַךְ אַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן אֵין זֶה כְּבוֹד שָׁמַיִם שֶׁלֹּא לִקַּח נְקָמָה מִזֶּה שֶׁבִּזָּה עֶבֶד הַמֶּלֶךְ הֶחָבִיב לוֹ מְאֹד. וְלַעֲנשׁ אֶת זֶה שֶׁבִּזָּה אֶת הַצַּדִּיק בִּשְׁבִיל זֶה גַּם־כֵּן לֹא נִיחָא קָמֵהּ, כִּי גַּם עָנוֹשׁ לַצַּדִּיק לֹא טוֹב.


Still, this tzaddik is a beloved servant of the king, and it is not fitting that the insult go unpunished. But to punish the man just for insulting the tzaddik is also not desirable, “for the righteous to punish is also not good.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 29:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:13)


עַל־כֵּן הוּא יִתְבָּרַךְ מִסִּבּוֹת מִתְהַפֵּךְ, וּמַעֲמִיד עַל הַצַּדִּיק אִישׁ כָּזֶה שֶׁהוּא חַיָּב מִיתָה וָעֹנֶשׁ מִכְּבָר. וּמְזַמְּנָם שְׁנֵיהֶם לְפֻנְדָּק אֶחָד, וְזֶה מְבַזֶּה אֶת הַצַּדִּיק, וְהַצַּדִּיק נִפְטָר בָּזֶה מִן דִּינוֹ כַּנַּ"ל. וְזֶה הָאִישׁ נֶעֱנָשׁ אַחַר כָּךְ וְנִתְקַדֵּשׁ שֵׁם שָׁמַיִם שֶׁבִּשְׁבִיל כְּבוֹד הַצַּדִּיק נֶעֱנַשׁ. וּבֶאֱמֶת 'גַּבְרָא קְטִילָא קְטַל' כִּי כְּבָר הוּא חַיָּב עָנְשׁוֹ מִקֹּדֶם.


Therefore, God brings it about that the man who insults the tzaddik should be one who has already been condemned to death. They may happen to be in the same inn (*Makkot* 10b), and this individual insults the tzaddik. The shame saves the tzaddik from a more permanent judgment. In order to protect the tzaddik’s honor, the other is then punished. The name of Heaven is thus sanctified (cf. *Yebamot* 79a; *Sotah* 8b) – but actually it was a dead man who was killed, for he was previously condemned.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 29:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/29:14)


וְדַרְכֵי ה' יְשָׁרִים וְלֹא עַוְלָתָה בּוֹ.


Thus we see that God’s ways are straight (Hosea 14:10), and there is no unrighteousness in Him (Psalms 92:16).


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/30:1)


וּבְאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל הָיָה עוֹסֵק בְּתוֹרָה וּתְפִלָּה, וּבְכָל יוֹם כָּתַב בְּעַצְמוֹ תּוֹרָתוֹ.


While in the Holy Land, the Rebbe was constantly involved with Torah and prayer. Every day, he would write down what he perceived in the Torah.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/30:2)


וְהָיָה מִתְפָּאֵר מְאֹד וְאָמַר: שֶׁהַחִלּוּק בֵּין תּוֹרַת חוּץ־לָאָרֶץ לְתוֹרַת אֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל כִּרְחוֹק מִזְרָח מִמַּעֲרָב.


When the Rebbe spoke about this, he said, “The difference between the understanding of the Torah in the Holy Land and elsewhere is like the difference between east and west.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 30:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/30:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/30:3)


וּבְכָל יוֹם וָיוֹם הָלְכוּ אֵלָיו הַגְּדוֹלִים עִם הַקְּטַנִּים לְשַׁמְּשׁוֹ כְּדֵי לִשְׁמֹעַ חָכְמַת תּוֹרָתוֹ הַקְּדוֹשָׁה וְהַנּוֹרָאָה מְאֹד מְאֹד.


Every day, people came to him, great and small alike, to serve him and thereby hear his awesome holy words of wisdom.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:1)


וּפַעַם אַחַת בָּא אֵלָיו אֶחָד מֵהַגְּדוֹלִים, שֶׁהָיָה חָשׁוּב מְאֹד בְּאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל וּבְחוּץ־לָאָרֶץ, וְקִלְּסוּהוּ שֶׁהוּא בָּקִי בְּכָל הַשַּׁ"ס כִּמְעַט בְּעַל פֶּה, וְשֶׁהוּא גַּם־כֵּן מִמָּרֵי קַבָּלָה.


The Rebbe was once visited by one of the renowned leaders of the Holy Land. This leader was universally respected and was reputed to know the entire Talmud by heart. He was also a master of Kabbalah.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 31:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:2)


וּבָא אֶל רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, וְהִזְהִיר לְהוֹצִיא כָּל אִישׁ מֵעֲלֵיהֶם, וְלֹא עָמַד אִישׁ אִתָּם בְּהִתְוַעֲדָם יַחַד. אַךְ הָאִישׁ הנ"ל שֶׁהָיָה עִם רַבֵּנוּ, ז"ל נִשְׁאַר שָׁם.


When he visited the Rebbe, he asked all those present to leave. They were left alone together, except for the Rebbe’s attendant.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 31:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:3)


וְהִפְצִיר אוֹתוֹ הַגָּדוֹל הַנַּ"ל וְאָמַר לְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, בְּלָשׁוֹן זֶה: יָדַעְנוּ שֶׁכְּבוֹד־תּוֹרָתוֹ לֹא בָּא לְאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל כִּשְׁאָר הֶהָמוֹן בְּמֹחִין דְּקַטְנוּת, דְּהַיְנוּ כְּדֵי לֵילֵךְ אַרְבַּע אַמּוֹת בְּאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל וְיִהְיֶה בֶּן עוֹלָם הַבָּא אוֹ שְׁאָר מַחֲשָׁבוֹת כָּאֵלּוּ כְּדֶרֶךְ אֲנָשִׁים פְּשׁוּטִים.


The leader pressed the Rebbe, saying, “I know that Your Excellency did not come to the Holy Land like most people. Others arrive with constricted consciousness,⁶⁸, a Kabbalistic term referring to the immature state of the transcendental intellects. Cf. *Etz Chaim* 22. merely to walk four steps in the Holy Land and thus be worthy of the World to Come,⁶⁹ 111a. or for similar, small-minded reasons.

footnotes:
⁶⁸ Mochin deKatnut
⁶⁹ Regarding the concept of four steps, see Ketubot


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 31:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:4)


רַק בְּוַדַּאי כְּבוֹד־תּוֹרָתוֹ בָּא לְכָאן בְּמֹחִין דְּגַדְלוּת וְלִפְעֹל הַשָּׂגָה גְּדוֹלָה בַּעֲבוֹדַת הַבּוֹרֵא יִתְבָּרַךְ. עַל־כֵּן רְצוֹנֵנוּ לֵידַע בְּאֵיזֶה נְקֻדָּה מִנְּקֻדַּת אֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל נִכְנַס כְּבוֹד־תּוֹרָתוֹ, וּמַה הוּא רוֹצֶה לִפְעֹל כָּאן בַּעֲבוֹדַת הַבּוֹרֵא? וַאֲנִי מוּכָן לְשָׁרְתוֹ בְּגוּפִי וּבְנֶפֶשׁ־רוּחַ־נְשָׁמָה שֶׁלִּי וְכוּ'.


“But Your Excellency certainly arrived here in a state of expanded consciousness,⁷⁰, a Kabbalistic term referring to the mature state of the transcendental intellects. to accomplish great acts of devotion. There are many points of devotion in the Holy Land, and we long to know into which of these points Your Excellency has entered. If you reveal the Godly things you wish to do here, I am prepared to serve you with body and soul.”

footnotes:
⁷⁰ Mochin DeGadlut


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 31:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:5)


וְהֵשִׁיב לוֹ רַבֵּנוּ: יְדִיד נַפְשִׁי, אַל תְּצַעֲרוּ אוֹתִי בְּדָבָר זֶה. כִּי לֹא דָּבָר קַל הוּא לְגַלּוֹת לָכֶם, ח"ו, מָה הָעֲבוֹדָה הַזֹּאת וּבִשְׁבִיל מָה בָּאתִי לְפֹה. וְאוּלַי אֲנִי מֻשְׁבָּע וְעוֹמֵד עַל זֶה וְכוּ'.


The Rebbe replied, “My dear friend, please do not pain me with this. To reveal my reasons and devotions here is by no means a simple matter. It is possible that I am already bound by an oath in this area … .”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 31:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:6)


וְהִתְחִיל שׁוּב הָרַב הַגָּדוֹל הַנַּ"ל לְהַפְצִיר אוֹתוֹ מְאֹד, וְאָמַר: שְׁאֵלָתֵנוּ וּבַקָּשָׁתֵנוּ עַל־כָּל־פָּנִים יְלַמְּדֵנוּ רַבֵּנוּ אֵיזֶה חִדּוּשׁ מֵחִדּוּשָׁיו הַנֶּחֱמָדִים וְהַנְּעִימִים אֲשֶׁר חֲנָנוֹ ה'. יְחָנֵּנוּ גַּם־כֵּן וְיַטְעִימֵנוּ מֵהֶם. וְאֵין כַּוָּנָתֵנוּ ח"ו לְאֵיזֶה פְּנִיָּה. רַק כַּוָּנָתֵנוּ לִשְׁמֹעַ חִדּוּשֵׁי תּוֹרָה מִפִּיו הַקָּדוֹשׁ, אוּלַי יִתְעוֹרֵר לְבָבֵנוּ מֵחָדָשׁ לַעֲבוֹדָתוֹ יִתְבָּרַךְ, וְגַם אוּלַי נִזְכֶּה מִמֵּילָא לְהָבִין וּלְהַשְׂכִּיל אֵיזֶה רְמִיזָא מֵהַנְּקֻדָּה הַנַּ"ל.


The leader began to urge him again. “All we ask is that our master at least teach us one of the beautiful, precious thoughts with which God endowed him. We do not have any ulterior motive, Heaven forbid. We only want to hear from your holy lips some new Torah concept that will arouse our hearts to serve God. If God wills, we may also be worthy of perceiving some hint of the point we asked about before.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 31:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:7)


מִיָּד הִתְחִיל רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, לְהִתְלַהֵב מְאֹד בְּרִשְׁפֵּי רִשְׁפֵּי שַׁלְהֶבֶת י"ק, וּפָנָיו נִבְעֲרוּ וְנִתְלַהֲבוּ כְּלַפִּידִים מַמָּשׁ. וּמֵחֲמַת הַהִתְלַהֲבוּת תִּסָּמֵר שַׂעֲרוֹת ראֹשׁוֹ, וְהִשְׁלִיךְ הַכּוֹבַע עֶלְיוֹן מֵעַל ראֹשׁוֹ, וְהִתְחִיל לְדַבֵּר בְּלָשׁוֹן זֶה: "הַיְדַעְתֶּם סוֹד כַּוָּנוֹת הַתְּפִלִּין"?


Immediately, the Rebbe began to light up, flashing fire, the flame of God (Song of Songs 8:6). His face began to glow, literally burning like fire. He was in such an intense state that his hair stood on end, casting his hat to the floor.

Like tongues of flame, a question formed on his lips: “Do you understand the secret meaning of the *tefilin*?”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 31:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:8)


הֵשִׁיב לוֹ הַנַּ"ל קְצָת כַּוָּנוֹת.


The other replied, offering some Kabbalistic concepts.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 31:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:9)


אָמַר לוֹ: "לָאו, לֹא זוֹ הַדֶּרֶךְ שֶׁל כַּוָּנַת תְּפִלִּין, וְכֵיוָן שֶׁאֵינְכֶם יוֹדְעִים סוֹד כַּוָּנוֹת תְּפִלִּין אֵינְכֶם יוֹדְעִים סוֹד אַרְבַּע רוּחוֹת שֶׁל אֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל. וְעַכְשָׁו אַתְחִיל לְגַלּוֹת לָכֶם אֵיזֶה רֶמֶז מִזֶּה".


The Rebbe transfixed him, “No! This is not the way of perceiving the *tefilin*. And because you do not understand the true meaning of *tefilin*, you do not know the secret of the four directions in the Holy Land. Of this, I will begin to give you some hint.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 31:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:10)


וַיְהִי כַּאֲשֶׁר הִתְחִיל לְדַבֵּר הִתְחִיל לָצֵאת דָּם מִגְּרוֹנוֹ, וְאָמַר לְהֶחָכָם הנ"ל: "עַתָּה רְאוּ בְּעֵינֵיכֶם כִּי אֵין מַסְכִּימִים מִן הַשָּׁמַיִם לְגַלּוֹת לָכֶם דָּבָר".


The Rebbe began the explanation, but as soon as he started to speak, blood began to pour from his throat. He said, “Now you see with your own eyes that I am forbidden from on high to reveal anything to you.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 31:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/31:11)


וְתֵכֶף כְּשֶׁרָאָה הֶחָכָם הַנַּ"ל זֹאת בְּעֵינָיו, נִעְנַע לוֹ ראֹשׁוֹ, וּבִקֵּשׁ אוֹתוֹ מְאֹד שֶׁיִּמְחוֹל לוֹ מַה שֶּׁהִטְרִיחַ אוֹתוֹ כָּל־כָּךְ, וְנִבְהַל מְאֹד בָּזֶה. וְלֹא רָצָה לָזוּז מִשָּׁם עַד שֶׁמָּחַל לוֹ רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל.


As soon as the other saw this, he bowed his head and begged the Rebbe to pardon him for having troubled him so much. He was very frightened, and would not move from the spot until the Rebbe had forgiven him.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:1)


גַּם תֵּכֶף כְּשֶׁבָּא לְשָׁם לִטְבֶרְיָה, שָׁלַח אֵלָיו הָרַב הֶחָסִיד הַמְפֻרְסָם הַגְּבִיר מוֹרֵנוּ הָרַב מֹשֶׁה ז"ל (בֶּן הָרַב הַקָּדוֹשׁ הַמְפֻרְסָם מוֹרֵנוּ הָרַב מְנַחֵם מֶנְדִּיל זצ"ל וִויטֶפְּסְקֶר) שֶׁרוֹצֶה לְהִתְחַתֵּן עִמּוֹ.


When the Rebbe first came to Tiberias, the renowned chassid and philanthropist, Rabbi Moshe, son of the holy Rabbi Menachem Mendel of Vitebsk,⁷¹mentions that his son, Moshe, had a son named Shmuel. Most probably, Shmuel was the one matched with Rebbe Nachman’s daughter, Miriam. sent to the Rebbe, announcing that he would like to make a match between their children.

footnotes:
⁷¹ Rabbi Menachem Mendel (1730-1788) was a leading disciple of Rabbi Dov Ber, the Maggid of Mezeritch. He led a massive pilgrimage to the Holy Land in 1777 and settled in Tiberias, becoming the leader of the Chassidic community in Israel (see above, n.46). A biography published together with his Likutey Amarim


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 32:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:2)


וְהֵשִׁיב רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל: "הַאֵיךְ יְכוֹלִים לְמָאֵן בְּשִׁדּוּךְ? מִן הַסְּתָם מֵהַשֵּׁם יָצָא הַדָּבָר שֶׁתִּהְיֶה בִּתִּי בָּאָרֶץ הַקְּדוֹשָׁה" וְלֹא דִּבֵּר יוֹתֵר.


The Rebbe replied, “How can one refuse such a match? It is certainly God’s will that my daughter live in the Holy Land.”


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 32:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:3)


וּמִיָּד הָלַךְ הַשַּׁדְכָן וְהֵשִׁיב לְהָרַב רַבִּי מֹשֶׁה הַנַּ"ל שֶׁרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל מְרֻצֶּה. וְתֵכֶף עָשָׂה סְעֻדָּה גְּדוֹלָה.


The Rebbe said nothing else, and the matchmaker immediately went and told Rabbi Moshe that the Rebbe had agreed. They immediately planned a great feast to celebrate the match.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 32:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:4)


וְהָיוּ עַל הַסְּעֻדָּה כָּל גְּדוֹלֵי אֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל הֵן מִמְּדִינַת לִיטָא וְהֵן מִמְּדִינַת פּוֹלִין, כֻּלָּם כְּאֶחָד גְּדוֹלִים וּקְטַנִּים. וְהָיָה לָהֶם שִׂמְחָה גְּדוֹלָה. וּבְתוֹךְ הַסְּעֻדָּה דִּבְּרוּ דִּבְרֵי־תּוֹרָה בֵּינֵיהֶם.


All the leaders of the Holy Land, from Lithuania and Poland, great and small alike, were assembled at the feast. It was a most joyous gathering, and the meal was accompanied by a flowing conversation covering the length and breadth of the sacred literature.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 32:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:5)


וְרֹאשׁ הַמְדַבְּרִים הָיָה זֶה הַגָּדוֹל הַנַּ"ל שֶׁרָצָה שֶׁרַבֵּנוּ יְגַלֶּה לוֹ הַנְּקֻדָּה וְכוּ'. בְּתוֹךְ דִּבְרֵיהֶם נִשְׁכַּח מֵהֶם אֵיזֶה רַשִׁ"י אַיֵּה אֵפוֹא מְקוֹמָהּ?. וְרָצוּ לְהַעֲלוֹתָהּ עַל זִכְרוֹנָם כִּי הָיָה מֻכְרָח לָהֶם מְאֹד לְעִנְיָנָם. וְלֹא יָכְלוּ לָזוּז מִשָּׁם מֵחֲמַת זֶה, וְהָיָה לָהֶם לְפֶלֶא. כִּי כָּל אֶחָד וְאֶחָד מֵהֶם סִיֵּם בְּסָמוּךְ כָּל הַשַּׁ"ס וְאֵין אֶחָד מֵהֶם יוֹדֵעַ מְקוֹם הָרַשִׁ"י הַנַּ"ל אֵיפֹה הִיא נִשְׁנֵית.


The leading speaker was the one mentioned earlier, who had asked the Rebbe his intentions in the Holy Land. The conversation led them to a discussion of a quotation from *Rashi*,⁷² (1040-1105), leading commentator on the Talmud and *Tanakh* (Hebrew Bible). but nobody could remember its location. The conversation stopped, frustrated by the lack of this key quotation. Try as they did, no one could recall it. This in itself was a wonder, for here were the foremost sages, each one having recently reviewed the entire Talmud. Still, not one of them could locate this particular quotation.

footnotes:
⁷² Acronym for Rabbi Shlomo Yitzchaki


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 32:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:6)


וְרַבֵּנוּ הָיָה דּוּמָם כְּמוֹ שֶׁאֵינוֹ יוֹדֵעַ גַּם־כֵּן, אַךְ בִּרְאוֹתוֹ שֶׁיֵּשׁ לָהֶם צַעַר גָּדוֹל מִזֶּה, וְהֵם הִפְצִירוּ בּוֹ עַד בּוֹשׁ, נַעֲנָה וְאָמַר לָהֶם: 'הֲלֹא רַשִׁ"י זוֹ מְקוֹמָהּ בְּמַסֶּכֶת זְבָחִים בְּדַף זֶה עַל רֹאשׁ הָעַמּוּד', וְכֵן הָיָה. וְהָיָה לָהֶם לְשָׂשׂוֹן וּלְשִׂמְחָה.


Throughout this discussion, the Rebbe sat quietly as if he had nothing to contribute. But he realized how distressed they were, and when they urged him until he could no longer refuse, he said, “Is it not a quotation from *Rashi’s* commentary to *Zevachim*,⁷³.” at the very top of this page?” They realized that this was what they were seeking, and rejoiced immensely.

footnotes:
⁷³ The Talmudic tractate dealing with sacrificial offerings. There is a tradition that this quotation appeared on page 45a, “hilkhata liMashicha


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 32:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:7)


וְנִמְשְׁכָה סְעֻדָּה זוֹ מִזְּמַן מִנְחָה גְּדוֹלָה עַד אוֹר הַיּוֹם, וְנַעֲשָׂה הִתְקַשְּׁרוּת בֵּינֵיהֶם.


The feast lasted from early noon until dawn the next day, and the match was formalized.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 32:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:8)


אַךְ אַחַר כֵּן, כְּשֶׁיָּצָא רַבֵּנוּ מִשָּׁם מֵאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל לְחוּץ־לָאָרֶץ, נִפְטַר הֶחָתָן בְּנוֹ שֶׁל הָרַב מוֹרֵנוּ הָרַב רַבִּי מֹשֶׁה הַנַּ"ל, וְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל נִשְׁתַּדֵּךְ עִם בִּתּוֹ שֶׁהָיְתָה הַכַּלָּה שֶׁל בֶּן הָרַב מוֹרֵנוּ רַבִּי מֹשֶׁה הַנַּ"ל, עִם כְּבוֹד הַגָּאוֹן הַמְפֻרְסָם הָרַב דִּקְהִלַּת קֹדֶשׁ וָלִיטְשִׁיסְק. וְנִתְגַּלְגֵּל אַחַר־כָּךְ מֵאֵת ה' שֶׁנָּסַע הָרַב הַגָּאוֹן הַנַּ"ל לָאָרֶץ הַקְּדוֹשָׁה, וְלָקַח אֶת בְּנוֹ חֲתַן רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל עִמּוֹ לְשָׁם כְּדֵי שֶׁיִּלְמַד אֶצְלוֹ שָׁם. וְאַחַר־כָּךְ נָסְעָה בַּת רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, גַּם כֵּן לְשָׁם.


Soon after the Rebbe returned home, Rabbi Moshe’s son died. The Rebbe’s daughter [then] married the son of the renowned Rabbi of Volochisk.⁷⁴, L-15). His son, Rabbi Pinchas, was engaged to Rebbe Nachman’s third daughter, Miriam, in Elul 5560/1800, fifteen months after his return from the Holy Land. They were married four years later, in Cheshvan 5565/1804 (*Tzaddik* *#*114, #117). God willed that the Rabbi of Volochisk would then settle in the Holy Land, taking along his son, the Rebbe’s son-in-law, to study with him. Not too long after that, the Rebbe’s daughter also settled there.⁷⁵ #36; *Tzaddik* #142; *Alim LeTerufah* (Jerusalem, 5728), unnumbered letter dated 12 Av 5569).

###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 32:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/32:9)


וְנִתְקַיְּמוּ דִּבְרֵי רַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, שֶׁאָמַר שֶׁמֵּאֵת הַשֵּׁם הוּא שֶׁבִּתּוֹ תִּהְיֶה בָּאָרֶץ הַקְּדוֹשָׁה. וְדָבָר אֶחָד מִדְּבָרָיו אָחוֹר לֹא יָשׁוּב רֵיקָם.


Thus, the Rebbe’s words were fulfilled, for he had said that it was God’s will that his daughter settle in the Holy Land. For not one word that he spoke returned in vain.⁷⁶blessings, from Isaiah 55:11.

⁷⁶ Haftarah


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:1)


וְאַחַר כָּל אֵלֶּה, אַחַר אֲשֶׁר עָשָׂה כָּל מִינֵי עֲבוֹדוֹת הַנַּ"ל בֶּאֱמֶת וּבְתָמִים, וְהִרְבָּה בְּתַעֲנִיתִים וּבִתְפִלּוֹת רַבּוֹת מְאֹד וּבְסִגּוּפִים וּשְׁבִירַת תַּאֲווֹת וּמִדּוֹת, וְעָמַד בְּנִסְיוֹנוֹת רַבּוֹת וּשְׁאָר כָּל עִנְיָנִים הַנַּ"ל, וְהִרְבָּה בְּהִתְבּוֹדְדוּת מְאֹד מְאֹד לְדַבֵּר בֵּינוֹ לְבֵין קוֹנוֹ וּלְפָרֵשׁ שִׂיחָתוֹ בִּלְשׁוֹן אַשְׁכְּנַז וְכוּ' כַּנַּ"ל.


No matter how high he stood, the Rebbe was never satisfied with his accomplishments. He had practiced honestly and earnestly every type of devotion mentioned above, fasting and praying and forcing himself to do things to overcome his desires and emotions. He had already withstood countless temptations and spent days and years secluding himself with God to express his thoughts in his own words.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 33:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:2)


עַד שֶׁזָּכָה לְמַדְרֵגָה גְּבֹהָה וְעֶלְיוֹנָה מְאֹד מְאֹד בְּתַכְלִית הַקְּדֻשָּׁה וּבְתַכְלִית הַבִּטּוּל וּבְהִתְקָרְבוּת גָּדוֹל לה' יִתְבָּרַךְ בְּמַדְרֵגַת גְּדוֹלֵי בְּנֵי עֲלִיָּה. וְכָל זֶה זָכָה בִּימֵי יַלְדוּתוֹ מַמָּשׁ, כִּי יוֹמָם לֹא נָח לַיְלָה לֹא נָם וְלֹא שָׁקַט. וְהִתְמִיד מְאֹד בַּעֲבוֹדָתוֹ מִיּוֹם אֶל יוֹם מִתְּחִלַּת יְמֵי קַטְנוּתוֹ וִימֵי נְעוּרָיו. וְיָגַע וְטָרַח מְאֹד מִיּוֹם אֶל יוֹם, עַד שֶׁזָּכָה בִּימֵי נְעוּרָיו מַמָּשׁ לְכָל הַנַּ"ל לִקְדֻשָּׁה גְּבוֹהָה וְנוֹרָאָה וְהַשָּׂגָה עֲצוּמָה מְאֹד וְכוּ' בְּמַדְרֵגַת גְּדוֹלֵי בְּנֵי עֲלִיָּה.


Constantly striving in this manner, he merited to an extremely high and lofty level of the utmost sanctity and the utmost selflessness, with a degree of closeness to God on the level of the greatest denizens on high. The Rebbe attained all this while he was literally in his childhood. He neither rested by day nor slept or was silent by night. Never keeping still, he toiled and labored day in and day out, until literally in his youth he achieved lofty, awesome sanctity and profound perception on the level of the greatest denizens on high.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 33:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:3)


וְאַחַר אֲשֶׁר זָכָה לְכָל זֶה, אַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן לֹא הָיָה מְקָרֵר דַּעְתּוֹ בָּזֶה וְלֹא הִנִּיחַ עַצְמוֹ לַעֲמֹד. רַק גַּם אַחַר־כָּךְ, בְּכָל יוֹם וּבְכָל שָׁעָה הָיָה מִשְׁתּוֹקֵק וּמִתְגַּעְגֵּעַ וְנִכְסָף לה' יִתְבָּרַךְ כְּאִלּוּ לֹא הִתְחִיל עֲדַיִן כְּלָל בְּשׁוּם עֲבוֹדָה בָּעוֹלָם. וַאֲפִלּוּ אַחַר שֶׁבָּא מֵאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל וְזָכָה שָׁם לְהַשָּׂגָה עֶלְיוֹנָה וּגְבֹהָה מְאֹד מְאֹד אֲשֶׁר אֵין לְשַׁעֵר כְּלָל, אַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן מִיּוֹם שֶׁבָּא מֵאֶרֶץ־יִשְׂרָאֵל עַד הַיּוֹם שֶׁנִּסְתַּלֵּק בְּשָׁלוֹם לְמַעְלָה לְמַעְלָה, הָיָה לוֹ תָּמִיד גַּעְגּוּעִים וְכִסּוּפִין וְכוּ' לה' יִתְבָּרַךְ כְּאִלּוּ לֹא הִתְחִיל עֲדַיִן כְּלָל.


But even after achieving all this, the Rebbe was not satisfied and did not allow himself to stagnate. Every hour and every day, he would desire, long and yearn for God as if he had not yet begun to serve Him at all. Even after the Rebbe returned from the Holy Land – where he attained a perception so high that it was beyond measure – until the day he departed from the world in peace, he always had this longing and yearning for God. He may have stood on the loftiest heights, but in his mind he had not yet begun the climb.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 33:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:4)


וּלְסַפֵּר בְּעִנְיָן זֶה תִּקְצַר הֲמוֹן יְרִיעוֹת אֲפִלּוּ מַה שֶּׁזָּכִינוּ לְהָבִין קְצָת בְּעִנְיָן זֶה כְּפִי מַה שֶּׁשָּׁמַעְנוּ מִפִּיו הַקָּדוֹשׁ וְרָאִינוּ בְּעֵינֵינוּ. כִּי בְּכָל פַּעַם הָיָה דַּרְכּוֹ שֶׁהָיָה מִתְגַּעְגֵּעַ לה' יִתְבָּרַךְ, וְהָיָה שָׁפָל בְּעֵינֵי עַצְמוֹ בֶּאֱמֶת, וְהָיָה לוֹ רַחֲמָנוּת גָּדוֹל עַל־עַצְמוֹ כְּאִלּוּ לֹא הֵרִיחַ עֲדַיִן מֵעוֹלָם שׁוּם רֵיחַ שֶׁל עֲבוֹדַת הַשֵּׁם, וּכְמוֹ שֶׁלֹּא הִתְחִיל עֲדַיִן כְּלָל.


It would consume many volumes to tell even the small amount we were worthy of understanding from what we heard from his holy lips and saw with our own eyes. For this was the Rebbe’s way. He would constantly thirst for God, genuinely humbling and pitying himself as if he had never inhaled the perfume of true devotion and had not yet achieved even the level of a beginner.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 33:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:5)


וּבֶאֱמֶת אֲפִלּוּ מִי שֶׁעָבַר עַל כָּל הַתּוֹרָה כֻּלָּהּ, ח"ו, כַּמָּה וְכַמָּה פְּעָמִים, וּכְשֶׁזֶּה הָאִישׁ נִתְעוֹרֵר בִּתְשׁוּבָה בֶּאֱמֶת, שֶׁאָז בְּוַדַּאי כְּשֶׁמִּסְתַּכֵּל בְּעַצְמוֹ הֵיכָן הוּא בָּעוֹלָם בְּוַדַּאי יֵשׁ לוֹ לֵב נִשְׁבָּר מְאֹד וְיֵשׁ לוֹ רַחֲמָנוּת גָּדוֹל מְאֹד עַל־עַצְמוֹ בְּהַכְנָעָה גְּדוֹלָה וְכוּ', וְאֵין צָרִיךְ לְהַאֲרִיךְ בָּזֶה בְּדָבָר הַמּוּבָן מֵעַצְמוֹ, אַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן, אֲפִלּוּ זֹאת הָרַחֲמָנוּת וְהַלֵּב נִשְׁבָּר שֶׁל הַבַּעַל תְּשׁוּבָה הַנַּ"ל לֹא יַגִּיעַ לְחֵלֶק מֵאֶלֶף וּרְבָבָה שֶׁל גֹּדֶל הָרַחֲמָנוּת וְהַלֵּב נִשְׁבָּר בְּהַכְנָעָה גְּדוֹלָה וְכוּ' שֶׁהָיָה לְרַבֵּנוּ ז"ל, בְּכָל פַּעַם וּפַעַם קֹדֶם שֶׁבָּא לְאֵיזֶה הַשָּׂגָה.


To understand this, you must imagine a person who had committed every possible sin time and time again. Imagine now that such a person was aroused to true repentance. Try to depict the way he would look at himself. He would certainly have great humility and self-pity, and be bitter and brokenhearted because of his past deeds. All his self-pity and brokenheartedness would not come to a thousandth, or even a millionth, of the great humility, self-pity and brokenheartedness that the Rebbe experienced each time before he attained a new degree of perception.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 33:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:6)


וְהַכְּלָל, כִּי לְעוֹלָם לֹא נָח וְלֹא שָׁקַט אֲפִלּוּ בִּימֵי גְּדֻלָּתוֹ. אַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁכְּבָר זָכָה לְמַה שֶּׁזָּכָה לְהַשָּׂגַת אֱלֹקוּת בְּמַדְרֵגָה גְּבֹהָה עֲצוּמָה וְנוֹרָאָה מְאֹד, אַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן לֹא הָיָה מִסְתַּפֵּק עַצְמוֹ בָּזֶה, וְטָרַח וְיָגַע בְּכָל־עֵת וּבְכָל שָׁעָה, וְקִבֵּל יִסּוּרִין עַל־עַצְמוֹ יִסּוּרִים קָשִׁים וּמָרִים אֲשֶׁר כִּמְעַט אֵין דֻּגְמָתָם בָּעוֹלָם, וְהִרְבָּה בִּתְפִלּוֹת וְתַחֲנוּנִים וּבְהַפְצָרוֹת וּבַקָּשׁוֹת רַבּוֹת וּבְגַעְגּוּעִים וְכִסּוּפִין גְּדוֹלִים וְנוֹרָאִים מְאֹד מְאֹד, עַד שֶׁבָּא לְהַשָּׂגָה וּמַדְרֵגָה יוֹתֵר עֶלְיוֹנָה. וְאַחַר־כָּךְ, תֵּכֶף שֶׁזָּכָה לְזֹאת הַהַשָּׂגָה וְאָז הָיָה קְצָת בְּשִׂמְחָה.


The Rebbe was a man who never rested or stood still. Even as an adult, when he had already attained an awesome perception of the Godly, he was not satisfied and continued to aspire for more. He accepted upon himself unparalleled suffering and abounded with prayers and petitions urging God to help him. He continued with a powerful, intense yearning until he would finally attain an even higher level of perception.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 33:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:7)


וְלִפְעָמִים זָכִינוּ לִשְׁמֹעַ מִפִּיו שֶׁהוֹדִיעַ לָנוּ שֶׁכָּעֵת נוֹדַע לוֹ חֲדָשׁוֹת וְכוּ' וְאָז הָיָה שָׂמֵחַ קְצָת. וְאַחַר־כָּךְ, תֵּכֶף שֶׁזָּכָה לָזֶה אַחַר־כָּךְ הִתְחִיל עוֹד מֵחָדָשׁ וְשָׁכַח כָּל הֶעָבָר כְּאִלּוּ לֹא הִתְחִיל עֲדַיִן כְּלָל, וְחָזַר וְהִתְחִיל מֵחָדָשׁ כְּמוֹ שֶׁמַּתְחִיל לֵילֵךְ וְלִכְנֹס בִּקְדֻשַּׁת יִשְׂרָאֵל.


When he achieved a new level, he would be happy for a brief time. Occasionally, we were worthy of hearing new insights from his mouth and he would experience some joy. But then he would immediately begin anew. All his previous efforts would be forgotten as if he had not yet even taken the first step. He would begin afresh, like one taking his first steps into the realm of holiness.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 33:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:8)


וְלִפְעָמִים הָיִינוּ שׁוֹמְעִים מִפִּיו הַקָּדוֹשׁ בְּפֵרוּשׁ שֶׁאָמַר בִּלְשׁוֹן הִשְׁתּוֹקְקוּת וְכִסּוּפִין: אֵיךְ זוֹכִין לִהְיוֹת יְהוּדִי? וְאָמַר זֹאת בֶּאֱמֶת וּבִתְמִימוּת גָּדוֹל כְּאִלּוּ לֹא הִתְחִיל עֲדַיִן כְּלָל. וְכֵן הָיָה כַּמָּה פְּעָמִים.


Often we heard the Rebbe say with longing and yearning, “How is one worthy of being a Jew?” (see “*His Wisdom*” #159). He would mean these words sincerely, for he truly felt that he had not yet really taken the first step. This occurred numerous times.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 33:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:9)


וְהָיָה תָּמִיד הוֹלֵךְ וְעוֹלֶה מִמַּדְרֵגָה לְמַדְרֵגָה. אַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁזָּכָה לְמַדְרֵגוֹת גְּבוֹהוֹת וְנוֹרָאוֹת מְאֹד לְמַעְלָה לְמַעְלָה וְכוּ', אַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן מֵעוֹלָם לֹא הָיָה מְקָרֵר דַּעְתּוֹ בָּזֶה כְּלָל, וְתֵכֶף אַחַר־כָּךְ הָיָה לוֹ לֵב נִשְׁבָּר וְהָיָה לוֹ רַחֲמָנוּת גָּדוֹל עַל־עַצְמוֹ בְּהַכְנָעָה גְּדוֹלָה וְכוּ' כַּנַּ"ל, עַד שֶׁזָּכָה לְמַדְרֵגָה יוֹתֵר גְּבוֹהָה. וְכֵן הָיָה דַּרְכּוֹ תָּמִיד עַד הַסּוֹף.


Although he reached awesomely high levels, he would constantly seek a higher level, soaring ever higher. Still, he was never satisfied with himself. As soon as he reached a new level, he would again begin anew with a broken heart and deep humility, until he was able to attain a still higher step. This was always the Rebbe’s way, even at the end.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 33:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:10)


וְכַמָּה וְכַמָּה פְּעָמִים הָיָה דַּרְכּוֹ שֶׁאָמַר שֶׁעַתָּה אֵינוֹ יוֹדֵעַ כְּלָל כְּלָל, וּכְלָל לֹא. וּפְעָמִים נִשְׁבַּע עַל זֶה שֶׁבֶּאֱמֶת אֵינוֹ יוֹדֵעַ כְּלָל. אַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁבַּיּוֹם הַקּוֹדֵם וּבַשָּׁעָה הַקּוֹדֶמֶת גִּלָּה דְּבָרִים עַתִּיקִים, אַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן אַחַר־כָּךְ אָמַר שֶׁאֵינוֹ יוֹדֵעַ כְּלָל. וּבְעִנְיָן זֶה הָיָה חִדּוּשׁ גָּדוֹל מְאֹד מְאֹד.


Many times the Rebbe would say, “Now I know nothing, nothing, nothing at all.” There are times when he would swear, “In truth, I know absolutely nothing at all.”⁷⁷, the Shabbat after Tisha B’Av, 1 Av 5570 (August 11, 1810), in Uman, just about two months before his passing. See "*His Wisdom*" #153; *Yemey Moharnat* #51; *Tzaddik* #112. He would even say this shortly after he had revealed words of enduring truth (cf. *Pesachim* 119a; *Bava Batra* 91b). His wisdom shone forth and still he insisted that he was totally ignorant. In this respect, the Rebbe was most unique.

footnotes:
⁷⁷ This occurred on Shabbat Nachamu


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 33:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/33:11)


וְאָמַר בְּפֵרוּשׁ עַל־עַצְמוֹ: שֶׁהַתּוֹרָה שֶׁלּוֹ הוּא חִדּוּשׁ גָּדוֹל מְאֹד, אֲבָל "הָאֵינוֹ יוֹדֵעַ" שֶׁלּוֹ הוּא חִדּוּשׁ גָּדוֹל בְּיוֹתֵר. הַיְנוּ עִנְיָן הַנַּ"ל, כִּי בְּכָל פַּעַם הָיָה דַּרְכּוֹ לוֹמַר שֶׁעַכְשָׁו אֵינוֹ יוֹדֵעַ כְּלָל (וְעַיֵּן עוֹד מִזֶּה בְּמָקוֹם אַחֵר)


The Rebbe said, “My teachings are very unique, but my ignorance is even more unique.” That is, he was unique in realizing his ultimate ignorance before God.⁷⁸ #51; "*His Wisdom*" loc. cit. Also see "*His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land*" #35; "*His Wisdom*" #3.

footnotes:
⁷⁸ Yemey Moharnat


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/34:1)


וּכְבָר נִשְׁמַע מִפִּיו הַקָּדוֹשׁ שֶׁאָמַר: כִּי יֵשׁ צַדִּיקִים שֶׁעָבְדוּ וְטָרְחוּ עַד שֶׁזָּכוּ לְאֵיזֶה מַדְרֵגָה וּמַעֲלָה כָּל אֶחָד כְּפִי מַעֲלָתוֹ, כְּמוֹ לְמָשָׁל אֵצֶל מֶלֶךְ שֶׁשָּׂרָיו זוֹכִין עַל־יְדֵי עֲבוֹדָתָם כָּל אֶחָד לְאֵיזֶה מַעֲלָה לְאֵיזֶה הִתְמַנּוּת גָּבֹהַּ כְּפִי עֲבוֹדָתוֹ.


We heard from the Rebbe’s own holy lips that some tzaddikim toil with devotion in order to reach a given plateau. They have a set level to which they aspire, and when they reach it, they are satisfied. They are like servants of a king who aspire to attain a particular rank of office through their efforts.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 34:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/34:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/34:2)


אֲבָל עַל־עַצְמוֹ אָמַר: שֶׁאִם הָיָה יוֹדֵעַ שֶׁהוּא עַכְשָׁו בְּאוֹתָהּ הַמַּעֲלָה וּמַדְרֵגָה שֶׁל אֶשְׁתָּקַד אֵינוֹ רוֹצֶה אֶת עַצְמוֹ כְּלָל (וְאָמַר בִּלְשׁוֹן גְּנַאי עַל־עַצְמוֹ עַל עִנְיָן הַנַּ"ל אִם הָיָה נִשְׁאָר בְּמַדְרֵגָה שֶׁל אֶשְׁתָּקַד) רַק בְּכָל פַּעַם הוּא עוֹלֶה מִמַּדְרֵגָה לְמַדְרֵגָה.


The Rebbe then said, “If I knew that I was now standing on the same level as last year, I would totally reject myself.” He would consider remaining at last year’s level the greatest detriment, for he constantly aspired to reach a higher level.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 34:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/34:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/34:3)


וְיֵשׁ הַרְבֵּה מְאֹד בְּעִנְיָן זֶה לְסַפֵּר, אַךְ אִי־אֶפְשָׁר לְסַפֵּר כָּל זֶה בִּכְתָב כְּלָל, כִּי־אִם מִי שֶׁזָּכָה לִרְאוֹת בְּעֵינָיו וְלִשְׁמֹעַ בְּאָזְנָיו הָיָה יָכוֹל לְשַׁעֵר בְּלִבֵּהּ מְעַט מִקְצָת עִנְיָן זֶה אֵיךְ לְעוֹלָם לֹא הָיָה עוֹמֵד עַל מַדְרֵגָה אַחַת בְּשׁוּם פַּעַם, רַק תֵּכֶף הָיָה מִשְׁתּוֹקֵק לַעֲלוֹת לְמַדְרֵגָה גָּבֹהַּ יוֹתֵר עַד שֶׁזָּכָה וְכוּ' וְכֵן לְעוֹלָם. וְהָיָה חִדּוּשׁ גָּדוֹל בְּעִנְיָן זֶה.


There is much to say here, but it cannot all be put into writing. Those who were worthy to see it with their own eyes and hear it with their own ears might be able to understand this to a small degree. They might perceive how the Rebbe never stood still at any level, but always yearned for the next step until he was worthy. This was always true of him.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 34:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/34:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/34:4)


וְכָל זֶה הָיָה כְּפִי תְּפִיסָתֵנוּ הַמְּעוּטָה, וְיוֹתֵר מִזֶּה יֵשׁ בְּעִנְיָן זֶה סִתְרֵי נִסְתָּרוֹת פִּלְאֵי פְּלָאוֹת, וְדַי בָּזֶה.


Even according to our meager understanding, the Rebbe was unique in every way. Beyond this are marvelous wonders and hidden secrets. But we must suffice with this.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/35:1)


וּבְעִנְיַן תַּכְלִית הַיְדִיעָה אֲשֶׁר לֹא נֵדַע, סִפֵּר גַּם־כֵּן קְצָת עִמָּנוּ אֵיזֶה פְּעָמִים, אֵיךְ בְּכָל יְדִיעָה יֵשׁ זֶה הַתַּכְלִית. וְעַל־כֵּן אַף־עַל־פִּי שֶׁזּוֹכִים לָבוֹא לְזֶה הַתַּכְלִית אֲשֶׁר לֹא נֵדַע, אַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן עֲדַיִן אֵין זֶה הַתַּכְלִית הָאַחֲרוֹן, כִּי עֲדַיִן אֵין זֶה תַּכְלִית הַיְדִיעָה כִּי אִם בִּידִיעָה זוֹ וּצְרִיכִים אַחַר־כָּךְ לִטְרֹחַ לִזְכּוֹת לְתַכְלִית גָּבוֹהַּ יוֹתֵר לִזְכּוֹת לְמַדְרֵגַת הַתַּכְלִית אֲשֶׁר לֹא נֵדַע בִּידִיעָה הַגָּבוֹהַּ יוֹתֵר, וְכֵן לְעוֹלָם.


The Rebbe spoke to us somewhat about the concept that the true goal of knowledge is the realization of one’s “not knowing.” He said that this is true of every area of knowledge. Even though a person may attain the goal of realizing his ignorance, it may only be in one area of knowledge. He must then begin to work on a higher plane, aspiring to realize his "not knowing" at this higher level. And no matter how high one reaches, there is still the next step.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 35:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/35:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/35:2)


נִמְצָא שֶׁלְּעוֹלָם אֵין יוֹדְעִין כְּלָל, וְאַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן לֹא הִתְחִיל עֲדַיִן לְהַשִּׂיג הַתַּכְלִית. (וּכְבָר מְבֹאָר מִזֶּה קְצָת בִּדְבָרֵינוּ בְּמָקוֹם אַחֵר) וְעִנְיָן זֶה עָמֹק וְנִסְתָּר מְאֹד מְאֹד. (וְעוֹד יֵשׁ בְּעִנְיָן זֶה מַה שֶּׁשָּׁמַעְנוּ פַּעַם אַחַת אַחַר פֶּסַח וְיִתְבָּאֵר בְּמָקוֹם אַחֵר אִם יִרְצֶה הַשֵּׁם.)


Therefore, we never know anything, and still do not attain the true goal. This is a very deep and mysterious concept. We heard more regarding this once after Pesach⁷⁹ #282). Although there is a misprint in the date, it can be resolved on the basis of *Tzaddik* #150. and will discuss it elsewhere (see “*His Wisdom*” #3).

footnotes:
⁷⁹ This was Pesach 5565/1805 (Tzaddik


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/36:1)


פַּעַם אֶחָד שָׁלַח מֶלֶךְ אֶחָד שְׁלֹשָׁה אֲנָשִׁים לִמְדִינָה עִם דְּבַר סֵתֶר לְמֶלֶךְ אַחֵר. וְהֵם עָבְרוּ בְּתוֹךְ הַמְּדִינוֹת שֶׁהָיוּ שָׁם חוֹלְקִים עַל הַמֶּלֶךְ.


Once a king sent three of his servants to deliver a secret message to another king in a distant land. On the way, they had to pass through lands that were at war with their king.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 36:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/36:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/36:2)


וְהָרִאשׁוֹן נָהַג עַצְמוֹ בְּחָכְמָה, וְהֶעֱלִים הָעִנְיָן לְגַמְרֵי, וְעָבַר הַמְּדִינָה וְלֹא הֵבִינוּ כְּלָל שֶׁיֵּשׁ לוֹ דְּבַר סֵתֶר.


The first messenger was clever enough to conceal his purpose completely. He passed through the hostile land without its inhabitants ever realizing that he was carrying a secret message.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 36:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/36:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/36:3)


וְהַשֵּׁנִי עָבַר לְשָׁם, וְעָמְדוּ בְּנֵי הַמְּדִינָה עַל הַדָּבָר, וְהֵבִינוּ שֶׁיֵּשׁ לוֹ דְּבַר סֵתֶר וְתָפְסוּ אוֹתוֹ שֶׁיַּגִּיד לָהֶם. וְעָשָׂה בְּחָכְמָה אוֹ בִּגְבוּרָה וְנִמְלַט מִיָּדָם.


The second messenger set out through the unfriendly country and was discovered. The people realized that he was carrying a secret message and were going to force him to reveal it. But through his wisdom and endurance, he too was able to escape without revealing the message.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 36:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/36:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/36:4)


הַשְּׁלִישִׁי עָבַר שָׁם וְעָמְדוּ עָלָיו גַּם־כֵּן, וְהֵבִינוּ בּוֹ שֶׁיֵּשׁ לוֹ סוֹד, וְתָפְסוּ אוֹתוֹ, וְהָיוּ מְעַנִּים אוֹתוֹ בְּכַמָּה וְכַמָּה עִנּוּיִים הַרְבֵּה מְאֹד, וְכָל מַה שֶּׁעָשׂוּ לוֹ עִנּוּיִים וְיִסּוּרִים וְצָרוֹת גְּדוֹלוֹת וְהוּא לֹא הִגִּיד דָּבָר וְעָמַד בַּנִּסָּיוֹן וְלֹא גִּלָּה הַסּוֹד. עַד אֲשֶׁר רָאוּ שֶׁאֵין מוֹעִיל לוֹ שׁוּם דָּבָר וְהִנִּיחוּהוּ (כִּי חָשְׁבוּ מִסְּתָמָא אֵינוֹ יוֹדֵעַ כְּלָל). וְעָבַר וּבָא לְהַמֶּלֶךְ.


The third messenger was also discovered. Realizing that he too was bearing a secret message, the people imprisoned him and subjected him to all kinds of tortures. They tortured him in the cruelest ways possible, but despite his great agony, he refused to tell them anything. He withstood the test without yielding his secret. Seeing that their tortures were to no avail, his tormentors assumed that they were mistaken and that he actually had no secret. They let him go, and he passed through their land to deliver his message to the other king.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 36:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/36:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/36:5)


וְהָיוּ חוֹקְרִים, לְמִי מֵהַשָּׁלֹשׁ הַנַּ"ל מַגִּיעַ שָׂכָר יוֹתֵר?. קְצָתָם אָמְרוּ, לְהָרִאשׁוֹן מַגִּיעַ יוֹתֵר, שֶׁעָשָׂה בְּחָכְמָה לְהַעֲלִים לְגַמְרֵי. קְצָתָם אָמְרוּ, לְהַשֵּׁנִי מַגִּיעַ יוֹתֵר שֶׁכְּבָר עָמְדוּ עַל דַּעְתּוֹ וְאַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן נִמְלַט מֵהֶם בְּחָכְמָתוֹ וְכוּ' כַּנַּ"ל.


When the three messengers returned, everyone had an opinion as to which of them deserved the greatest reward. Some said that the first messenger was most deserving, for he had acted cleverly enough to hide his secret completely. Others gave the most credit to the second messenger, for though he was discovered, he was smart enough to escape.


###### Shivchei HaRan, An Account of His Pilgrimage to the Holy Land 36:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/36:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Shivchei%20HaRan/s/An%20Account%20of%20His%20Pilgrimage%20to%20the%20Holy%20Land/r/36:6)


אָמַר הַמֶּלֶךְ: לְהַשְּׁלִישִׁי מַגִּיעַ שָׂכָר יוֹתֵר מִכֻּלָּם, כִּי כְּבָר נִתְפַּס בִּמְצוּדָתָם וּבְוַדַּאי גַּם הוּא הָיָה רוֹצֶה לְהַעֲלִים אַךְ לֹא עָלְתָה בְּיָדוֹ, וְנִתְפַּס בְּיָדָם. וְעִנּוּ אוֹתוֹ וְיִסְּרוּ אוֹתוֹ בְּיִסּוּרִים וְעִנּוִּיים כָּאֵלּוּ שֶׁאִם הָיָה מְגַּלֶּה סוֹד אֶחָד הָיוּ הַכֹּל רָצִים אַחֲרָיו וְאַף־עַל־פִּי־כֵן עָמַד בְּנִסָּיוֹן כָּזֶה וְלֹא גִּלָּה דָּבָר עַל כֵּן מַגִּיעַ לוֹ שָׂכָר יוֹתֵר מִכּוּלָם


But the king said that the third messenger deserved the greatest reward of all. This messenger had already been caught in their net. Certainly, he had also wanted to hide his purpose, but was not successful. After being captured, he underwent every possible torture and torment. Had he revealed even one secret, he would have received the greatest honors [from his persecutors]. Nevertheless, he withstood the test, revealing nothing. Therefore, his reward is above all the rest.

(וְהַנִּמְשָׁל מוּבָן קְצָת מְעַט לַמְּבִינִים)


[Those who comprehend will to some extent understand the parable.]
